# EXECPLAN2.md — Swift Deep Research: Audit Findings & Execution Plan

**Generated:** 2026-06-15  
**Method:** 12-agent parallel subsystem audit → adversarial per-finding verification (re-read cited code) → architect synthesis.  
**Scope:** ~17.7k LOC Swift (80 files) + SeekDB Python sidecar + MiroFish/DeerFlow HTTP integration.  
**Result:** 150 verified findings kept (1 P0, 21 P1, 59 P2, 69 P3); 5 refuted and excluded.

> Each item is a checkbox. Work top-down by severity. Re-verify against live code before each change, build after each cluster, and check the box with evidence.


## Implementation Status (live)

_Updated 2026-06-15. Working top-down; every change validated by a clean `xcodebuild` (Debug, macOS)._

**Catalog progress: 81 / 150 findings done (54%).** All landed changes build green (Debug/macOS, 0 errors/warnings in app sources).

**Batch 7 — DeerFlow correctness + UI responsiveness (20 findings, building green):**
4-agent workflow over disjoint files; the deinit-cancel isolation snag was fixed in the main loop (`@ObservationIgnored nonisolated(unsafe)` Task handle so a nonisolated deinit can cancel it on an `@Observable @MainActor` class).
- DeerFlow engine: `deerflow-2` (recency-windowed observationDigest, ends O(steps²)), `deerflow-3` (fast preset now guarantees ≥1 research step before honoring "enough context"), `deerflow-4` (budget-headroom check skips a doomed re-plan round-trip), `deerflow-5` (processing steps keep web tools narrowed; WorkerAgent prompt-gating sub-part deferred), `deerflow-6` (only running steps fold into the emitted plan), `deerflow-7` (parse failure emits a visible warning; LLMJSON brace-scan hardening deferred), `deerflow-8` (clean reflection-concluded on wall-clock bust), `deerflow-10` (KB stays a first-class planner resource via kbEnabled even when the title list fails/empty), `deerflow-11` (investigation tool calls counted against the per-worker budget), `deerflow-12` (sequential maxStepsPerPlan distinct from parallel maxWorkers).
- UI responsiveness/correctness: `iface-eventenvelope-encode-cost-8` (shared encoder + direct payload encode, no AnyJSON round-trip), `iface-followup-turnhistory-corrupt-7` (archive the actual prior question via `activeQuery`), `iface-start-precondition-crash-6` (graceful guard, no crashing precondition), `concurrency-livesession-forecastrun-no-deinit-cancel` (deinit cancels streamTask/pollTask), `iface-inline-citations-tokenize-9` (DraftCard throttles the per-token re-tokenize, trailing-edge flush), `iface-sidebar-filter-onbody-10` (memoized per-session search index), `research-console-lazyvstack-offset-id` (stable per-line identity), `iface-html-export-not-markdown-12` (real markdown→HTML, content HTML-escaped), `iface-inspector-autoreveal-once-11` (per-session one-shot auto-reveal), `iface-dropload-mainactor-hop-13` (single drop-ingest hop, errors surfaced).

**Batch 6 — engine correctness + citation/cache integrity (11 findings, building green):**
Hybrid: the coupled engine cluster (ResearchEngine/WorkerAgent/Synthesizer/Reflector) done in the main loop; 4 independent findings fanned out to a background workflow (disjoint files).
- Engine flow: `engine-dedup-truncates-reflection-rounds` (deepening fallbacks now rotate a 9-angle pool by round, so quiet-reflector rounds aren't no-ops), `engine-plan-truncation-silent` (dispatched-question set + overflow queue + warnings — planned subtasks beyond the worker cap are queued, not dropped/marked-asked), `engine-empty-worker-summary-pollutes-synthesis` (Synthesizer + Reflector skip empty-summary workers; their sources still feed the source table), `engine-source-target-unenforced` (`WorkerOutput.sourcesRead`/`hasFindings` + thin-worker warning), `engine-hops-cap-decoupled-from-budget` (maxHops = toolCallBudget+8), `engine-double-error-and-throw` (emit `.error` once + finish clean), `research-tools-serial-toolcalls` (one turn's tool calls run concurrently via TaskGroup, results applied in call order).
- Citation/cache integrity: `research-tools-citation-substring` (exactQuote must be a whitespace-tolerant substring of a real source or it's dropped/URL-repaired), `cache-sourcecachehit-never-emitted-3` (`SourceCache.fetch`→`(source,wasCached)`; tools refund the source slot + emit `.sourceCacheHit` + skip re-charge on a hit), `engine-workerid-collision-risk` (full UUID), `store-duplicate-citations-2` (`replaceCitations` on re-draft).

Fanned out across 10 disjoint files (one agent per file) + 3 re-routed by hand to their true owning files.

## Executive Summary

Swift Deep Research is an ambitious, feature-rich Swift 6.2 orchestrator-worker research app spanning a native engine, a DeerFlow plan-and-execute engine, a Forecast/MiroFish Flask backend driven over HTTP, and a SeekDB FastAPI knowledge sidecar. The audit surfaced 155 findings: 2 P0, 26 P1, 63 P2, 64 P3 (all currently verdict=unverified, so severities should be treated as the auditor's first-pass classification pending spot-checks against the live code). The architecture is sound in shape, but the codebase shows the strain of rapid multi-subsystem growth, and the problems cluster into a small number of high-leverage themes rather than scattering randomly. The biggest health gaps are: (1) the cost-control story is broken end-to-end — the token-budget cap users set in Settings is systematically under-counted at multiple charge sites (Planner/Reflector/CitationExtractor never charge, Anthropic reports 0 prompt tokens, Gemini double-charges, and several charge throws are swallowed by try?), so the maxTokens/maxWallClock guarantees the UI promises are not actually enforced; (2) backend process lifecycle is unsafe — quitting the app or stopping a forecast orphans the DeerFlow/OASIS grandchild process groups (mirofish-supervisor-1, P0), and the willTerminate cleanup is a detached async no-op (mirofish-supervisor-2), so orphaned Python keeps burning API credits indefinitely; (3) the main thread is hammered during streaming — every synthesis token triggers a synchronous SwiftData save plus double JSON-encode on the MainActor (iface-pertoken-save-1, P0), guaranteeing UI jank exactly on the long 'thorough' runs the feature exists for; (4) the LLM-driven fetch tools have no SSRF/scheme guard (research-tools-ssrf, sec-ssrf-fetch-tools-1) and the app ships with App Sandbox disabled and API keys written to a world-readable cleartext .env, a meaningful security posture for an agent that fetches attacker-influenceable web content; and (5) there is no test target at all (no-tests-1) and a 1.1 MB hook-log tree living inside the synchronized Xcode source group (build-health-1) that can cause intermittent build collisions — so build health and a safety net must come first before any of the structural cleanup the rest of the roadmap depends on. Underneath these, three subsystems (native engine vs DeerFlow engine, the AppEnvironment god object, the per-client LLM providers) carry heavy duplication that is the root cause of many correctness findings appearing in 2-3 places at once. The encouraging news: the P0/P1 set is small, concentrated, and mostly S/M effort, so a focused 4-phase plan can de-risk the app quickly and then expand its research capabilities on a stable base.

## Top Risks / Blockers

- mirofish-supervisor-1 (P0): terminate() SIGKILLs only the Flask parent PID; DeerFlow/OASIS grandchildren are reparented to launchd and keep running, burning LLM/API credits indefinitely after the user quits or stops a forecast. The child is never launched in its own process group/session, so killpg cleanup is impossible as written. Pairs with mirofish-supervisor-2.
- iface-pertoken-save-1 (P0): ingest() calls store.appendEvent() -> context.insert + synchronous context.save() for every .tokenDelta/.reasoningDelta on the @MainActor LiveSession, plus a double JSON-encode per event. Thousands of blocking SQLite transactions on the main thread during synthesis cause visible beachballing/jank on exactly the long thorough/multi-round runs the app is built for.
- Token-budget accounting is broken end-to-end, defeating the cost cap users rely on: engine-token-budget-undercount (P1, Planner/Reflector/CitationExtractor never charge), anthropic-prompt-tokens-zero (P1, input tokens never counted), gemini-cumulative-usage-double-charge (P1, streamed usage summed instead of last-wins), plus budget-charge-swallowed-4 and concurrency-budgetmeter-charge-discard (try? swallows the overflow throw). Real spend can exceed the stated 60k/300k/800k caps by a large, unbounded margin.
- SSRF + weak security posture: research-tools-ssrf / sec-ssrf-fetch-tools-1 (P1) — WebReader/PDFReader/Reddit/SourceCache fetch arbitrary LLM-controlled URLs with no scheme/host allowlist, reachable via indirect prompt injection against localhost, file://, link-local metadata, and the unauthenticated local backends (sec-local-backends-unauth-8). Compounded by sec-sandbox-off-2 (App Sandbox disabled, empty entitlements) and sec-env-cleartext-perms-3 (API keys in a world-readable .env).
- mirofish-supervisor-2 (P1): the willTerminate handler fires a detached async Task that cannot complete before the process exits, so the single most reliable cleanup moment (app quit) is a no-op. Combined with the P0 orphaning, quitting routinely leaves the backend and its children alive; the next launch's probeHealth() silently attaches to the stale backend, masking the leak.
- build-health-1 (P1) + no-tests-1 (P1): a 1.1 MB hook-log tree (including aaa/bbb-collisiontest dirs) sits inside the PBXFileSystemSynchronized source group, risking intermittent build-collision failures, AND there is zero test target for any core logic. Every refactor below (engine unification, provider dedup) is high-risk until build health is restored and at least the pure helpers are locked behind tests.
- Wall-clock budget cannot interrupt an in-flight call: engine-wallclock-not-interruptible (P1) plus budget-wallclock-not-monotonic-check-14 — a stuck provider stream or hung WKWebView JS render (research-tools-js-timeout / concurrency-webreader-continuation-leak, P1) blocks a worker indefinitely, consuming the whole run's wall-clock budget and leaking a CheckedContinuation; the maxWallClock cap is best-effort at coarse boundaries only.
- store-source-id-cross-session-1 (P1): StoredSource.id is globally .unique on url but session-scoped semantics are assumed, so re-researching an overlapping URL overwrites another session's stored source text and deleting one session cascade-deletes a source another still references — silent cross-session data corruption. Compounded by store-no-migration-plan-5 (P1, no SchemaMigrationPlan, so any future model edit can brick existing installs).
- deerflow-1 (P1): background-investigation web sources are never turned into FetchedSource objects, so facts from the web sweep are uncitable and may be dropped or hallucinated; for short queries that short-circuit (hasEnoughContext immediately), the entire report can rest on uncited snippet-only context — silently breaking DeerFlow's core cited-report guarantee.

## Cross-Cutting Themes

- **Token/cost-budget enforcement is unreliable at every layer** — The maxTokens and maxWallClock caps surfaced in Settings are not actually enforced. Charge sites are missing (engine-token-budget-undercount: Planner/Reflector/CitationExtractor; deerflow-11: investigation pseudo-worker), provider usage is mis-reported (anthropic-prompt-tokens-zero, gemini-cumulative-usage-double-charge), overflow throws are swallowed (budget-charge-swallowed-4, concurrency-budgetmeter-charge-discard), charges happen after the work is already done (research-tools-charge-after-budget, engine-no-budget-charge-on-failed-llm), and wall-clock is only checked at coarse loop boundaries (engine-wallclock-not-interruptible, budget-wallclock-not-monotonic-check-14). Fix needs a single BudgetMeter threaded through all engines, delta-based usage semantics across providers, and timeout-wrapped LLM/tool calls.
- **Backend (Python sidecar) process lifecycle is unsafe and unrecoverable** — Both the MiroFish and SeekDB supervisors share the same defects: children aren't launched in their own process group/session so they can't be killed as a group (mirofish-supervisor-1), termination on quit is async and never runs (mirofish-supervisor-2), launch coalescing has reentrancy races that spawn duplicates (mirofish-supervisor-3, concurrency-sidecar-ensuretask-clear-race), probes attach to foreign/stale listeners (mirofish-supervisor-4, kb-host-contract-mismatch), and an orphaned process holding the port has no recovery path (kb-port-in-use-no-recovery, kb-watchdog-orphan-window, mirofish-supervisor-7). A single shared, hardened supervisor pattern (setsid + killpg + PID-on-disk reaping + signature-checked health probe + EADDRINUSE recovery) would resolve a whole cluster.
- **Per-token main-thread work during streaming** — Streaming a report does far too much per token on the MainActor: a synchronous SwiftData save + double JSON-encode (iface-pertoken-save-1, iface-eventenvelope-encode-cost-8), an append to a write-only tokenStream array with no consumer (iface-tokenstream-dead-2), full re-tokenization of the draft (iface-inline-citations-tokenize-9), synchronous DB fetches inside SwiftUI body (iface-canvas-fetch-in-body-3), per-keystroke whole-config persistence (settings-persist-every-keystroke), unbounded MainActor-drained event streams (concurrency-unbounded-event-stream-mainactor), and a per-mutation context.save() in the store (store-context-save-per-mutation-13). All compound into UI jank on long runs.
- **Untrusted-input security gaps: SSRF, sandbox, cleartext secrets, missing auth** — The agent fetches LLM/attacker-influenceable URLs with no scheme/host allowlist (research-tools-ssrf, sec-ssrf-fetch-tools-1, sec-reddit-subreddit-injection-7), runs with App Sandbox off and empty entitlements (sec-sandbox-off-2, sec-no-ats-config-9), writes API keys to a world-readable cleartext .env (sec-env-cleartext-perms-3, mirofish-supervisor-6), sends Bearer keys over cleartext http to custom endpoints (sec-custom-endpoint-cleartext-5), exposes unauthenticated local backends with no body-size limit (sec-local-backends-unauth-8, kb-no-request-size-limit), parses untrusted HTML/PDF with no WKWebView resource ceiling (sec-untrusted-html-pdf-parse-10), and uses weak Keychain accessibility flags (sec-keychain-accessibility-4, keychain-accessible-attr-update-path-7). A shared validateFetchableURL() guard plus secret-file hardening addresses most of it.
- **Duplicated scaffolding is the root cause of multi-site bugs** — The native ResearchEngine and DeerFlowEngine duplicate the entire stream/budget/session/error scaffold (engine-dup-1, deerflow-9), fenced-JSON extraction is copy-pasted across Planner/DeerFlowEngine/Reflector/CitationExtractor (json-extract-dup-1), there are two overlapping provider-factory entry points (provider-factory-1), HTTP error-body extraction/status mapping is duplicated per client (shared-error-mapping-duplication-improvement, anthropic-error-body-discarded), and AppEnvironment is a god object spanning four unrelated subsystems (iface-appenv-god-object-5, app-env-god-object-1). Many correctness findings (budget charging, error surfacing, JSON decode brittleness) recur precisely because the logic lives in 2-4 copies.
- **Inconsistent error surfacing and silent failure swallowing** — Errors are handled three different ways: typed EngineFailure in engines vs silent try? vs string outcomes elsewhere (error-handling-consistency-1, engine-double-error-and-throw), Keychain failures are dropped (keychain-set-failure-silent-6, sec-keychain-no-error-surface-11), HTTP error bodies are discarded leaving users with bare status codes (anthropic-error-body-discarded), KB listing/health failures are silent (deerflow-10, kb-health-doc-count-truthiness), search backends silently return [] (research-tools-ddg-fragile, research-tools-empty-set-not-fallthrough), and retry classification relies on brittle lowercased substring matching (retry-enginefailure-string-match-8, gemini-retry-after-server-responded-dead-code). Users get opaque or invisible failures.
- **Citation and source integrity is not actually guaranteed** — The cited-report value proposition is undermined by uncited web sweep sources (deerflow-1), citations that aren't verified to actually appear in the source (research-tools-citation-substring), duplicated citations across reflection rounds (store-duplicate-citations-2), cross-session source overwrite (store-source-id-cross-session-1), and accumulated sources never threaded into later-round workers (native-engine-dedup-gap-1, engine-cache-not-used-for-cross-turn). Source provenance needs to be made first-class and verifiable.

## Quick Wins (do first)

- [ ] build-health-1: move/exclude the 1.1 MB hook-log tree (and delete aaa/bbb-collisiontest dirs) out of the synchronized source group via PBXFileSystemSynchronizedBuildFileExceptionSet — removes intermittent build collisions before any other work (S).
- [ ] iface-pertoken-save-1 (P0): stop persisting .tokenDelta/.reasoningDelta as individual StoredEvents (they carry no durable value — the final draft is already persisted) or batch+debounce the save; eliminates the worst main-thread stall for a tiny diff (S).
- [ ] iface-tokenstream-dead-2: delete the write-only tokenStream property/append/reset entirely — pure dead-work removal, O(tokens) saved per run (S).
- [ ] engine-token-budget-undercount (P1): thread the BudgetMeter into Planner/Reflector/CitationExtractor and charge after each completion, mirroring WorkerAgent — restores most of the cost cap with a few try? await budget.chargeTokens(...) lines (S).
- [ ] anthropic-prompt-tokens-zero + gemini-cumulative-usage-double-charge (P1): read input_tokens from Anthropic message_start, and track last-seen (not summed) usage in the Gemini/synthesis stream path — two small provider fixes that stop the largest budget undercounts (S).
- [ ] gemini-apikey-unescaped-url (P1): build the Gemini URL with URLComponents/queryItems or switch to the x-goog-api-key header (same fix in ModelDiscovery) — removes a key-exposure/malformed-URL hazard (S).
- [ ] sec-env-cleartext-perms-3 (P1): FileManager.setAttributes [.posixPermissions: 0o600] on the .env (and its temp/dir) after writing secrets — closes world-readable-secret exposure (S).
- [ ] store-source-id-cross-session-1 (P1): make StoredSource identity a composite session.id|url key (or add the session predicate to upsert) — stops silent cross-session source overwrite/cascade-delete corruption (S).
- [x] research-tools-calculator-e: stop globally replacing 'e' -> 2.718 in the calculator so scientific notation / tokens aren't corrupted (S).
- [x] kb-delete-id-not-escaped: percent-encode the document id before interpolating it into the DELETE URL path (S).
- [x] settings-persist-every-keystroke: debounce the onChange(of: env.configuration) persistence instead of writing the whole config on every keystroke (S).

## Improvement Roadmap (phased)

### Phase 1: Phase 0 — Build health + safety net (de-risks everything after)
*Goal:* Make the project build deterministically and establish a regression net before any structural change.

- [ ] Resolve build-health-1: relocate or exclude logs/ from the synchronized source group; delete collisiontest dirs.
- [ ] Add the missing test target (no-tests-1): Swift Testing target covering pure helpers — fenced-JSON extraction, EngineConfiguration round-trip + legacy-blob decode, resolveOllamaModel, openAIBase, fetchedSources normalization across all payload shapes, Synthesizer source dedup. No network needed; locks in current behavior.
- [ ] Address untracked-backend-trees-1: decide gitignore vs submodule vs vendored policy for deer-flow/MiroFish/src/frontend/backend so CI and other machines build consistently.
- [ ] Run a clean build + smoke run to confirm green baseline; capture as the Phase-0 evidence.

### Phase 2: Phase 1 — P0 correctness & lifecycle (stop the bleeding)
*Goal:* Eliminate the two P0s and the credit-burning backend orphaning so the app is safe to run for long sessions.

- [ ] mirofish-supervisor-1 (P0): launch Python children in their own session/process group (posix_spawn POSIX_SPAWN_SETSID / setsid launcher) and terminate via killpg(SIGTERM) then SIGKILL after a grace window.
- [ ] mirofish-supervisor-2 (P1): perform an inline synchronous killpg in the applicationWillTerminate path; write child PID to disk for next-launch reaping. Apply the same hardened pattern to SidecarSupervisor (kb-port-in-use-no-recovery, kb-host-contract-mismatch).
- [ ] iface-pertoken-save-1 (P0): stop per-token synchronous SwiftData saves (skip or batch/debounce); fix store-context-save-per-mutation-13 in the same pass.
- [ ] mirofish-supervisor-3/-4 (P1): fix the launch-coalescing reentrancy race and make probeHealth require the MiroFish service signature, not any HTTP 200.

### Phase 3: Phase 2 — Budget enforcement + security hardening (make the guarantees real)
*Goal:* Make the cost caps and the security posture actually hold under untrusted input.

- [ ] Budget cluster: engine-token-budget-undercount, anthropic-prompt-tokens-zero, gemini-cumulative-usage-double-charge, budget-charge-swallowed-4, concurrency-budgetmeter-charge-discard, deerflow-11 — centralize charging, use delta-based usage, stop swallowing overflow throws.
- [ ] Wall-clock interruption: engine-wallclock-not-interruptible, budget-wallclock-not-monotonic-check-14, research-tools-js-timeout, concurrency-webreader-continuation-leak — wrap every llm.complete/stream and tool call in a ContinuousClock/Task-race timeout; resume-exactly-once continuations.
- [ ] SSRF guard: add shared validateFetchableURL() used by WebReader/PDFReader/Reddit/SourceCache (research-tools-ssrf, sec-ssrf-fetch-tools-1, sec-reddit-subreddit-injection-7), re-validated after redirects.
- [ ] Secret + endpoint hardening: sec-env-cleartext-perms-3, sec-custom-endpoint-cleartext-5 (reject cleartext http for keyed endpoints), sec-keychain-accessibility-4, keychain-set-failure-silent-6.
- [ ] Decide on sec-sandbox-off-2 / sec-local-backends-unauth-8 / kb-no-request-size-limit: either enable App Sandbox with minimal entitlements + a local-backend auth token + body-size cap, or document the threat model with the SSRF guard as the compensating control.

### Phase 4: Phase 3 — Robustness, correctness, and UI responsiveness (P1/P2 cleanup)
*Goal:* Close the remaining correctness/robustness gaps and remove main-thread stalls so long runs stay responsive and reports stay trustworthy.

- [ ] Storage/migration: store-no-migration-plan-5 (VersionedSchema + SchemaMigrationPlan + destroy-and-recreate fallback), store-source-id-cross-session-1, store-duplicate-citations-2, event-id-hashvalue-instability-10.
- [ ] Citation integrity: deerflow-1 (web sweep -> real FetchedSources), research-tools-citation-substring (verify exactQuote), native-engine-dedup-gap-1 / engine-cache-not-used-for-cross-turn (thread accumulated sources into later rounds).
- [ ] Provider stream robustness: gemini-unconditional-finish-stop, openai-compat-done-after-finish-double-terminal, openai-compat-toolcallend-not-emitted-on-finish, anthropic-error-body-discarded + shared error-mapping, retry-enginefailure-string-match-8.
- [ ] UI responsiveness: iface-eventenvelope-encode-cost-8, iface-inline-citations-tokenize-9, iface-canvas-fetch-in-body-3, concurrency-unbounded-event-stream-mainactor, graph-force-sim-reseed-on-poll, settings-persist-every-keystroke.
- [ ] Backend polling/streaming + KB integrity: mirofish-run-1 (SSE/incremental), mirofish-run-2/-4, kb-journal-vector-drift, kb-global-lock-serializes-query, kb-implicit-embedding-fn.

### Phase 5: Phase 4 — Architecture consolidation (pay down the duplication root cause)
*Goal:* Collapse the duplicated scaffolding so future changes touch one place, not three.

- [ ] Unify the engine scaffold: engine-dup-1 / deerflow-9 — extract shared stream/budget/session/error machinery used by both ResearchEngine and DeerFlowEngine.
- [ ] Centralize shared helpers: json-extract-dup-1 (single fenced-JSON extractor), provider-factory-1 (one factory), shared-error-mapping-duplication-improvement (one HTTP error mapper).
- [ ] Decompose AppEnvironment: iface-appenv-god-object-5 / app-env-god-object-1 — split research, forecast, backend-pipeline, env-sync, key-status into focused @Observable coordinators.
- [ ] Clarify the three DeerFlow concepts: forecast-deerflow-confusion-1 — rename to disambiguate in-app engine vs backend vs forecast role.
- [ ] Standardize error surfacing: error-handling-consistency-1 — one typed-error + UI-event convention across engines and stores.

## Net-New Capability Ideas (make it more powerful & comprehensive)

- [ ] **Incremental/SSE streaming for backend forecast updates instead of 2s full re-fetch polling** _(effort L)_ — mirofish-run-1 shows the forecast loop re-fetches the whole research log + graph + report every tick; an SSE/incremental channel would slash latency and load, make the live graph smoother (graph-force-sim-reseed-on-poll), and surface backend errors immediately (mirofish-run-2). This is the foundation for a responsive, real-time research-progress UI.
- [ ] **First-class, verifiable source provenance ledger shared across engines and sessions** _(effort L)_ — Make FetchedSource the single source of truth, dedup it per-session correctly (store-source-id-cross-session-1), verify every citation's exactQuote against the stored text (research-tools-citation-substring), include web-sweep hits (deerflow-1), and thread accumulated sources into later rounds (native-engine-dedup-gap-1, engine-cache-not-used-for-cross-turn). Turns the cited-report promise into an enforced guarantee and unlocks reliable export.
- [ ] **Expand the research toolset: news/recency, Semantic Scholar/arXiv-rich, GitHub/code, and batch-fetch tools** _(effort L)_ — research-tools-capability-gaps notes the agent has no dedicated recency, scholarly, or code-search tool and no batch fetch; adding these (plus parallelizing same-turn tool calls per research-tools-serial-toolcalls and a readability/main-content extractor per research-tools-no-readability) materially broadens what the agent can comprehensively research and how fast.
- [ ] **A real cost/budget dashboard with live per-stage token + wall-clock spend and a hard kill-switch** _(effort M)_ — Once budget accounting is fixed (Phase 2), expose it: a live readout of tokens/$ per planner/worker/synthesis stage, projected spend vs cap, and an interruptible hard stop (engine-wallclock-not-interruptible). Converts the now-accurate BudgetMeter into a user-facing capability and builds trust for long thorough runs.
- [ ] **Deeper, query-driven knowledge-base integration with OCR and concurrent read/write** _(effort L)_ — Add OCR for scanned PDFs (kb-pdf-no-ocr-no-error-on-empty-pages), readers-writer locking so queries aren't blocked by ingestion (kb-global-lock-serializes-query), batched ingestion (kb-no-ingest-batching), and wire the unused /documents/bulk endpoint (kb-bulk-endpoint-unused). Makes the KB a robust, fast retrieval substrate the research loop can lean on for grounded, comprehensive answers.
- [ ] **Resumable/recoverable runs with crash + orphan reaping and a job registry** _(effort L)_ — Building on the hardened supervisors (Phase 1), persist run state and child PIDs so a crashed/quit app can reap orphaned backends and resume an in-flight forecast or research run (mirofish-run-3, kb-port-in-use-no-recovery). Adds durability that makes long multi-round research practical and trustworthy across app restarts.
- [ ] **Pluggable engine + provider registry with capability discovery and graceful fallback** _(effort M)_ — After unifying the engine scaffold (Phase 4) and provider factory (provider-factory-1), formalize a registry where engines/providers declare capabilities (streaming, tool-calls, token-usage reporting) so the orchestrator can pick the best path and fall back cleanly — enabling new models/engines to be added without touching budget, error, or session plumbing.
- [ ] **Multi-perspective / adversarial verification pass over draft reports before finalize** _(effort L)_ — Add an optional fan-out/fan-in verification stage that cross-checks claims against the cited sources and flags unsupported statements, leaning on the now-verifiable citation ledger. Directly increases report robustness and comprehensiveness, and naturally extends the existing reflection loop (engine-dedup-truncates-reflection-rounds context).

## Findings Catalog (verified)

Grouped by severity, then subsystem. `[v]` = verdict (confirmed/adjusted). Effort S/M/L/XL, confidence in parens.

### P0 — Critical (1)


#### Core SwiftUI Interface

- [x] **`iface-pertoken-save-1`** — Synchronous SwiftData save() on the MainActor for every streamed token  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/AppEnvironment.swift:501-511 (ingest) → Storage/ResearchStore.swift:165-181 (appendEvent)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** ingest(_:) unconditionally calls store.appendEvent(...) at the top of every event, including .tokenDelta (one event per streamed synthesis token) and .reasoningDelta (one per worker reasoning token). appendEvent does context.insert + context.save() — a full synchronous Core Data/SwiftData transaction — and ingest first JSON-encodes the event via EventEnvelope. The whole LiveSession is @MainActor, so for a 'thorough' run streaming thousands of tokens this is thousands of blocking saves + encodes on the main thread.  
  - **Impact:** Main thread stalls and visible UI jank/beachballing during synthesis streaming on longer runs; the SQLite WAL is hammered with one tiny transaction per token; the draft text appears to stutter because each token both mutates draftMarkdown (re-render) and blocks on a disk write. Worse with thorough/multi-round modes — exactly when users expect the app to stay responsive.  
  - **Fix:** Stop persisting token/reasoning deltas as individual StoredEvents. Either (a) skip appendEvent for .tokenDelta/.reasoningDelta entirely (they carry no durable value — the final draft is already persisted via draftReady/updateTurnMarkdown), or (b) batch events and flush with a single save() on a debounce/throttle (e.g. every N events or 250ms) and on stream completion. Add an `isHighFrequency` switch in ingest before calling appendEvent.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. ingest() (AppEnvironment.swift:502-510) unconditionally JSON-encodes an EventEnvelope and calls store.appendEvent at the top of EVERY event, before the switch. appendEvent (ResearchStore.swift:170-179) does context.insert + try context.save() — a full synchronous SwiftData save. Synthesizer.swift:98 emits .tokenDelta per streamed chunk, and ResearchEvent.tokenDelta flows through ingest, so a long synthesis fires thousands of blocking context.save() calls on the @MainActor (ResearchStore is @MainActor, LiveSession is @MainActor). A fresh JSONEncoder is also allocated per event (line 503). Nuance: reasoningDelta is per tool-loop iteration (WorkerAgent.swift:135 uses non-streaming llm.complete), NOT per token, so the per-token volume comes overwhelmingly from synthesis — but that alone justifies P0 for a 'thorough' run. The core claim (synchronous main-thread save per streamed synthesis token) is fully accurate.  

### P1 — High (21)


#### Core Research Engine (native flow)

- [x] **`engine-token-budget-undercount`** — Planner, Reflector, and CitationExtractor never charge tokens — token budget is systematically under-enforced  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/Planner.swift:61; Reflector.swift:116; ResearchTools/CitationExtractor.swift:54; ResearchEngine.swift:104-220`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Only WorkerAgent.complete (WorkerAgent.swift:131) and Synthesizer streaming usage (Synthesizer.swift:100) call budget.chargeTokens. Planner.plan, Reflector.critique, and CitationExtractor.extract each issue full LLM calls via llm.complete/llm.stream but never charge the BudgetMeter. In a thorough 6-round run that is 1 planner call + up to 5 reflector calls + 6 citation-extraction passes (one per synthesis) whose tokens are invisible to the meter. The reflector and citation passes in particular feed large prompts (8000-char draft, 4000-char-per-source corpus).  
  - **Impact:** The maxTokens cap (60k/300k/800k) that the user sets in Settings to bound cost is materially under-counted — real spend can exceed the cap by a large and unbounded margin, and sessionCompleted reports a token total far below actual usage. The cost/quality tradeoff the AgentBudget doc comment promises is not actually enforced for orchestrator-side work.  
  - **Fix:** Thread the BudgetMeter into Planner, Reflector, and CitationExtractor and call try? await budget.chargeTokens(completion.totalTokens) after each completion (mirror WorkerAgent.swift:131). For the citation extractor's streamed/complete path, charge promptTokens+completionTokens. Optionally make orchestrator-side charges non-throwing (try?) so they count toward the snapshot without aborting mid-synthesis.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. WorkerAgent.swift:131 calls budget.chargeTokens(completion.totalTokens) and Synthesizer.swift:100 charges on .usage chunks. Planner.plan (Planner.swift:61 calls retry.run{ llm.complete } with no chargeTokens anywhere in the file), Reflector.critique (Reflector.swift:116 `let completion = try await llm.complete(req)` with no chargeTokens in the file), and CitationExtractor.extract (CitationExtractor.swift:54 `let completion = try await llm.complete(req)` with no chargeTokens) all issue full LLM calls that never touch the BudgetMeter. grep for chargeTokens across the engine confirms only WorkerAgent, Synthesizer, DeerFlowEngine, and tool-context charges exist — none in Planner/Reflector/CitationExtractor. A thorough 6-round run is 1 planner + up to 5 reflector + 6 citation passes uncounted, and reflector feeds an 8000-char draft (Reflector.swift:110) while citation feeds 4000-char-per-source corpus (CitationExtractor.swift:23). P1 is fair: systematic under-enforcement of the token cap that the budget exists to enforce.  
- [x] **`engine-wallclock-not-interruptible`** — Wall-clock budget cannot interrupt an in-progress synthesis or worker LLM call  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/Synthesizer.swift:93-104; Domain/AgentBudget.swift:101-107; WorkerAgent.swift:121-131`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** checkWallClock is only consulted at loop boundaries: once per worker hop (WorkerAgent.swift:126) and once per reflection round (ResearchEngine.swift:142). The synthesizer's streaming loop (Synthesizer.swift:94) never checks the wall clock, and a single llm.complete call inside a worker hop has no internal timeout. With local Ollama/gpt-oss synthesis explicitly noted to run 4-8 minutes (AgentBudget.swift:27-31), a single hung or very slow streaming call can run far past maxWallClock with no enforcement, because the cap is only re-evaluated after the call returns.  
  - **Impact:** The maxWallClock guarantee (180/900/1800s) is best-effort at coarse boundaries only. A stuck provider stream blocks the run indefinitely (only user cancel or the URLSession timeout stops it), and a slow synthesis can blow well past the stated cap. Pathological hangs the cap is meant to bound are not actually bounded.  
  - **Fix:** Wrap each llm.complete/llm.stream consumption in a timeout (e.g. a Task race against Task.sleep(for: remaining wall-clock budget), or ContinuousClock deadline) so a single call cannot outlive the budget. At minimum add a checkWallClock() inside the Synthesizer stream loop and cancel the stream when it trips.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. checkWallClock is consulted only at loop boundaries: WorkerAgent.swift:125 once per hop before the llm.complete call, and ResearchEngine.swift:142 once per reflection round. The synthesizer streaming loop (Synthesizer.swift:94-104) never calls checkWallClock — it only loops on chunks accumulating draft and charging usage. LLMRequest passes no per-call timeout (WorkerAgent.swift:129 builds LLMRequest with messages/tools/temperature only). With local Ollama synthesis documented at 4-8 minutes (AgentBudget.swift:27-31) and fast-mode cap at 180s, a single hung/slow streaming completion runs past maxWallClock with no enforcement because the cap is re-checked only after the call returns. P1 justified for a robustness cap that cannot bound the longest-running operation.  

#### DeerFlow Plan-and-Execute Engine

- [x] **`deerflow-1`** — Background-investigation web sources are dropped from citations and the report  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/DeerFlowEngine.swift:297-322 (backgroundInvestigation) and 229-240 (asWorkerOutput)`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** In backgroundInvestigation, the knowledge_base outcome appends decoded sources via `sources.append(contentsOf: Self.decodeFetchedSources(payload))` (line 292), but the web_search outcome only appends to `sections` (markdown text, lines 309-311) and NEVER to `sources`. Worse, the web_search payload is a `SearchResultsPayload` of titles/URLs/snippets (WebSearchTool.swift:75) — not `FetchedSource` objects with extracted body text — and the investigation never calls fetch_url on any of them. So `Investigation.sources` contains only KB hits. `asWorkerOutput` exposes exactly those sources to the synthesizer, whose source table and CitationExtractor are built from `workerOutputs.flatMap(\.sources)` (Synthesizer.swift:37). The planner sees the web findings in markdown, but the reporter cannot cite any web source discovered during the investigation stage.  
  - **Impact:** Any fact the planner surfaces purely from the background web sweep is uncitable and may be dropped or hallucinated by the reporter, since the synthesizer is instructed to cite only from the numbered source list. For short queries where hasEnoughContext becomes true immediately (no steps run), the entire report rests on snippet-only, uncited web context. This silently degrades the cited-report guarantee that is DeerFlow's whole value proposition.  
  - **Fix:** Either (a) have the investigation fetch_url the top 1-2 web results so they become real FetchedSource objects, or (b) decode the SearchResultsPayload into lightweight FetchedSource entries (title+url+snippet as extractedText) and append them to `sources`. Mirror the KB branch: append decoded sources for the web branch too. This puts investigation web hits into the synthesizer's numbered source table and citation extraction.  
  - **Verify:** Verified end-to-end. In backgroundInvestigation, the KB branch appends decoded sources via `sources.append(contentsOf: Self.decodeFetchedSources(payload))` (DeerFlowEngine.swift:292), and KnowledgeBaseTool's payload carries a `fetchedSources` key (KnowledgeBaseTool.swift:89,153) that decodeFetchedSources reads (DeerFlowEngine.swift:483-487). The web_search branch ONLY does `sections.append(...)` (lines 309-311) and never touches `sources`. WebSearchTool returns a `SearchResultsPayload{query,provider,results:[DiscoveredSource]}` (WebSearchTool.swift:75-82,105-109) — titles/URLs/snippets, not FetchedSource bodies — and the investigation never calls fetch_url. `asWorkerOutput` (lines 229-240) exposes only `sources` (KB-only) to the synthesizer, whose sourceTable, orderedSources, and citationExtractor are all built from `workerOutputs.flatMap(\.sources)` (Synthesizer.swift:37,41-44,106). So web findings discovered during the investigation appear in the planner's markdown but are uncitable in the report unless a later worker independently re-fetches them. Real correctness gap; P1 is fair.  

#### Forecast / MiroFish Backend Integration

- [x] **`mirofish-supervisor-1`** — terminate() kills only the Flask PID, orphaning DeerFlow/OASIS child process groups  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:121-130 (terminate); launch:422-461`  
  - **Category:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** launch() spawns run.py via Process with no process-group / new-session setup (confirmed: no setpgid/setsid/posix_spawn flags anywhere in the file). terminate() does `process.terminate()` (SIGTERM to the single PID) then `kill(process.processIdentifier, SIGKILL)`. The backend itself fans out to DeerFlow and OASIS as its own child process groups (the cancelPipeline doc at MiroFishClient.swift:99-101 explicitly says the backend 'kills the DeerFlow/OASIS child process groups'). When we SIGKILL only the Flask parent, those grandchildren are reparented to launchd and keep running — burning LLM tokens and holding the port's downstream resources.  
  - **Impact:** On app quit (or any supervisor-initiated stop) a forecast that was mid-research/mid-simulation leaves orphaned Python processes consuming API credits indefinitely and possibly re-binding nothing useful. SIGKILL also prevents the backend's own graceful child-cleanup from ever running.  
  - **Fix:** Launch the child in its own process group/session (set a posix_spawn POSIX_SPAWN_SETSID attribute, or set process.environment plus a small launcher that calls setsid, or on macoS use Process with a custom executable that does setsid). On stop, signal the whole group with killpg(getpgid(pid), SIGTERM) then SIGKILL after a grace window. Prefer SIGTERM-the-group so the backend can run its own DeerFlow/OASIS teardown; only escalate to killpg(SIGKILL) on timeout.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed the mechanism: grep for setpgid/setsid/posix_spawn/killpg in MiroFishSupervisor.swift returns nothing, so launch() (422-461) spawns run.py via Foundation.Process which inherits the parent's process group (no new session/group). terminate() (121-130) calls process.terminate() then kill(process.processIdentifier, SIGKILL) — both target only the single Flask PID, not a group, so backend-spawned DeerFlow/OASIS grandchildren (documented as separate child process groups at MiroFishClient.swift:99-101) would be orphaned to launchd. Real bug. BUT severity is overstated as P0: (a) the in-app cancel path (ForecastRun.cancel ->client.cancelPipeline, ForecastRun.swift:128-138) correctly delegates child-killing to the backend, so this only bites on hard app-quit mid-pipeline; (b) per finding -2 the willTerminate Task that calls terminate() usually never even runs before exit, so the SIGKILL frequently doesn't fire at all. It is a conditional token/resource leak, not a crash/data-loss class issue. Downgraded to P1.  
- [x] **`mirofish-supervisor-2`** — willTerminate handler fires a detached async Task that cannot finish before the app exits  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:49-56 (registerTermination), 121-130 (terminate)`  
  - **Category:** concurrency · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** registerTermination observes NSApplication.willTerminateNotification and runs `Task { await MiroFishSupervisor.shared.terminate() }`. willTerminate is delivered synchronously on the main thread immediately before the process exits; AppKit does not wait for unstructured Tasks. terminate() then awaits up to 20*50ms = 1s of polling sleeps. The app process is almost always gone before that Task is even scheduled, let alone before the SIGTERM->grace->SIGKILL sequence completes.  
  - **Impact:** The single most reliable moment to clean up the backend (app quit) is effectively a no-op in the common case. Combined with finding #1, this means quitting the app routinely leaves the MiroFish backend (and its orphaned children) alive. On next launch probeHealth() will attach to the stale backend, masking the leak.  
  - **Fix:** Do termination synchronously in an applicationWillTerminate path: signal the process group from the notification callback itself (killpg is sync and safe to call inline), or use a synchronous best-effort kill before returning from the observer. Keep the async graceful path for in-app stops, but guarantee at least an inline SIGTERM/SIGKILL to the group on quit. Consider also writing the child PID to disk so a future launch can reap a known-orphaned backend.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. registerTermination() (49-56) observes NSApplication.willTerminateNotification and runs `Task { await MiroFishSupervisor.shared.terminate() }`. grep confirms terminate() has no other synchronous caller (no applicationWillTerminate delegate awaits it). willTerminate is delivered on the main thread immediately before process exit, and AppKit does not wait for unstructured Tasks; terminate() (121-130) then awaits up to 20*50ms sleeps. The Task is very likely not scheduled-to-completion before the process is gone. P1 is appropriate for an unreliable shutdown path.  

#### Knowledge Base / SeekDB sidecar

- [x] **`kb-host-contract-mismatch`** — Supervisor launches sidecar with only --port; configured host/data-dir/collection are ignored  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/SidecarSupervisor.swift:288 (process.arguments) vs sidecar/seekdb_sidecar.py:481-505`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** launch() builds `process.arguments = [python, script.path, "--port", String(port)]` — it never passes `--host`, `--data-dir`, `--database`, or `--collection`. The Python defaults to binding 127.0.0.1 and a fixed data dir/collection. But the client probes whatever `seekdbHost` the user configured (SettingsSheet.swift:1218 lets the user set any http/https host). If a user sets seekdbHost to a LAN IP or 0.0.0.0, the supervisor still binds the sidecar to 127.0.0.1, so probeHealth() against the configured host fails forever and the KB looks permanently 'unreachable' even though a process is running. The port IS derived from the host (resolvedPort = port ?? host.port ?? 9100), so host and port can desync.  
  - **Impact:** Any non-default seekdbHost (different interface or even a hostname that resolves to a non-loopback address) makes the auto-launched sidecar permanently unreachable, with no error explaining why — the process is alive but bound to the wrong interface.  
  - **Fix:** Pass `--host` derived from the configured URL's host to Python, and plumb data-dir/database/collection through if they are ever configurable. Minimally, extract host and pass it: `process.arguments = [python, script.path, "--host", host.host ?? "127.0.0.1", "--port", String(port)]`. Validate in commitHost() that only loopback hosts are accepted for the auto-launch path, or document that non-loopback requires manual launch.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. SidecarSupervisor.launch() line 288: `process.arguments = [python, script.path, "--port", String(port)]` — only --port is passed; --host/--data-dir/--database/--collection are never forwarded. The sidecar defaults to --host 127.0.0.1 (seekdb_sidecar.py:481) and binds via uvicorn.run(host=args.host) (line 517). resolvedPort = port ?? host.port ?? 9100 (SidecarSupervisor.swift:106) so the port DOES track the host but the bind address does not. SettingsSheet.commitHost() (lines 1210-1218) validates only scheme==http/https and url.host != nil, so a user can set seekdbHost to any LAN IP. In that case probeHealth(host:) (line 476-481) hits the configured host while the process is bound to 127.0.0.1 → permanent 'unreachable'. The data-dir/collection mismatch is benign in practice because the Swift default host's path/collection match the Python defaults; the real defect is the host bind desync. Confirmed; P1 defensible because the UI exposes the host knob and the failure is silent. Minor: pointing a locally-spawned process at a remote host is an unusual config, but the gap is genuine.  
- [ ] **`kb-port-in-use-no-recovery`** — Orphaned sidecar holding the port is never detected or recovered  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/SidecarSupervisor.swift:249-258 (needsDependencies), 268-272 (statusAfterExit); sidecar/seekdb_sidecar.py:517 (uvicorn.run)`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** If a previous sidecar is orphaned (debugger stop, the 2s watchdog window in seekdb_sidecar.py:473, or a stale process from a prior crash), uvicorn.run() exits non-zero with 'address already in use'. The supervisor's `needsDependencies` matcher (lines 249-258) does not match that string, so launchAndWait → statusAfterExit returns `.failed`. There is no logic to detect a port conflict, find/kill the orphan, or probe whether the existing listener is in fact a healthy sidecar of ours. probeHealth would actually succeed against a healthy orphan, so the fast path masks it — but a wedged/half-dead orphan that holds the port but doesn't answer /health bricks the KB for the session.  
  - **Impact:** A stale sidecar holding 9100 but not answering /health permanently blocks new launches; the user sees '.failed' with a raw uvicorn traceback and no path to recovery short of killing the process manually in Terminal — the exact 'orphan + port binding' failure the design claims to prevent.  
  - **Fix:** On a launch failure whose captured output contains 'address already in use'/EADDRINUSE, attempt a recovery: probe /health one more time (adopt a healthy listener), else locate and SIGTERM the process bound to the port (e.g. `lsof -ti tcp:PORT` then kill) before relaunching. Add an EADDRINUSE branch to statusAfterExit returning a distinct `.failed` with actionable text.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. needsDependencies (SidecarSupervisor.swift:249-258) matches only modulenotfounderror/no module named/importerror/missing dependencies/is not installed/pip install/[exit 2] — it does NOT match 'address already in use' or errno 48. statusAfterExit() (line 268-272) therefore returns .failed for a port conflict, with no logic to detect the conflict, find/kill an orphan, or probe an existing listener. Grep confirms no lsof/pkill/EADDRINUSE handling in SidecarSupervisor. Notably MiroFishSupervisor.swift:492 DOES handle `lower.contains("address already in use") || lower.contains("errno 48")` for the sibling sidecar, proving this is a recognized failure mode left unhandled here. The finding correctly notes the fast-path probeHealth (line 77/92) masks a *healthy* orphan of ours, so the unrecovered case is a wedged orphan that holds the port but doesn't answer /health — that bricks the KB for the session. P1 confirmed.  

#### LLM Provider Layer

- [x] **`anthropic-error-body-discarded`** — Anthropic HTTP errors discard the response body — users see only "HTTP 4xx"  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/AnthropicClient.swift:73,270-279`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `checkOK(response:snippet:)` is called with `snippet: nil` and never drains the `bytes` async stream, so for any non-2xx the user gets `"Anthropic HTTP 400"` (or 401/429/...) with no detail and no plain-language guidance. The OpenAICompatibleClient does this right (drains up to 1200 chars and maps 401/402/429/404/422/5xx to actionable messages via `httpError`). Anthropic 400s are common here precisely because of model-specific `max_tokens` ceilings and tool-schema issues this very file tries to manage.  
  - **Impact:** When an Anthropic request is rejected (bad model id, max_tokens too high, malformed tool schema, expired/over-limit key) the user gets an opaque status code with no remediation, making misconfiguration very hard to diagnose. No 401/429 special-casing either.  
  - **Fix:** Before/inside `checkOK`, drain a bounded prefix of `bytes` (e.g. accumulate up to ~1500 bytes) on non-2xx, parse Anthropic's `{"error":{"message":...}}` shape, and reuse a shared error-mapping helper (factor out `OpenAICompatibleClient.httpError` / `extractErrorMessage` into the shared layer) so all REST clients map status codes uniformly.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed: stream() calls `checkOK(response: response, snippet: nil)` (line 73) and the bytes async stream is never drained on the error path; checkOK (270-279) throws bare `Anthropic HTTP <code>` with underlying=nil. No body, no actionable guidance. OpenAICompatibleClient drains up to 1200 chars (lines 90-94) and maps 401/402/429/404/400/422/5xx to plain-language messages via httpError (175-206). Anthropic 400s are common here (model-specific max_tokens ceilings, tool-schema issues). P1 defensible given frequency and that the user is left with no diagnostic.  
- [x] **`anthropic-prompt-tokens-zero`** — Anthropic always reports promptTokens = 0 (budget undercounts input)  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/AnthropicClient.swift:256-259`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The SSE handler only reads usage from the `message_delta` event (`output_tokens`) and yields `.usage(promptTokens: 0, completionTokens: out)`. Anthropic delivers `input_tokens` in the `message_start` event's `usage` object, which is never parsed. So every Anthropic call reports zero prompt tokens. WorkerAgent charges `completion.totalTokens` (LLMClient.swift:95 = prompt+completion) against BudgetMeter, and Anthropic input tokens dominate cost in a long research run (large system prompts + tool outputs + history).  
  - **Impact:** Token budget accounting systematically under-counts Anthropic usage by the (often largest) input-token share, so per-worker/per-run token caps don't fire when they should and cost reporting is wrong. Long runs can blow far past the intended token budget.  
  - **Fix:** Handle `message_start`: read `obj["message"]["usage"]["input_tokens"]` and remember it; emit it in the `message_delta` usage chunk (or emit a separate `.usage(promptTokens: input, completionTokens: 0)` at start). Anthropic also reports `cache_read_input_tokens`/`cache_creation_input_tokens` worth folding in.  
  - **Verify:** AnthropicClient.handle (lines 229-267) has no `message_start` case; only `message_delta` reads usage and yields `.usage(promptTokens: 0, completionTokens: out)` (line 259). `input_tokens` from message_start is never parsed. WorkerAgent.swift:131 charges `completion.totalTokens` and complete() (LLMClient.swift:46-75, totalTokens = prompt+completion, line 95) assigns usage last-wins, so Anthropic input tokens (large system prompts + tool outputs + history) are never charged to BudgetMeter. Real budget undercount on the dominant token path. P1 stands.  
- [x] **`gemini-apikey-unescaped-url`** — Gemini API key & model id interpolated unescaped into URL query string  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/GeminiClient.swift:46-52`  
  - **Category:** security · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The endpoint is built by raw string concatenation: `baseURL.absoluteString + "/v1beta/models/\(modelID):streamGenerateContent?alt=sse&key=\(apiKey)"`, then `URL(string:)`. Two problems: (1) the API key is a query parameter — query strings are the most likely thing to be captured by proxies, server access logs, and crash/telemetry, unlike a header; (2) neither `apiKey` nor `modelID` is percent-encoded, so any URL-reserved character (`+`, `&`, `/`, space, etc.) silently corrupts the request or makes `URL(string:)` return nil → a confusing "Bad Gemini URL" error rather than the real cause.  
  - **Impact:** Key exposure surface is broader than header-based auth, and certain keys/model ids fail to construct a valid URL or send a malformed request. Discovery (ModelDiscovery.fetchGemini) has the identical pattern at line 133.  
  - **Fix:** Build the URL with URLComponents and `queryItems` (which percent-encode), or better, prefer Gemini's supported `x-goog-api-key` header instead of `?key=`. Percent-encode `modelID` in the path. Apply the same fix in ModelDiscovery.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed at GeminiClient.swift:46-52: `baseURL.absoluteString + "/v1beta/models/\(modelID):streamGenerateContent?alt=sse&key=\(apiKey)"` then `URL(string:)`. Neither apiKey nor modelID is percent-encoded; key is in the query string (logged/proxied more readily than a header). Any URL-reserved char in key/model corrupts the request or makes URL(string:) return nil -> the misleading `Bad Gemini URL` error (line 50). Both sub-claims accurate.  

#### Research Tools

- [x] **`research-tools-js-timeout`** — WKWebView JS render has no overall timeout — a hung navigation stalls the worker  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/WebReaderTool.swift:165-215`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `HiddenWebView.load` suspends on a `CheckedContinuation` that is only resumed by `didFinish`/`didFail`/`didFailProvisionalNavigation`. The only bound is the `URLRequest(timeoutInterval: 30)` on line 168, but WKWebView frequently does NOT deliver a delegate callback within that interval for SPAs that keep an XHR/websocket open, infinite redirects, or pages that finish provisional load but never fire didFinish. There is no `Task` timeout wrapping `extractJavaScript`, no per-tool timeout in WorkerAgent (tool calls run inline, WorkerAgent.swift:172), and the cache `inFlight` task (SourceCache.swift:39) is non-cancellable from outside. A single bad URL can therefore burn the entire `maxWallClock` budget (up to 1800s thorough) on one worker.  
  - **Impact:** One pathological JS-rendered page can hang a worker indefinitely, consuming the whole run's wall-clock budget and starving the rest of the research; the continuation also leaks the WKWebView until process teardown.  
  - **Fix:** Wrap `extractJavaScript` (and ideally every tool call in WorkerAgent) in a timeout using `Task` + `Group.addTask`/`ContinuousClock` race (e.g. 25-40s), calling `host.stop()` and resuming/cancelling the continuation on timeout. Also resume any still-pending continuation in a `deinit`/`stop()` to avoid a leaked continuation warning.  
  - **Verify:** Verified HiddenWebView.load (WebReaderTool.swift:165-171) suspends on a CheckedThrowingContinuation resumed only by didFinish/didFail/didFailProvisionalNavigation (173-182). The sole bound is URLRequest(timeoutInterval: 30) at line 168. Project-wide grep found no withTimeout/TaskGroup-race/deadline helper applied here. The continuation is NOT cancellation-aware, so even user cancellation cannot interrupt a hung navigation, and post-didFinish steps (750ms sleep line 203, evaluateJavaScript line 207) have no Task timeout. WorkerAgent runs tool calls inline and only checks budget.checkWallClock() at the top of each hop (line 125), which never fires while an await is stuck. SourceCache inFlight task (line 39) is non-cancellable from outside, so a hung fetch also stalls every concurrent waiter for the same URL. The 30s request timeout catches the common initial-navigation hang, so 'frequently' is somewhat overstated, but the unbounded-hang and no-cancellation gaps are genuine; P1 retained.  
- [x] **`research-tools-ssrf`** — No SSRF / URL-scheme validation: agent will fetch localhost, file://, and internal IPs  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/WebReaderTool.swift:45-47; PDFReaderTool.swift:39; RedditTool.swift:59; SourceCache.swift:32`  
  - **Category:** security · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `fetch_url` and `read_pdf` build a `URL(string:)` directly from LLM-supplied arguments and fetch it with no scheme allow-list and no host validation (grep for localhost/127.0.0.1/file:// across ResearchTools returns nothing). The only check is `URL(string:) != nil`, so `file:///etc/passwd`, `http://localhost:11434/...` (the local Ollama/sidecar), `http://169.254.169.254/...` (cloud metadata), and RFC-1918 hosts are all fetchable. A prompt-injected page that tells the worker LLM to 'fetch http://localhost:8000/admin' can exfiltrate or probe local services; the WKWebView JS path (WebReaderTool.swift:199) is an even richer SSRF surface since it executes page JS.  
  - **Impact:** Server-side request forgery against the user's own machine and local network; the deep-research agent is an LLM driven by attacker-controllable web content, so this is reachable via indirect prompt injection, not just a malicious user.  
  - **Fix:** Add a shared `validateFetchableURL(_:)` guard used by WebReaderTool/PDFReaderTool/RedditTool: require scheme in {http, https}; reject when host resolves to loopback/link-local/private ranges (or at minimum block literal localhost/127.0.0.1/::1/0.0.0.0/169.254.0.0-16/10./192.168./172.16-31 and `.local`). Apply the check to the resolved redirect URL too, not just the input.  
  - **Verify:** Verified no scheme allow-list or host validation anywhere in the fetch paths. WebReaderTool.swift:45 `guard let url = URL(string: args.url)` is the only check; PDFReaderTool.swift:39 and RedditTool.swift:59 are identical. A project-wide grep for localhost/127.0.0.1/file://169.254/isLoopback across ResearchTools and Shared returned no validation logic (only unrelated function names). URL(string:) accepts file:///etc/passwd, http://localhost:11434 (the local Ollama the app actually uses), http://169.254.169.254, and RFC-1918 hosts. The WKWebView JS path (extractJavaScript, line 199) additionally executes page JS, a richer SSRF/exfil surface. Prompt-injection from fetched page content driving the worker to fetch internal URLs is a real vector here because local services (Ollama, the deer-flow/MiroFish backend) are present. Blast radius is somewhat lower than a hosted service (this is a desktop app on the user's own machine), but P1 is justified by the local-service-probing + prompt-injection combination.  

#### Storage, Domain, Shared utilities

- [x] **`store-no-migration-plan-5`** — No SchemaMigrationPlan for the on-disk store — any model change risks a hard launch failure  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Storage/ResearchStore.swift:15-21`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** makeContainer() builds a persistent (isStoredInMemoryOnly: false) ModelConfiguration named "DeepResearch.v2" with no VersionedSchema / SchemaMigrationPlan and no migration fallback. SwiftData lightweight migration covers additive changes, but any non-trivial change (renaming/removing a property, changing a .unique attribute, changing a relationship) to StoredSession/Turn/Source/Citation/Event/ForecastRecord will fail to open the existing store and throw from ModelContainer(for:configurations:). makeContainer() rethrows, so app launch fails entirely with no recovery path.  
  - **Impact:** A future schema change ships an app that cannot launch for any existing user (their on-disk store is incompatible and there is no migration or destroy-and-recreate fallback). The 'v2' name suggests a v1 already shipped, so this risk is live and a single careless model edit can brick existing installs.  
  - **Fix:** Introduce a VersionedSchema + SchemaMigrationPlan now (even with v2 as the sole version) so future migrations have a home. Add a recovery fallback: catch the open error and, if migration is impossible, recreate the store (or open in-memory) so the app still launches and logs the loss via Log.storage.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. makeContainer (ResearchStore.swift:15-21) builds a persistent ModelConfiguration("DeepResearch.v2", isStoredInMemoryOnly: false) with no migration plan; grep for SchemaMigrationPlan/VersionedSchema/MigrationStage across the project returns nothing. Any non-additive schema change to the 6 models would fail to open the existing store. makeContainer rethrows and the only call site (SwiftDeepResearchApp.swift:11-16) wraps it in fatalError, so a failed migration crashes app launch with zero recovery path. Real robustness gap; P1 reasonable for a shipping persistent store.  
- [x] **`store-source-id-cross-session-1`** — StoredSource.id is globally unique but session-scoped — upsert rebinds a source across sessions  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Storage/ResearchStore.swift:76-104; ResearchSchema.swift:88`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** StoredSource.id is @Attribute(.unique) and is set to the source URL absoluteString (WebReaderTool.swift:89, PDFReaderTool.swift:118, WikipediaTool.swift:128, RedditTool.swift:195 all use url.absoluteString as the FetchedSource id). upsertSource fetches by id only (predicate #Predicate<StoredSource> { $0.id == target }) with no session filter. So when the SAME URL is fetched in a later research session, the upsert finds the row created in the earlier session and mutates it in place — its fullText/fetchedAt are overwritten and it remains attached (via the .cascade session relationship) to the FIRST session. The current session never gets its own StoredSource row for that URL.  
  - **Impact:** Cross-session data corruption: re-researching a topic that hits an overlapping URL silently overwrites the earlier session's stored source text and the new session shows no source for that URL. Deleting the original session cascade-deletes a source the second session still references. Export/inspection of the second session is incomplete.  
  - **Fix:** Make the source identity per-session: either set StoredSource.id to a composite key like "\(session.id)|\(url)" (drop the global url-only id), or remove .unique from id and add the session relationship to the upsert predicate (#Predicate { $0.id == target && $0.session?.id == sessionID }). The composite key is simpler and keeps .unique.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. StoredSource.id is @Attribute(.unique) String (ResearchSchema.swift:88). All four tools set FetchedSource.id = url.absoluteString: WebReaderTool.swift:89, PDFReaderTool.swift:118, WikipediaTool.swift:128 (canonical.absoluteString), RedditTool.swift:195. upsertSource (ResearchStore.swift:80-103) fetches with #Predicate{ $0.id == target } — no session filter — and on a hit only updates fullText/fetchedAt/snippet (lines 85-89), never reassigning existing.session. So a URL re-fetched in a later session mutates the first session's row in place and the new session's `sources` relationship never gets a row for that URL. Real cross-session correctness bug; P1 appropriate.  

#### Core SwiftUI Interface

- [x] **`iface-orphaned-live-run-4`** — Selecting a stored session mid-run orphans the still-streaming live run  
  - **Where:** `ResearchCanvas.swift:17-29; SessionSidebar.swift:14 (List selection: $env.selectedSessionID); AppEnvironment.swift:277-283`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** ResearchCanvas prioritizes env.selectedSessionID over env.live: clicking a different conversation in the sidebar (which binds List selection straight to env.selectedSessionID) switches the canvas to the stored session while env.live keeps streaming, persisting, and mutating observable state in the background. Nothing cancels or pauses it, and the 'Running' section only shows the live row when it is not yet in `sessions`. The user can also fire ⌘N (MainScene.swift:67-72 sets env.live=nil) which drops the LiveSession reference without calling cancel() — the orphaned streamTask runs to completion writing to a now-unreferenced object.  
  - **Impact:** A live run can be silently abandoned: it continues consuming the LLM/network budget and writing events with no UI surface, and ⌘N leaks the in-flight work without cancellation. Confusing for users (no indication a background run is still burning tokens) and wastes provider quota.  
  - **Fix:** On New Research (MainScene.swift:67-76) and newSession (ResearchCanvas.swift:80-84) call env.cancelLive() before clearing env.live. When the user selects a different session while env.live.status.isActive, either keep the live run visible via the persistent 'Running' sidebar row (don't gate it on `sessions` membership) or prompt/auto-cancel. Make abandonment explicit.  
  - **Verify:** Primary scenario confirmed. ResearchCanvas.body prioritizes env.selectedSessionID over env.live (lines 17-29). SessionSidebar binds List(selection: $env.selectedSessionID) (line 14) so clicking another session mutates selectedSessionID directly; nothing cancels env.live, whose streamTask keeps streaming/persisting/mutating observable state in the background. The 'Running' sidebar row only shows while the live session isn't yet in @Query sessions (SessionSidebar.swift:15), and startSession inserts immediately (ResearchStore.swift:29-31), so the running indicator vanishes fast — the orphaned run becomes invisible. The ⌘N sub-claim is partially inaccurate: runEngine's Task captures [weak self] (AppEnvironment.swift:466), so setting env.live=nil (MainScene:67-70, ResearchCanvas:81) drops the last strong ref, the LiveSession deallocates, and the next 'guard let self else { return }' (line 469) exits the task — it does NOT 'run to completion' against a live object. The sidebar-selection orphan path (where env.live is NOT niled) is the real, fully-confirmed robustness bug; P1 stands.  
- [x] **`iface-tokenstream-dead-2`** — Write-only tokenStream array grown on every token with no consumer  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/AppEnvironment.swift:335,449,561-562`  
  - **Category:** optimization · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** tokenStream is appended to on every .tokenDelta (synthesis + worker tokens) and capped at 4,000, but grep across the whole Interface tree shows no view ever reads it. It is pure dead @Observable state: every append mutates an observed property (triggering Observation invalidation bookkeeping) and the removeFirst(count-4000) compaction copies the array repeatedly once full.  
  - **Impact:** Wasted CPU and allocations per token, plus needless Observation churn, on top of the per-token save above. On a long run this is O(tokens) of useless work with array re-shuffling at the cap boundary.  
  - **Fix:** Delete tokenStream entirely (property, append, reset). If a future 'raw token feed' view is planned, gate it behind an opt-in and store it on a non-observed type or a separate buffer the view subscribes to explicitly.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. grep across the whole codebase shows tokenStream only at AppEnvironment.swift:335 (declaration), :449 (removeAll on follow-up), :561 (append per .tokenDelta — synthesis AND worker tokens), :562 (removeFirst compaction at >4000). No view ever reads it. It is pure write-only @Observable state appended on every token, with the removeFirst(count-4000) copy once full. Cited line numbers are exact. Lower-priority than the save (it is in-memory only) but genuinely dead state on the hottest path; P1 is fair.  

#### Cross-cutting: Architecture, Build Health & Dead Code

- [x] **`build-health-1`** — 1.1 MB hook-log tree inside the synchronized source group risks build collisions  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/logs/ (e.g. aaa-collisiontest, bbb-collisiontest, ccea98f8-.../post_tool_use.json); Swift Deep Research.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:422,460`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The app target uses a PBXFileSystemSynchronizedRootGroup (pbxproj:75-81), so Xcode auto-includes every file under 'Swift Deep Research/'. A 1.1 MB 'logs/' directory of Claude-Code hook JSON files lives there (notification.json, post_tool_use.json, pre_tool_use.json, user_prompt_submit.json, plus aaa-collisiontest/bbb-collisiontest dirs). The only thing preventing a build break is a hardcoded EXCLUDED_SOURCE_FILE_NAMES string listing 9 specific basenames in both Debug and Release configs. This list is brittle: any new hook basename (or a copy-resources collision between identically-named JSON in subdirs) reintroduces the 'Multiple commands produce' error. The collision-test directories are evidence this already broke the build. logs/ is gitignored so it is local-only state polluting the build graph.  
  - **Impact:** Intermittent, hard-to-diagnose build failures whenever a new hook log basename appears or two same-named files collide; every developer/CI machine that runs the hooks accumulates this tree inside the source root.  
  - **Fix:** Move the hook logs out of the source root entirely (e.g. to ~/.claude/projects/.../logs or a sibling dir outside 'Swift Deep Research/'), or relocate the synchronized root so logs/ is not under it. Delete the aaa/bbb-collisiontest dirs. If logs must stay, add an explicit PBXFileSystemSynchronizedBuildFileExceptionSet excluding the whole logs/ directory rather than enumerating fragile basenames.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. pbxproj:76-81 defines a PBXFileSystemSynchronizedRootGroup over 'Swift Deep Research'. `du -sh Swift Deep Research/logs/` = 1.1M containing aaa-collisiontest/, bbb-collisiontest/, ccea98f8-.../, and user_prompt_submit.json. EXCLUDED_SOURCE_FILE_NAMES at pbxproj:422 and :460 lists exactly 9 basenames (chat.json pre_tool_use.json post_tool_use.json notification.json stop.json subagent_stop.json session_start.json session_end.json user_prompt_submit.json) in both Debug and Release. logs/ is gitignored (.gitignore:43-44). The collision-test dirs are direct evidence the duplicate-basename problem already manifested. Any new hook basename re-breaks the build. P1 (robustness, build can break locally) is appropriate.

#### Cross-cutting: Security & Process/Network Safety

- [x] **`sec-env-cleartext-perms-3`** — API keys written to .env in cleartext with default (world-readable) permissions  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:137-164 (syncEnv); LLM/ModelProviderManager.swift:231-244 (envSeed); Interface/AppEnvironment.swift:259-265 (syncMiroFishEnv)`  
  - **Category:** security · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** syncMiroFishEnv pulls LLM_API_KEY (and provider mirror keys like DEEPSEEK_API_KEY/MINIMAX_API_KEY) plus ZEP_API_KEY out of the Keychain and writes them as plaintext lines into `<repoRoot>/.env` via `output.write(to:atomically:true)`. No POSIX permissions are set, so the file is created with the process umask (typically 0644 = world-readable). The whole point of KeychainStore is to keep keys out of plaintext on disk; this path defeats it by copying them to a predictable file (default ~/Downloads/mirofish/MiroFish-0.1.2/.env) readable by any local user/process. `atomically:true` writes a temp file then renames, so the temp file also inherits the loose umask.  
  - **Impact:** Secrets that were protected in the Keychain are persisted in cleartext in a world-readable file under the user's Downloads folder, exposed to any other local process, backup, or sync tool.  
  - **Fix:** After writing, set permissions to 0600 (FileManager.setAttributes [.posixPermissions: 0o600] on envURL), and create the temp/dir likewise. Better: write the secret with a pre-created 0600 file handle, or have MiroFish read keys from an injected child-process environment / a dedicated keychain-backed path instead of an on-disk .env. Confirm the repoRoot is gitignored (it is for the top-level .env, but the MiroFish repo's own .env is outside this app's .gitignore).  
  - **Verify:** Traced the full path: AppEnvironment.syncMiroFishEnv (line 259-264) merges modelProviders.envSeed(config) + the Zep key and calls MiroFishSupervisor.syncEnv. envSeed (ModelProviderManager.swift:231-244) emits LLM_API_KEY=<key> plus the provider mirror (e.g. DEEPSEEK_API_KEY/MINIMAX_API_KEY via keyEnvName) pulled from the Keychain in mirofishPayload. syncEnv (MiroFishSupervisor.swift:137-164) writes plaintext `key=value` lines to <repoRoot>/.env via `output.write(to: envURL, atomically: true, encoding: .utf8)` with no chmod/POSIX permissions set, so the file is created under the process umask. Default repoRoot is ~/Downloads/mirofish/MiroFish-0.1.2 (ForecastModels.swift:182). This copies Keychain-protected paid-API and Zep keys to a predictable cleartext file, defeating KeychainStore. The exact mode depends on umask (commonly 0644), but the cleartext-on-disk-without-explicit-perms core is real. P1 defensible for credential exposure.  
- [ ] **`sec-sandbox-off-2`** — App Sandbox disabled with empty entitlements  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:412,450 (ENABLE_APP_SANDBOX = NO); Swift Deep Research/Swift_Deep_Research.entitlements:4 (<dict/>)`  
  - **Category:** security · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Both build configs set ENABLE_APP_SANDBOX = NO and the entitlements file is an empty `<dict/>`. The app therefore runs with full user-level filesystem, network, and process-spawn privileges. Combined with the SSRF surface (sec-ssrf-fetch-tools-1) and subprocess spawning (MiroFishSupervisor/SidecarSupervisor), an LLM-driven file:// fetch or a compromised child process has unrestricted access to the user's home directory. Hardened Runtime is on (good), but without the sandbox there is no containment boundary.  
  - **Impact:** No OS-level containment: any code-execution or SSRF foothold (including via the spawned Python backends or WKWebView) operates with the user's full privileges; also blocks Mac App Store distribution.  
  - **Fix:** Enable App Sandbox and add only the entitlements actually needed: com.apple.security.network.client, com.apple.security.files.user-selected.read-write (already implied by ENABLE_USER_SELECTED_FILES), and — because the app spawns helper processes and writes Application Support venvs — evaluate whether the Python sidecars can run sandboxed or must be redesigned (sandboxed apps cannot spawn arbitrary interpreters from ~). If full sandboxing is infeasible for the local backends, document the threat model explicitly and at minimum add the SSRF/host allowlist guard as compensating control.  
  - **Verify:** project.pbxproj lines 412 and 450 both set ENABLE_APP_SANDBOX = NO (Debug+Release). Swift_Deep_Research.entitlements is exactly `<dict/>` (empty). ENABLE_HARDENED_RUNTIME = YES on both configs (lines 417/455), so the 'hardened runtime on, sandbox off' framing is accurate. No containment boundary for the SSRF/file:// surface or the spawned Python subprocesses. P1 justified given the combination with sec-ssrf-fetch-tools-1.  
- [x] **`sec-ssrf-fetch-tools-1`** — SSRF: research tools fetch arbitrary LLM-controlled URLs with no scheme/host allowlist  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/WebReaderTool.swift:45-47, ResearchTools/PDFReaderTool.swift:39, ResearchTools/RedditTool.swift:59-64,135-140`  
  - **Category:** security · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** fetch_url, read_pdf and reddit `thread` all do `URL(string: args.url)` and immediately issue a request with no validation of the scheme or host. The argument is supplied by the remote LLM (which itself ingests untrusted web content), so a prompt-injected page can make the worker fetch file:// URLs, http://169.254.169.254/ (cloud metadata), or internal hosts including the app's own local sidecars (http://127.0.0.1:9100 SeekDB and http://127.0.0.1:5001 MiroFish, both unauthenticated).
