WebReaderTool additionally renders fetched content in a WKWebView (extractJavaScript), so a file:// or javascript-capable target executes script in-process. With App Sandbox disabled (see sec-sandbox-off-2), file:// reads any user file.  
  - **Impact:** A malicious or prompt-injected source can pivot the agent to read local files, hit link-local/internal metadata endpoints, or call the unauthenticated local backends — local network and local file SSRF driven by untrusted LLM output.  
  - **Fix:** Add a shared URL guard used by every fetch tool: require scheme in {http,https} (https-only is ideal for read_pdf/reddit/fetch_url), reject URLs whose resolved host is a loopback/link-local/private/reserved IP (parse via getaddrinfo and check against 127.0.0.0/8, ::1, 169.254.0.0/16, 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, fc00::/7), and reject non-public hosts. Re-validate after redirects via a URLSession delegate (willPerformHTTPRedirection). For reddit `thread`, additionally pin host to reddit.com.  
  - **Verify:** Verified no scheme/host allowlist exists anywhere in the fetch path. WebReaderTool.swift:45 `guard let url = URL(string: args.url)` then immediately fetches; PDFReaderTool.swift:39 and RedditTool.swift:59 (thread) do the same. SourceCache.fetch (Shared/SourceCache.swift) only normalizes the URL, adds no validation. URLSession honors file:// and arbitrary http hosts; WebReaderTool's JS fallback loads the URL into a real WKWebView (HiddenWebView.load, line 169) so file:// / loopback targets are reachable and rendered in-process. Default sidecars are http://127.0.0.1:9100 (SeekDB) and http://127.0.0.1:5001 (MiroFish), both unauthenticated, and ollama/lmstudio on localhost — all hittable via an LLM-supplied URL. With App Sandbox off (sec-sandbox-off-2 confirmed), file:// reads arbitrary user files. P1 is appropriate; the LLM ingests untrusted web content so prompt injection is a realistic driver.  

### P2 — Medium (59)


#### Core Research Engine (native flow)

- [x] **`engine-dedup-truncates-reflection-rounds`** — Reflection dedup against the entire accumulated plan starves later rounds of new subtasks  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/ResearchEngine.swift:171-179, 185-192`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Each round appends newSubtasks into plan.subtasks (line 189), and the next round's dedup builds `asked` from the full accumulated plan (line 172) and filters newSubtasks by exact lowercased question equality. The engine-injected fallback subtasks (synthesizeDeepeningSubtasks) are deterministic and templated on userQuery, so on rounds where the reflector returns nothing, the same three fallback questions are generated every round and filtered out as duplicates after the first time — triggering the `newSubtasks.isEmpty` early break (line 174-178). A thorough 6-round run can therefore silently collapse to far fewer effective rounds, defeating the stated goal that 'Round 4/6 is not a no-op'.  
  - **Impact:** Thorough mode does not reliably deliver the extra depth its doc comment and budget promise; later rounds frequently no-op out, so fast/standard/thorough converge in practice whenever the reflector goes quiet. Wasted reflector calls (which also cost untracked tokens) with no added research.  
  - **Fix:** Make synthesizeDeepeningSubtasks vary by round (it already takes `round` but only uses it in rationale text — incorporate it into the question text/queries so each round is distinct) and/or dedup only against questions asked in the current round rather than the entire cumulative plan. Alternatively seed fallback questions from worker findings/gaps instead of fixed templates.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. synthesizeDeepeningSubtasks (ResearchEngine.swift:316-349) builds three subtask questions templated purely on plan.userQuery; the `round` param appears only in each rationale (lines 323/331/340), never in the question text, so the three questions are byte-identical every round. After the first fallback round, those questions are added to plan.subtasks (line 189); the next round's `asked` set is built from the full accumulated plan (line 172) and filters newSubtasks by exact lowercased question equality (line 173). On any later round where the reflector returns nothing, the same three fallbacks are regenerated and fully filtered, hitting the newSubtasks.isEmpty early break (line 174-178). This collapses a thorough 6-round run to fewer effective rounds when the reflector goes quiet, defeating the 'Round 4/6 is not a no-op' intent stated in the file header. P2 appropriate (degrades depth, not correctness of output).  
- [x] **`engine-empty-worker-summary-pollutes-synthesis`** — Workers that produce no final text still return and are fed to synthesis/reflection  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/WorkerAgent.swift:113-211`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** lastText starts as "" and is only updated when completion.text is non-empty (WorkerAgent.swift:133-136). If a worker exhausts hops on tool calls without ever emitting final prose, or breaks out on a budget/tool error before producing a summary, it returns WorkerOutput(summary: "", sources: collectedSources). The synthesizer then renders an empty '## Worker w-... — <question>' section (Synthesizer.swift:27-32) and the reflector lists it as '- <question> → ' (Reflector.swift:38-40), injecting noise and implying the question was answered when it was not.  
  - **Impact:** Empty/garbage worker sections dilute the synthesis prompt and mislead the reflector into thinking a sub-question is covered, suppressing legitimate gap-finding. Wastes synthesis context budget on blank sections.  
  - **Fix:** In runWorkers, drop or flag WorkerOutputs whose summary is empty (treat like a failed worker: emit .warning and exclude from accumulatedOutputs, or synthesize a 'no findings' marker the reflector can target). Keep their sources if any were fetched so they still feed the cache/citations.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. lastText starts as "" (WorkerAgent.swift:114) and is only assigned when !completion.text.isEmpty (line 133-136). If the worker breaks on a tool-call cap (line 156-159), a budget/provider error (line 202-203), or exhausts maxHops while only emitting tool calls, it returns WorkerOutput(summary: lastText="", sources: collectedSources) at line 208-211. Synthesizer renders '## Worker <raw> — <question>\n<empty>' (Synthesizer.swift:27-32) and Reflector lists '- **<question>** → ' (Reflector.swift:38-40). This injects empty sections implying an answered question. P2 reasonable.  
- [x] **`engine-plan-truncation-silent`** — Plan subtasks beyond maxWorkers are silently dropped, never researched even across later rounds  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/ResearchEngine.swift:108-119, 193-204`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Round 1 runs only Array(plan.subtasks.prefix(config.budget.maxWorkers)) (line 109) and refinement rounds run Array(newSubtasks.prefix(maxWorkers)) (line 194). The planner is instructed to emit 1-4 subtasks (Planner.swift:32) and reflector 3-6 gaps (Reflector.swift:61), but fast mode caps maxWorkers at 2. Subtasks 3-4 from the planner, and gaps beyond the worker cap each round, are dropped on the floor with no event, no carry-forward to a later round, and no record. They are also added to plan.subtasks (line 189) so the dedup later treats them as 'already asked' even though they were never researched.  
  - **Impact:** Planned/identified research is silently discarded — in fast mode half the plan may never run, and dropped reflector gaps are marked asked so they're never revisited. Users get a thinner answer than the plan implies with no indication work was skipped.  
  - **Fix:** Queue overflow subtasks for subsequent rounds instead of truncating, or emit a .warning naming the dropped subtasks. When adding to plan.subtasks for dedup, only mark subtasks that were actually dispatched, so unran ones remain eligible.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. Round 1 runs Array(plan.subtasks.prefix(config.budget.maxWorkers)) (ResearchEngine.swift:109) and refinement runs Array(newSubtasks.prefix(config.budget.maxWorkers)) (line 194). Planner is told to emit 1-4 subtasks (Planner.swift:33) and reflector 3-6 gaps (Reflector.swift:61/74), but fast mode caps maxWorkers at 2 (AgentBudget.swift:34). Subtasks/gaps beyond maxWorkers are dropped with no event and no carry-forward. Critically, the FULL newSubtasks (not the prefix) are appended to plan.subtasks at line 189, while only the prefix is researched at line 194 — so dropped subtasks are recorded in plan.subtasks and the dedup `asked` set (line 172) then treats them as 'already asked' even though they were never researched, permanently excluding them. P2 appropriate: silent loss of planned research scope.  
- [x] **`engine-source-target-unenforced`** — Per-worker source target is prompt-only; nothing enforces minimum source diversity  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/WorkerAgent.swift:64-96, 121-205; Domain/AgentBudget.swift:85-89`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** sourceTarget drives only the natural-language instruction ('fetch AT LEAST N URLs', WorkerAgent.swift:82) and the registerSource hard cap is an upper bound, not a floor. A worker whose LLM ignores the instruction and reads 0-1 sources still returns a WorkerOutput and is treated as a successful result by runWorkers (ResearchEngine.swift:284-288). There is no check that collectedSources.count meets a minimum, no retry/escalation when a worker triangulated nothing, and lastText (the summary) is returned even when empty.  
  - **Impact:** Synthesis can be built on workers that read almost no sources, producing ungrounded drafts while the run reports success. The 'a worker that only reads 1-2 sources cannot triangulate' rule is unenforced — exactly the failure mode the prompt warns about is silently accepted.  
  - **Fix:** After the worker loop, if collectedSources.count < a floor (e.g. min(2, sourceTarget)) and budget remains, run an additional forced fetch hop or emit a .warning and down-weight that worker in synthesis. At minimum surface a structured signal (e.g. sourcesRead count) on WorkerOutput so the synthesizer/reflector can prioritize gaps.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. sourceTarget only feeds the natural-language instruction (WorkerAgent.swift:67-68 compute minFetches/maxFetches, injected at line 82 'AT LEAST ... UP TO ... distinct URLs'). registerSource (AgentBudget.swift:85-89) returns n <= maxSourcesPerWorker — an upper bound only; there is no floor. runWorkers (ResearchEngine.swift:284-288) appends any non-nil WorkerOutput without checking collectedSources.count, and WorkerAgent.run returns WorkerOutput regardless of source count (line 208-211). No retry/escalation exists when a worker triangulated nothing, and summary is returned even if empty. P2 fair: degraded-quality output, not a crash.  

#### DeerFlow Plan-and-Execute Engine

- [x] **`deerflow-2`** — Observation digest grows unbounded and double-feeds context the planner already threads  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/DeerFlowEngine.swift:155, 477-481 (observationDigest), 102-113 (plan call)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** observationDigest maps EVERY entry in `allOutputs` (background investigation + all steps from all prior plan iterations) into a per-step prompt, each clipped to 2_000 chars but with no cap on the NUMBER of entries (lines 477-481). It is recomputed and prepended to each sequential step's extraContext (line 155). Across a thorough run (up to 3 plan iterations × up to 4 steps = ~12 worker outputs plus the investigation), a late step's prompt can carry ~13 × 2KB ≈ 26KB of observations on top of the worker's own system prompt and parent query. Separately, the planner already receives `priorObservations` (allOutputs.dropFirst, line 107) AND the investigation markdown — so the same findings are threaded into both planner and every worker, multiplying token spend. There is no dedup or recency window like ConversationContext.plannerPreamble uses (suffix(6)).  
  - **Impact:** Token cost scales O(steps²) within a run and risks exceeding the worker model's context window on long thorough runs (the comment at line 476 claims clipping prevents this, but only per-entry, not in aggregate). Large prompts also slow each LLM round-trip and can push the run into the wall-clock budget bust path (line 146-149), truncating later steps.  
  - **Fix:** Bound observationDigest to a recency window (e.g. the last N=4-6 outputs) and/or a total char budget, matching plannerPreamble's suffix() pattern. Consider passing only the immediately-preceding step's full output plus a compact one-line digest of earlier ones, since the planner already owns cross-step gap analysis.  
  - **Verify:** observationDigest maps EVERY entry in allOutputs with no entry-count cap, each clipped to 2_000 chars (DeerFlowEngine.swift:477-481), and is recomputed+prepended to each sequential step's extraContext (line 155). For thorough: maxPlanIterations = min(maxRounds=6,3)=3, maxSteps = min(4,maxWorkers=6)=4, so up to ~12 worker outputs + the investigation ≈ 13 × ~2KB ≈ 26KB of observations carried into a late step on top of system prompt + parent query. Separately the planner gets priorObservations (allOutputs.dropFirst, line 106) AND the investigation markdown — same findings threaded to both planner and every worker. Confirmed there is no dedup or recency window, unlike ConversationContext.plannerPreamble which uses `priorTurns.suffix(6)` (ConversationContext.swift:52). Token-spend bottleneck is real; P2 appropriate. (Minor: WorkerAgent's '12 standard, 24 thorough' comment is stale vs actual 20/36, but that doesn't affect this finding.)  
- [x] **`deerflow-3`** — Fast preset (maxPlanIterations==1) can run zero research steps when planner declares enough context  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/DeerFlowEngine.swift:87-91, 134-139, 179-184`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** maxPlanIterations = max(1, min(iteration.maxRounds, 3)); for the fast preset (IterationController.fast.maxRounds == 1) this is 1, so the planning loop body runs exactly once. If the planner returns hasEnoughContext:true (or empty steps), the loop breaks at line 138 and NO worker steps execute — the report is synthesized solely from the background investigation (snippets only, per deerflow-1). Even when steps ARE emitted in the single iteration, they execute, but there is then no re-plan to address gaps the steps reveal. The re-plan emit block (lines 179-184) is gated on `planIteration < maxPlanIterations`, so it never fires in fast mode. The net effect: fast mode is structurally one planner call plus at most one step batch, with the very common 'planner declares sufficiency on a strict prompt' path producing a near-empty research run.  
  - **Impact:** Fast-mode DeerFlow runs can return a report grounded almost entirely on uncited snippet text with no deep research performed, which is materially weaker than the native fast flow (which always runs its round-1 workers). Users selecting DeerFlow + fast may get qualitatively worse output than native + fast for the same budget.  
  - **Fix:** Guarantee at least one research-step batch runs before honoring hasEnoughContext on the FIRST iteration (e.g. ignore hasEnoughContext when planIteration == 1 and steps are non-empty, or force a minimal direct step when the planner short-circuits with empty steps and no prior step outputs exist). Alternatively raise the floor: maxPlanIterations = max(2, ...) so fast still gets one re-plan.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. IterationController.fast.maxRounds == 1 (IterationController.swift:19), so maxPlanIterations = max(1,min(1,3)) = 1 (DeerFlowEngine.swift:87); the `planning` while loop body runs exactly once. The planner system prompt explicitly pushes hasEnoughContext judgment ('be STRICT', lines 404-407) but the LLM can still return hasEnoughContext:true or empty steps; the guard at line 134 (`if dfPlan.hasEnoughContext || newSubtasks.isEmpty`) then breaks at line 138 with zero worker steps executed, leaving the report synthesized only from the investigation (which per deerflow-1 holds web snippets uncitably plus KB sources). The re-plan emit block (lines 179-184) is gated on `planIteration < maxPlanIterations` so it never fires when maxPlanIterations==1. Structural one-planner-call-plus-at-most-one-step-batch behavior is real; P2 appropriate.  
- [x] **`deerflow-5`** — processing steps still receive web/fetch tools through the worker's default tool list  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/DeerFlowEngine.swift:151-166; WorkerAgent.swift:69-97`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** For stepType == .processing the engine narrows stepTools to calculator/current_datetime/knowledge_base (lines 152-153), correctly excluding web_search/fetch_url. But the WorkerAgent system prompt is fixed (WorkerAgent.swift lines 69-97) and unconditionally instructs the worker to 'start with web_search', issue 2 web_search calls, and fetch AT LEAST minFetches distinct URLs — even when those tools are absent from toolSpecs. A processing-step worker is told to do web research it has no tools for. Since unknown/absent tools just won't be in `tools`, the worker either ignores the instruction (best case) or emits calls the loop reports as 'Unknown tool' (WorkerAgent.swift:179), burning hops and tool-call budget.  
  - **Impact:** Processing steps get a prompt that contradicts their available tools, wasting LLM round-trips on impossible web_search/fetch_url calls and producing confused 'I cannot search' outputs. The DeerFlow distinction between research and processing steps is therefore only half-implemented.  
  - **Fix:** Give WorkerAgent a step-kind or a tool-aware prompt: when web_search/fetch_url are not in the tool set, drop steps 1-3 of the strategy and instruct the worker to compute/analyze from the threaded earlier findings only. Simplest: pass a `mode` parameter to WorkerAgent.run and branch the system prompt, or build the strategy text from the actual `tools.keys`.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. For stepType==.processing the engine narrows stepTools to calculator/current_datetime/knowledge_base (DeerFlowEngine.swift:152-153), excluding web_search/fetch_url. But WorkerAgent's system prompt is fixed (WorkerAgent.swift:69-97) and unconditionally instructs 'For most questions start with web_search' (line 74), 'issuing 2 separate web_search calls' (line 80), and 'call fetch_url ... on AT LEAST minFetches' (lines 81-83) — only the knowledge_base instruction is conditional (line 56). toolSpecs are derived from the (narrowed) tools dict (line 105), so a processing worker is told to do web research with no web tools present. If it emits such a call, the loop returns `.failed(message: "Unknown tool: ...")` (WorkerAgent.swift:179), burning a hop and a tool-call budget slot (registerToolCall fires at line 156 before the lookup at 162). Real correctness/efficiency issue; P2 reasonable.  

#### Forecast / MiroFish Backend Integration

- [ ] **`mirofish-run-1`** — Pure 2s polling re-fetches whole research log + graph + report every tick; no incremental/SSE streaming  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/ForecastRun.swift:195-239 (pollLoop), 269-305 (sideFetches)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Despite the file headers framing this as streaming, there is no SSE/EventSource anywhere in the Forecast module (confirmed by grep). Every 2 seconds the poll loop calls pipelineStatus, then sideFetches issues up to ~6 more serial GETs: researchProgress(lines:200) re-pulls the last 200 log lines wholesale each tick (researchLines = p.lines replaces the array, not appends), graphData(gid) re-downloads and rebuilds the entire ForecastGraph (ForecastGraph.from does an O(nodes+edges) degree sort every time, ForecastModels.swift:102-131) while the graph stage runs, runStatusDetail pulls recent_actions, and timeline re-pulls. reportAgentLog is the only endpoint using an incremental cursor (from_line). All of these run serially per tick on the MainActor-bound run.  
  - **Impact:** For a large graph or a long-running simulation this is significant redundant network + JSON decode + force-graph rebuild churn, repeated every 2s for the entire multi-minute/hour run, and it does redundant whole-array SwiftUI invalidations (replacing researchLines/graph/timeline each tick) that thrash @Observable diffing. Wasted bandwidth, CPU, and battery.  
  - **Fix:** Adopt the backend's incremental endpoints where they exist (researchProgress already returns total; track a line cursor and append like reportAgentLog does). Stop rebuilding ForecastGraph when node/edge counts are unchanged (compare MFGraphData.node_count/edge_count before from()). Run the independent side-fetches concurrently with async let / a TaskGroup instead of serially. Ideally back the research console and agent log with a real SSE stream (URLSession.bytes(for:).lines) and reserve polling for coarse status only.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. grep for EventSource/text-event-stream/SSE in Forecast/ returns nothing — pure polling. pollLoop (195-239) sleeps 2s then sideFetches (269-305) issues serial awaited GETs: researchProgress(lines:200) with `researchLines = p.lines` (273, full replace not append), graphData(gid) rebuilding the whole graph via ForecastGraph.from which re-runs the O(nodes+edges) degree sort (ForecastModels.swift:107-113) every tick while .graph runs, runStatusDetail + timeline, etc. Only reportAgentLog uses an incremental cursor (fromLine: agentLogCursor, 339). All serial on the @MainActor run. Accurate as an efficiency/bottleneck finding; P2 is fair (it is localhost, so impact is moderate).  
- [ ] **`mirofish-run-2`** — Poll loop treats decode/4xx errors the same as transport failures, so persistent non-transient errors take 60s to surface (or never)  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/ForecastRun.swift:228-237`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The catch block increments consecutiveFailures on ANY thrown error from pipelineStatus and only fails the run after 30 consecutive failures (~60s). MiroFishError.isTransient (MiroFishClient.swift:290-296) exists precisely to distinguish transient (unreachable/429/5xx) from permanent (decode, 4xx, api-level failure), but the poll loop ignores it. A permanent decode error (e.g. a backend response-shape change) or a 400/404 on the status endpoint will silently retry for 60s and then fail with that same error — or, if it intermittently succeeds, never converge. Conversely a genuinely fatal MiroFishError.api is swallowed for 60s instead of failing fast.  
  - **Impact:** Backend contract drift or a bad pipeline id produces a 60s 'spinning' UI before any error, and masks the real root cause behind a generic retry. Token spend continues server-side the whole time.  
  - **Fix:** Branch on (error as? MiroFishError)?.isTransient: for non-transient errors fail immediately (or after 1-2 retries); only apply the 30-strike budget to transient ones. Surface the underlying error message rather than the last retry's. Consider resetting consecutiveFailures handling so a 404 (pipeline gone) maps to .cancelled/.failed promptly.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. The catch block (228-237) increments consecutiveFailures on ANY thrown error and only calls failWith after >=30 (~60s of 2s ticks). It never consults MiroFishError.isTransient (MiroFishClient.swift:290-296), which exists precisely to separate transient (unreachable/429/5xx) from permanent (decode/4xx/api). A permanent MiroFishError.decode or .httpStatus(400/404) or .api on the status endpoint is retried for ~60s before surfacing instead of failing fast. Accurate. P2 reasonable.  
- [x] **`mirofish-supervisor-4`** — probeHealth accepts any HTTP 200 on the port — port-conflict false positive attaches to a foreign server  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:510-520`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** probeHealth only checks `(response as? HTTPURLResponse)?.statusCode == 200` on GET /health. It never decodes the body or verifies it is MiroFish. MFHealth (MiroFishClient.swift:316-319) carries a `service` field precisely for identification, but the supervisor's probe ignores it. Any unrelated server already bound to 127.0.0.1:5001 that answers 200 on /health (or even / via a permissive router) makes ensureRunning short-circuit to .running and the client then fires /api/research/run at a backend that doesn't exist there.  
  - **Impact:** If something else owns :5001, the user gets confusing decode/404 errors deep in the pipeline instead of a clear 'port taken' message. The supervisor's own statusAfterExit already has nice 'Port already taken' handling (line 492) that this bypasses, because we never try to launch.  
  - **Fix:** Decode the /health body and require the MiroFish service signature (e.g. MFHealth.service == expected or status == 'ok' plus a known marker). On a 200 that isn't MiroFish, treat the port as conflicted and return a .failed('port taken by non-MiroFish') status rather than attaching.  
  - **Verify:** Real but severity overstated. probeHealth (510-520) only checks `(response as? HTTPURLResponse)?.statusCode == 200` on GET /health and never decodes the body or checks the `service` field, even though MFHealth carries `service` (MiroFishClient.swift:316-319) and health() decodes it. So any foreign server bound to 127.0.0.1:5001 answering 200 on /health makes ensureRunning short-circuit to .running. Genuine robustness gap. But P1 overstates it: this is a localhost-only port-conflict edge case requiring an unrelated server to occupy 5001 and answer 200 on /health; the launch path already diagnoses 'address already in use' (statusAfterExit 492-494). Downgraded to P2.  
- [ ] **`mirofish-supervisor-5`** — waitForHealthOrExit can hang the full 120s after the child has already exited  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:524-531; handleExit:478-483`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** waitForHealthOrExit detects child death solely via `if process == nil`. But `process` is set to nil inside handleExit (line 480), which is invoked from the Process.terminationHandler via a hop: `Task { await self?.handleExit(...) }` (line 456-459). There is an unbounded scheduling gap between the OS reporting exit and that Task running on the actor. During that gap process is still non-nil, so the loop keeps sleeping 500ms slices up to 240 iterations. Worse, if the child crashes instantly on a bad venv, the readabilityHandler-driven appendLog and handleExit Tasks may not have run yet, so statusAfterExit() (called right after) sees an empty logTail and produces the generic 'exited before becoming healthy' message instead of the precise ModuleNotFoundError/config diagnostics it has special cases for.  
  - **Impact:** Launch failures can take far longer to surface than necessary and frequently lose the actionable diagnostic (ImportError / 配置错误 / ZEP_API_KEY) that statusAfterExit is designed to extract, degrading the onboarding/repair UX that is the whole point of this supervisor.  
  - **Fix:** Drive exit detection off the Process directly: check `process?.isRunning == false` (not `== nil`) in the wait loop so termination is observed synchronously, and before computing statusAfterExit, drain any remaining pipe output (readToEnd on the stdout pipe) so logTail is populated for the diagnostic heuristics. Capture the termination status synchronously in the wait rather than relying on the async handleExit hop.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. waitForHealthOrExit (524-531) detects child death only via `if process == nil`. process is set to nil in handleExit (480), which runs via `Task { await self?.handleExit(...) }` hopped from the terminationHandler (456-459). There is an unbounded scheduling gap between OS exit and that Task running on the actor, during which process is still non-nil so the loop keeps sleeping 500ms slices (up to 240 = ~120s). Also confirmed statusAfterExit() (485-506) is invoked immediately after waitForHealthOrExit returns false (lines 90/115); if appendLog/handleExit Tasks haven't drained yet, logTail can be empty and the generic 'exited before becoming healthy' message (505) is produced instead of the ModuleNotFoundError/配置错误 special cases (488-504). P2 appropriate.  
- [ ] **`mirofish-supervisor-6`** — syncEnv writes secrets (Zep/LLM API keys) to .env via a non-atomic-by-key, world-readable plaintext file with no key escaping  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:137-164; ForecastOnboarding.swift:187-198, 250-263`  
  - **Category:** security · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** syncEnv persists the Zep key (and, via AppEnvironment.syncMiroFishEnv, the LLM provider key) into <repoRoot>/.env as plaintext `KEY=VALUE`. The write uses String.write(atomically:true) but creates the file with default permissions (not 0600), so the secret lands in a Downloads-folder file readable by any process/user with home access. Values are written verbatim with no quoting/escaping: a key containing '#', spaces, newlines, or '=' would corrupt the .env (and the line-rewrite logic at 145-154 only matches the first '=' so a value with '=' round-trips wrong). There is also no validation that repoRoot is the intended folder before writing.  
  - **Impact:** API credentials are stored in cleartext in a predictable, broadly-readable location (defeating the Keychain storage the app otherwise uses), and a key with shell-special characters silently breaks backend boot (which then surfaces as the generic 'config error'). Security exposure plus a robustness footgun.  
  - **Fix:** chmod the .env to 0600 after writing (FileManager setAttributes posixPermissions 0o600). Quote/escape values (wrap in single quotes, escape embedded quotes) when writing. Consider injecting secrets via the child's environment for ephemeral runs where possible, and only writing .env when the backend's override=True semantics truly require it. Validate the value doesn't contain newlines before writing.  
  - **Verify:** Core security claim confirmed. syncEnv (137-164) writes pairs as verbatim `\(key)=\(value)` into <repoRoot>/.env (default ~/Downloads/mirofish/MiroFish-0.1.2, ForecastModels.swift:182). AppEnvironment.syncMiroFishEnv (259-264) feeds it the Zep key plus the LLM provider key (envSeed). The write uses String.write(atomically:true) with NO chmod 0600, so the temp+rename lands at default umask (~0644) plaintext — secret readable by other local processes/users. Values are written with no quoting/escaping, so a value containing '#', a space, or a newline corrupts the .env. NOTE one sub-claim is wrong: the line-rewrite uses firstIndex(of:'=') only to extract the KEY for matching (148-149) and never re-parses the value, so a value containing '=' round-trips fine — that specific concern is incorrect. The plaintext/permissions/escaping core stands; P2 reasonable for a local-only secret-at-rest exposure (key also lives in Keychain).  

#### Knowledge Base / SeekDB sidecar

- [x] **`kb-delete-id-not-escaped`** — Document id is interpolated directly into the DELETE URL path without percent-encoding  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/SeekDBClient.swift:56-58 (delete) and 125-130 (request URL building)`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** delete(id:) builds the path as `"/documents/\(id)"` and passes it to URL(string: path, relativeTo: host). doc ids are derived server-side as `doc-<sha1>` for auto-ids (seekdb_sidecar.py:373-376), which are URL-safe, BUT the client also lets callers pass arbitrary ids (upsert id: String?), and KnowledgeBase.delete(id:) forwards whatever id came back. If an id ever contains a space, '/', '#', '?' or other reserved character, URL(string:) returns nil and delete throws SeekDBError.unreachable (misleading), or worse routes to the wrong path. There's no addingPercentEncoding on the path segment (contrast KnowledgeBaseTool.swift:139-140 which DOES escape ids when building kb:// URLs).  
  - **Impact:** Deletion of any document whose id contains a URL-reserved character fails with a misleading 'unreachable' error and the document can't be removed from the UI.  
  - **Fix:** Percent-encode the id with `.urlPathAllowed` (minus '/') before interpolating, e.g. `id.addingPercentEncoding(withAllowedCharacters: .urlPathAllowed)`, mirroring the escaping already done in KnowledgeBaseTool.fetchedSource.  
  - **Verify:** Real but P2 is too high given current usage. delete(id:) builds `"/documents/\(id)"` with no percent-encoding (SeekDBClient.swift:57) and request() does `URL(string: path, relativeTo: host)` → throws SeekDBError.unreachable (a misleading message) if the path contains a reserved char that makes URL(string:) return nil (line 128-129). The contrast holds: KnowledgeBaseTool.fetchedSource DOES escape ids (lines 139-140 addingPercentEncoding). HOWEVER in practice all delete ids are doc.id from the journal, and the only ingest path (KnowledgeBase.ingest, line 91) never passes id, so the server always derives a URL-safe `doc-<sha1>` (seekdb_sidecar.py:373-376). DocumentUploadView.swift:272 deletes doc.id, which is always that safe form. The SeekDBClient.upsert(id:) arbitrary-id surface exists but has no caller. So this is a latent robustness gap with no current trigger → P3, not P2.  
- [ ] **`kb-global-lock-serializes-query`** — Single global lock + all-sync FastAPI endpoints serialize every query behind any ingestion  
  - **Where:** `sidecar/seekdb_sidecar.py:125 (_lock), 188-211 (upsert under lock), 258-263 (query under lock), 378-437 (sync def endpoints)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** Every endpoint is defined with plain `def` (not `async def`), so FastAPI dispatches each on the threadpool — good for not blocking the event loop. But KnowledgeBase mutations AND query() all acquire the same `threading.Lock` (`with self._lock`). A large document ingest holds the lock across chunking + `self._collection.add(...)` (embedding all chunks), during which any `/query` from a worker agent blocks. During a research run, the worker's knowledge_base tool calls can stall for the full duration of a concurrent upload. Worse, queryWithRecovery (KnowledgeBaseTool.swift:126-133) only retries 3 times with ≤2.1s total backoff, so a slow ingest can make the KB lookup time out and fall through to web search.  
  - **Impact:** Knowledge-base search latency spikes (potentially many seconds) whenever a document is being ingested, degrading the research workflow exactly when the user is actively building the KB. Read/write are fully serialized despite vector search being read-only.  
  - **Fix:** Hold the lock only around the minimal pyseekdb mutation, not chunking/embedding. Better: use a readers-writer lock so concurrent queries proceed while no write is in flight, and only serialize writers. Embedding in add() is the expensive part — consider performing chunking/embedding outside the lock and taking the lock only for the final add()/journal write.  
  - **Verify:** Core mechanism confirmed but a supporting number is wrong. All endpoints use plain `def` (health/list_docs/upsert/upsert_bulk/delete/reset/query, lines 378-437) so FastAPI runs them on the threadpool. upsert() holds self._lock across _split + _collection.add (embedding all chunks) at lines 188-201; query() acquires the SAME self._lock at lines 258-263. So a concurrent /query genuinely blocks behind an in-flight ingest. HOWEVER the finding's claim that queryWithRecovery 'only retries 3 times with ≤2.1s total backoff' is inaccurate: KnowledgeBaseTool.swift:126-127 sleeps attempt*700ms for attempts 1,2,3 = 0.7+1.4+2.1 = 4.2s cumulative, not 2.1s. More importantly, a query blocked on the lock does NOT raise — it waits up to the 30s URLSession request timeout (SeekDBClient.swift:25) then throws .unreachable; the retry loop only triggers on .unreachable/5xx, so the fall-through-to-web-search path is via the 30s timeout, not retry exhaustion. The bottleneck is real but single-user localhost concurrency (one ingest at a time vs. worker queries) makes P1 overstated; P2 is more appropriate.  
- [ ] **`kb-implicit-embedding-fn`** — Collection created with no explicit embedding function — silent dependence on pyseekdb default  
  - **Where:** `sidecar/seekdb_sidecar.py:152-154 (get_or_create_collection), 197-201 (add with documents=), 259-262 (query with query_texts=)`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** get_or_create_collection(name=...) is called with no embedding_function, and both add() (documents=chunks) and query() (query_texts=[text]) rely on pyseekdb auto-embedding text. This means the embedding model, its dimensionality, and any model download happen implicitly inside pyseekdb. There is no pinning of the model, no warm-up, and no handling if the default embedding model needs a network download on first use (which would make the very first ingest/query hang or fail unpredictably). If a future pyseekdb version changes the default embedding model, existing vectors become incompatible with new query embeddings silently.  
  - **Impact:** First-run cold start may stall on a model download with no progress signal; cross-version model drift can silently degrade or break similarity search; the distance scale feeding _distance_to_score is undefined, making scores non-portable.  
  - **Fix:** Pin an explicit embedding function/model in get_or_create_collection and document the expected distance metric. Add a startup warm-up (embed a tiny string) so any model download happens during the 'installing/launching' window the UI already shows, not on the user's first query.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. get_or_create_collection(name=settings.collection) is called with NO embedding_function (lines 152-154); grep confirms 'embedding_function' appears nowhere in the sidecar. add() passes documents=chunks (199) and query() passes query_texts=[text] (260), both relying on pyseekdb's default auto-embedding. The embedding model, its dimensionality, and any first-use model download are thus implicit and unpinned, with no warm-up and no handling of a cold first-use network download. A future pyseekdb default-model change would silently make new query embeddings incompatible with existing vectors. P2 confirmed.  
- [ ] **`kb-journal-vector-drift`** — Document journal (JSON) and vector store can drift out of sync on partial failures  
  - **Where:** `sidecar/seekdb_sidecar.py:188-211 (upsert), 221-231 (reset), 252-253 (list_documents)`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** list_documents() and the /health document count read ONLY the in-memory `_journal` (persisted to documents.json), which is maintained separately from the pyseekdb collection. In upsert(), `_collection.add(...)` runs, then `_journal[doc_id] = {...}` and `_persist_journal()`. If add() succeeds but the process is killed before _persist_journal (or _persist_journal raises), the vectors exist but the doc is invisible in the UI (and vice-versa: _delete_chunks_of swallows exceptions at line 246-248 and returns 0, yet the journal entry is still overwritten). reset() deletes the collection then clears the journal, but if delete_collection throws it's swallowed (line 225) and only the journal is wiped, orphaning vectors that still answer queries. There is no reconciliation between the two stores on startup.  
  - **Impact:** UI document list and search results can disagree: deleted docs still surface in queries, or ingested docs never appear in the list. Over time the KB shows phantom or missing documents, eroding trust in 'consulted private knowledge'.  
  - **Fix:** Treat the vector store as the source of truth and rebuild/reconcile the journal from collection metadata on startup (group chunk metadatas by doc_id). At minimum, make _delete_chunks_of failures propagate so a failed delete doesn't silently update the journal, and verify reset() actually emptied the collection before clearing the journal.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. list_documents() (line 252-253) and /health 'documents' (line 385) read only len(kb._journal) — the JSON journal, maintained separately from the pyseekdb collection. In upsert() the order is _collection.add (197-201) THEN _journal[doc_id]=... + _persist_journal() (202-210); a kill between add and persist leaves vectors with no journal entry (invisible in UI). _persist_journal itself is atomic (tmp + os.replace, 165-168) so partial journal writes aren't the risk — the cross-store gap is. _delete_chunks_of swallows exceptions and returns 0 (246-248) while upsert still overwrites the journal entry, and reset() swallows delete_collection failures (225) yet wipes the journal (230), orphaning queryable vectors. No startup reconciliation between journal and collection exists (KnowledgeBase.__init__ only loads the journal, 157-161). P2 confirmed.  
- [ ] **`kb-no-ingest-batching`** — Entire document embedded in one add() call with no batching or size cap  
  - **Where:** `sidecar/seekdb_sidecar.py:197-201 (single _collection.add for all chunks); Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/KnowledgeBase.swift:91 (single upsert per file)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** upsert() splits text into 1500-char chunks then passes ALL chunk ids/documents/metadatas to a single `self._collection.add(...)`. A large PDF (e.g. a 500-page book → thousands of chunks) embeds every chunk in one synchronous call while holding the lock, with no batching, no progress, and no upper bound on document size. The Swift HTTP client uses a 30s request timeout (SeekDBClient.swift:25), so a large ingest that embeds for >30s will hit URLError and surface as SeekDBError.unreachable to the UI, even though the sidecar keeps working and may eventually succeed — leaving the journal/store in an indeterminate state relative to what the UI thinks happened.  
  - **Impact:** Large-document ingestion can exceed the client's 30s timeout and appear to fail (KnowledgeBase.lastError set, isBusy cleared) while the sidecar is still embedding, and the long single add() holds the global lock the whole time, blocking queries (see kb-global-lock).  
  - **Fix:** Batch _collection.add in chunk groups (e.g. 64-128 at a time) so progress is incremental and the lock can be released between batches; raise the SeekDBClient timeout for the upsert path specifically (it is the only long-running call), or make ingestion async with a status/poll endpoint. The unused /documents/bulk endpoint (defined at seekdb_sidecar.py:405) is a natural place to add real batching.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. upsert() splits into chunk_chars=1500 chunks (line 178, _split) then passes ALL ids/documents/metadatas to a single self._collection.add(...) (lines 197-201) under self._lock — no batching, no progress, no document-size cap. KnowledgeBase.ingestFile → ingest → one client.upsert per file (KnowledgeBase.swift:91). SeekDBClient uses HTTPClientCommon.defaultSession(timeout: 30) (SeekDBClient.swift:25), whose timeoutIntervalForRequest=30 (Logging.swift:53). A large ingest whose synchronous add() exceeds ~30s of inactivity raises URLError → SeekDBError.unreachable surfaced to the UI even though the sidecar keeps working, leaving journal/store state indeterminate vs. what the UI shows. The /documents/bulk endpoint exists but isn't the batching primitive used here. P2 confirmed.  
- [ ] **`kb-watchdog-orphan-window`** — Parent-death watchdog polls every 2s and only checks getppid — leaves a real orphan window  
  - **Where:** `sidecar/seekdb_sidecar.py:447-476 (_start_parent_watchdog)`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The Python self-terminate-on-parent-death relies on a daemon thread polling getppid() every 2.0s and exiting when ppid==1 or ppid changes. There is a window of up to 2s where the parent died but the child still holds the port; if a new app instance launches in that window it collides on the port (and there is no recovery — see kb-port-in-use-no-recovery). On macOS, re-parenting to launchd does set ppid to 1 reliably, but the 2s cadence plus the lack of port-conflict recovery means rapid relaunch (quit + reopen) can intermittently fail. os._exit(0) also skips uvicorn shutdown, which is intentional but means the socket relies on SO_REUSEADDR/TIME_WAIT timing to free quickly.  
  - **Impact:** Quit-then-quickly-reopen (or Xcode stop+run) can intermittently hit 'address already in use' for up to ~2s, and with no port-conflict recovery the KB is dead until the orphan's watchdog fires.  
  - **Fix:** Reduce the poll interval (e.g. 0.5s) and/or use kqueue NOTE_EXIT on the parent pid for immediate notification. Pair with the EADDRINUSE recovery from kb-port-in-use-no-recovery so a brief overlap self-heals instead of failing.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. _start_parent_watchdog (lines 447-476) starts a daemon thread that loops: read getppid(), exit via os._exit(0) when ppid==1 or ppid != initial_parent, else time.sleep(2.0) (line 473). So there is up to a ~2s window where the parent has died but the child still holds the port; combined with the absence of port-conflict recovery (kb-port-in-use-no-recovery), a rapid quit+relaunch inside that window can collide on the port and fail. os._exit(0) (line 472) intentionally skips uvicorn shutdown, so socket release depends on SO_REUSEADDR/TIME_WAIT timing as the finding states. The getppid==1 re-parent-to-launchd reasoning is accurate for macOS. P2 confirmed.  

#### LLM Provider Layer

- [x] **`foundationmodels-delta-prefix-fragile`** — FoundationModels delta extraction via String(describing:) + prefix heuristic is fragile  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/FoundationModelsClient.swift:72-87`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The client reads streamed partials with `String(describing: partial)` and emits the delta only when the new snapshot `hasPrefix(lastSeen)`; otherwise it re-emits the ENTIRE snapshot. `String(describing:)` on a FoundationModels partial-response value is not a documented stable text accessor — it may include type/debug wrapping, and partial responses are not guaranteed to be strictly prefix-monotonic (whitespace normalization, retraction). When the prefix check fails it dumps the whole snapshot as a delta, duplicating already-streamed text. Token usage is also faked as `lastSeen.count / 4`.  
  - **Impact:** On-device generation can emit garbled or duplicated text (whole-snapshot re-emit) and the chars/4 token estimate misreports budget. Because `String(describing:)` isn't a contract, a FoundationModels SDK change can break text extraction outright.  
  - **Fix:** Use the documented content accessor on the partial response (the response's `.content` text property) rather than `String(describing:)`, and compute deltas against the previous accumulated content. If monotonicity isn't guaranteed, accumulate the full text and emit the suffix diff via a proper longest-common-prefix, or buffer and emit once. Drop the fake usage or compute it from the session's reported token count.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed at FoundationModelsClient.swift:72-87: snapshot = `String(describing: partial)`, emits delta only when `snapshot.hasPrefix(lastSeen)` else yields the ENTIRE snapshot (line 82), duplicating already-streamed text. String(describing:) is not a documented stable text accessor for FoundationModels partial responses and partials are not guaranteed strictly prefix-monotonic. Token usage faked as `lastSeen.count / 4` (line 86). All claims accurate; genuine fragility. P2 appropriate.  
- [x] **`gemini-cumulative-usage-double-charge`** — Gemini per-chunk cumulative usage double/triple-charged in synthesis  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/GeminiClient.swift:224-228 + Swift Deep Research/Engine/Synthesizer.swift:99-100`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** Gemini's `streamGenerateContent` emits `usageMetadata` with CUMULATIVE token counts on most/all SSE chunks. `parseEvent` yields a `.usage` chunk for each one. The streaming consumer in Synthesizer ADDS every usage chunk to the budget (`budget.chargeTokens(p + c)`), so cumulative counts are summed repeatedly — e.g. counts 10, 25, 40 are charged as 75 instead of 40. The `complete()` path (LLMClient.swift:63) ASSIGNS usage (last-wins, correct), so the two consumption paths disagree and only the streaming path is wrong.  
  - **Impact:** Synthesis stage (the main user-facing report, which streams) over-charges Gemini token budget by a large multiple, prematurely tripping budget limits or truncating the final report. Behavior differs between worker (complete) and synthesis (stream) paths.  
  - **Fix:** Make usage semantics consistent: have providers emit DELTA usage, or have streaming consumers track last-seen usage and charge the delta/final value rather than summing. Simplest fix: in Synthesizer keep `lastUsage` and charge once after the loop, mirroring `complete()`’s last-wins assignment.  
  - **Verify:** Mechanism confirmed: Synthesizer.swift:99-100 is the ONLY consumer that charges per `.usage` chunk and it ACCUMULATES (BudgetMeter.chargeTokens line 68 does `tokensUsed += n`); every other path uses complete() which ASSIGNS last-wins (LLMClient.swift:64). GeminiClient.parseEvent (224-228) yields a `.usage` chunk for every SSE event carrying usageMetadata. Synthesizer is fed by configurable synthesisLLM (ResearchEngine.swift:93), which can be Gemini. So IF Gemini emits cumulative usageMetadata on multiple SSE chunks, they are summed (the 10/25/40->75 example holds). Conditional on actual Gemini SSE emission (often only final-chunk), and impact is budget-accounting only (over-count -> may stop synthesis early), never output correctness. Downgrade P1->P2.  
- [ ] **`gemini-retry-after-server-responded-dead-code`** — Gemini/Anthropic transient retry is effectively dead for HTTP errors; inconsistent retry classifiers across clients  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/GeminiClient.swift:74-98 + Swift Deep Research/LLM/AnthropicClient.swift:71-95`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** In both clients, `serverResponded` is set true the instant `session.bytes(for:)` returns headers (before the HTTP status check). The status-code check then throws an EngineFailure, but the retry guard requires `!serverResponded`, so HTTP 429/5xx are never retried even though RetryPolicy.isTransient would classify them transient. Pre-response transient retry (connection dropped before headers) does work. Separately, three different transient classifiers exist: AnthropicClient/GeminiClient use `RetryPolicy.isTransient`, OpenAICompatibleClient uses its own private `isTransient`, and they include slightly different URLError sets — inconsistent behavior per provider.  
  - **Impact:** A transient Gemini/Anthropic 429 or 503 that arrives WITH headers is surfaced immediately with no retry (the OpenAI-compat client also never retries 4xx/5xx, but at least its comment says so deliberately). Worker-level handling stops the worker rather than retrying. Maintenance hazard from three divergent isTransient implementations.  
  - **Fix:** Decide the policy explicitly: if HTTP 429/503 should be retried with backoff, do it after the status check (honoring Retry-After) regardless of serverResponded; if not, remove the misleading retry scaffolding. Consolidate the three `isTransient` variants into one shared classifier (RetryPolicy.isTransient) used by every client.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed in both clients: GeminiClient sets `serverResponded = true` (line 75) immediately after `session.bytes(for:)` returns, BEFORE the status check (76-79); AnthropicClient does the same (line 72) before checkOK (73). The catch guard requires `!serverResponded` (Gemini 91, Anthropic 87), so HTTP 429/5xx EngineFailures are never retried even though RetryPolicy.isTransient would classify 429/5xx transient (RetryPolicy.swift:79-82). Pre-response transport retry still works. Three transient classifiers confirmed: Anthropic/Gemini use RetryPolicy.isTransient (URLError set at RetryPolicy 69-73), OpenAICompatibleClient uses its own private isTransient (different set, lines 155-166). Inconsistent per provider. All accurate.  
- [x] **`gemini-unconditional-finish-stop`** — Gemini overwrites real finishReason with hardcoded .stop and ignores per-candidate finishReason  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/GeminiClient.swift:84,198-223`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** After the SSE loop the client unconditionally yields `.finished(reason: .stop)`, and `parseEvent` never reads the per-candidate `finishReason` field (which can be `MAX_TOKENS`, `SAFETY`, `RECITATION`, `MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL`, etc.). So a truncated (length-capped) or safety-blocked Gemini response is reported to the engine as a clean `.stop`. Contrast with OpenAICompatibleClient which maps `length`/`content_filter` and even synthesizes `.length` when the stream drops with output (lines 461-471, 119-133).  
  - **Impact:** A Gemini answer cut off by max output tokens or blocked by a safety filter is treated as a complete, successful stop. The worker/synthesizer can't detect truncation, so partial or empty research output is silently accepted as final.  
  - **Fix:** Parse the candidate `finishReason` in `parseEvent`, map MAX_TOKENS→.length, SAFETY/RECITATION→.contentFilter, others→.stop, track the last seen reason, and emit that instead of a hardcoded `.stop`. Also surface `promptFeedback.blockReason` when no candidates are returned.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed: after the SSE loop GeminiClient unconditionally yields `.finished(reason: .stop)` (line 84), and parseEvent (193-229) never reads the per-candidate `finishReason` field. A MAX_TOKENS/SAFETY/RECITATION/MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL response is reported as a clean .stop; OpenAICompatibleClient maps length/content_filter and synthesizes .length (461-471, 119-133). Note: no engine consumer actually branches on finishReason (grep over Engine/ResearchTools found none; complete() exposes it but Worker/Reflector/Planner/DeerFlow/Citation read only text+toolCalls), so practical impact today is limited, but the data is genuinely discarded. P2 stands.  
- [x] **`ollama-no-http-error-body-truncation-risk`** — Ollama stream has no mid-stream truncation detection and no token-arg fragment reassembly  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/OllamaClient.swift:92-162`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Unlike OpenAICompatibleClient (which detects a byte stream ending without a terminal marker and flags truncation), the Ollama NDJSON loop simply `continuation.finish()`es when the byte stream ends. If the connection drops before the `"done": true` line, the worker receives whatever partial text arrived as if it were a clean, complete answer — no `.finished(.length)` flag, no error. The error-body read on non-2xx (lines 69-73) is fine, but a mid-stream drop after 200 OK is silently treated as success.  
  - **Impact:** A dropped local Ollama stream (model crash, OOM, server restart mid-generation) yields a silently truncated worker summary presented as final, with no signal to the engine that the answer is incomplete.  
  - **Fix:** Track whether a `done:true` line was seen; if the loop exits without it, emit `.finished(reason: .length)` (or throw if nothing was produced), mirroring OpenAICompatibleClient.swift:119-133.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed: OllamaClient NDJSON loop (92-161) only emits .usage/.finished inside the `done == true` branch (137-157); if the byte stream ends before a `"done": true` line it falls through to `continuation.finish()` (162) with no .finished(.length) flag and no error -> a mid-stream drop after 200 OK is silently treated as a clean complete answer. OpenAICompatibleClient detects this (no-terminal branch 119-133, emits .length or throws). The non-2xx error-body read (65-82) is fine and not the issue. Claim accurate. P2 appropriate.  

#### Research Tools

- [x] **`research-tools-calculator-e`** — Calculator globally replaces 'e' → 2.718…, corrupting scientific notation and tokens  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/CalculatorTool.swift:48`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `s = s.replacingOccurrences(of: "e", with: "\(M_E)")` replaces EVERY 'e' in the expression unconditionally. So `1e3` becomes `1×2.718…×3`-ish garbage (`12.71828…3`), `1.5e-10` is destroyed, and any future function name containing 'e' breaks. The allow-list at line 37 even permits `eExX` specifically to support exponent notation, but this substitution defeats it. The advertised constant `e` is the only intended target. NSExpression also does not understand `sqrt`/`abs`/`log` as written without function syntax, so the description over-promises.  
  - **Impact:** Silent wrong answers for any expression using scientific notation or the letter e in an unintended position — exactly the multi-step arithmetic this tool exists to make reliable. The LLM trusts the result and may propagate a wrong number into the final report.  
  - **Fix:** Replace `e` only as a standalone token via a word-boundary regex (e.g. `\be\b` with `.regularExpression`), do the same for `pi`, and run the substitution after exponent-notation handling. Add a couple of unit checks (`1e3 == 1000`, `2*pi`, `e`). Consider validating that NSExpression actually supports the advertised functions or trim the description.  
  - **Verify:** Verified CalculatorTool.swift:48 `s = s.replacingOccurrences(of: "e", with: "\(M_E)")` replaces every 'e' unconditionally, after pi substitution. I compiled and ran the exact clean() pipeline: '1e3' becomes '12.7182818284590453' and evaluates to 12.718 (should be 1000). Worse than described: '1.5e-10' becomes '1.52.718281828459045-10' which throws an uncaught NSInvalidArgumentException inside NSExpression(format:) -- a hard crash of the worker/app, not a graceful .failed. The allow-list at line 37 deliberately permits eExX for exponents, which this substitution defeats. One sub-claim is wrong: I confirmed NSExpression DOES evaluate sqrt(2)=1.414, abs(-5)=5, log(100)=2 as written (built-in function syntax), so 'does not understand sqrt/abs/log' is incorrect -- but it does not invalidate the core, confirmed corruption-and-crash bug. P2 is reasonable (arguably higher given the crash, but it requires the model to emit scientific notation).  
- [x] **`research-tools-citation-substring`** — CitationExtractor never verifies exactQuote actually appears in the cited source  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/CitationExtractor.swift:65-77`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The extractor's entire purpose (file doc comment lines 1-9) is exact-quote grounding: the LLM is told the quote 'must appear verbatim (whitespace tolerant) in the indicated source'. But the Swift code only decodes the JSON and maps it to `Citation` — it never checks that `c.exactQuote` is actually a substring of any `sources[*].extractedText`. So a hallucinated quote, or a paraphrase the model labeled as exact, is accepted as a verified citation. The promised whitespace-tolerant verification exists only in the prompt, not in code.  
  - **Impact:** Citations can be fabricated yet presented as grounded, defeating the anti-hallucination design and misleading users who trust the citation panel as evidence the claim is sourced.  
  - **Fix:** After decoding, verify each `exactQuote` against the source corpus with whitespace-normalized substring matching (collapse runs of whitespace on both sides, case-insensitive optional); drop or flag citations that don't match, and prefer matching against the specific source whose URL/title was named. Optionally repair the URL by locating which source actually contains the quote.  
  - **Verify:** Verified CitationExtractor.swift:65-77 only decodes the LLM's JSON into Wire and maps each entry to Citation, guarding only `URL(string: c.sourceURL)`. There is NO check that c.exactQuote is a (whitespace-tolerant) substring of any sources[*].extractedText. The verbatim-grounding requirement exists only in the prompt text (lines 33-34 'must appear verbatim (whitespace tolerant)'), not in code, so a hallucinated or paraphrased quote is accepted as a verified citation. The file doc comment (lines 1-9) advertises exact-quote enforcement that the Swift code does not perform. Confirmed at P2.  
- [x] **`research-tools-ddg-fragile`** — DuckDuckGo last-resort backend silently returns [] on layout change or empty parse  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/WebSearchTool.swift:265-297`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** DDG is the keyless final fallback in the chain. It scrapes `html.duckduckgo.com` with a single regex keyed on `class="result__a"`. If DDG changes markup, returns its anti-bot interstitial, or serves a captcha page (common for datacenter IPs), the regex matches nothing and `search` returns `[]` — which WebSearchTool treats as `"empty result set"` (line 84), not an error. When DDG is the only configured backend (no API keys set, the default per makeDefault), the whole `web_search` then reports `.failed` with 'empty result set', giving the LLM no usable web search at all and no signal that it was a scraping/bot-block issue.  
  - **Impact:** With no API keys configured (the out-of-box state), web search degrades to a brittle HTML scrape that fails silently and frequently, crippling the core research loop for free-tier users.  
  - **Fix:** Detect DDG anti-bot pages (challenge markers, captcha, or 0 results despite 200) and surface a distinct error; add a fallback regex/parse for the lite endpoint (`lite.duckduckgo.com/lite`); consider a SearXNG/Startpage backend or HTML structural parse via SwiftSoup (already a dependency) instead of one regex. At minimum, log the response length so empty-parse vs no-results is distinguishable.  
  - **Verify:** Verified WebSearchTool.swift:278 single regex keyed on class="result__a". makeDefault (lines 42-51) appends Tavily/Exa/Brave only when keys exist and ALWAYS appends DuckDuckGoBackend, so with no keys configured DDG is the only backend. If DDG changes markup or serves a captcha/anti-bot page (line 274 validate() only catches non-2xx, not a 200 captcha interstitial), the regex matches nothing and search returns [] (line 296). The caller treats [] as 'empty result set' (line 84), not an error, so web_search reports .failed with 'ddg: empty result set' (line 93-94), giving the LLM no usable search and a misleading signal. Confirmed at P2.  
- [x] **`research-tools-empty-set-not-fallthrough`** — A search backend that returns 0 results stops the fallback chain prematurely only via 'continue' — but empty sets don't cascade to a clear final error vs misconfig  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/WebSearchTool.swift:68-94`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The loop does correctly fall through to the next backend on an empty result set (line 83-84 appends a failure and continues), which is good. The real gap: when a higher-priority backend (e.g. Tavily) legitimately returns 0 results for a niche query, the chain proceeds, but the per-attempt 'empty result set' strings are concatenated into the final `.failed` message indistinguishably from real errors (401/429). The LLM sees 'All search backends failed … tavily: empty result set; exa: empty result set' and cannot tell 'rephrase the query' from 'your keys are broken'. Also, a backend returning a non-empty-but-all-unparseable result (e.g. every `URL(string:)` returns nil at line 170/215/253) yields an empty array and is reported as 'empty result set' though it was actually a parse failure.  
  - **Impact:** The LLM gets ambiguous failure signals; it may abandon a valid query thinking the providers are down, or keep retrying a broken-key provider, wasting tool-call budget.  
  - **Fix:** Distinguish 'empty (query returned nothing)' from 'error' in the aggregated message and in the returned outcome; when ALL backends returned genuinely-empty sets (no thrown errors), return an `.ok` with an empty-results payload + a note ('no web results; try rephrasing') rather than `.failed`, so the worker treats it as a normal empty search instead of a tool failure.  
  - **Verify:** Verified the loop does fall through on empty results (WebSearchTool.swift:83-84 appends 'empty result set' and continues -- the title's own 'good' caveat). The real gap holds: line 84 ('empty result set') and line 87 (real error string e.g. 'HTTP 401') both append to the same `failures` array, joined indistinguishably at line 93 into the final .failed message, so the LLM cannot tell 'rephrase' from 'keys broken'. Also confirmed: Tavily/Exa/Brave backends use `.compactMap { URL(string: ...) }` (lines 169-171, 214, 251-253); if every result URL fails to parse the array is empty and is reported as 'empty result set' though it was actually a parse failure. Confirmed at P2.  
- [ ] **`research-tools-no-readability`** — Static HTML extraction has no readability/main-content heuristic — text is whole-page soup  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/WebReaderTool.swift:111-123`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Extraction removes script/style/nav/footer/header/aside/noscript/iframe then takes `doc.text()` of the entire remaining body, collapses whitespace, and clips to 20k chars (Extracted.asFetched, line 92). There is no main-article detection (no `<article>`/`<main>`/role=main preference, no boilerplate scoring like Readability/Arc90). On large news/blog/docs pages, comment sections, related-article rails, cookie banners, and ad slots survive and consume the 20k budget, so the actually-relevant article may be truncated away or diluted, hurting downstream synthesis and citation grounding.  
  - **Impact:** Lower signal-to-noise in fetched sources; the citation extractor (CitationExtractor.swift, 4k chars/source) and synthesizer get padded boilerplate instead of article body, weakening grounding and answer quality on exactly the pages workers fetch most.  
  - **Fix:** Add a lightweight readability pass: prefer `article`, `main`, `[role=main]`, or the densest text container (SwiftSoup-based scoring) before falling back to full-body text. Strip common boilerplate selectors (`.comments`, `[aria-label*=cookie]`, `.related`, `.sidebar`). This reuses the existing SwiftSoup dependency.  
  - **Verify:** Verified WebReaderTool.swift:112 removes script/style/nav/footer/header/aside/noscript/iframe then line 114 takes doc.text() of the entire remaining body, collapses whitespace, and asFetched clips to 20_000 (line 92). There is no <article>/<main>/role=main preference and no boilerplate/density scoring (Readability/Arc90 style). The removed tags catch a lot of chrome, so the gap is narrower than 'no readability at all', but comment sections, related-article rails, cookie banners, and inline ad slots survive and consume the 20k budget, diluting/truncating the main article. Valid improvement; P2 reasonable.  
- [ ] **`research-tools-pdf-streaming`** — PDF and JS extraction buffer the entire response into memory before any size check  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/PDFReaderTool.swift:52-64; WebReaderTool.swift:103`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `read_pdf` and `fetch_url` use `session.data(for:)`/`dataWithRetry` which loads the full HTTP body into a `Data` before any inspection. There is no `Content-Length` cap and the `maxPages` limit only applies AFTER the entire PDF is in memory and parsed by PDFKit (line 102-107). A malicious or accidental multi-hundred-MB PDF or HTML page is fully downloaded and `PDFDocument(data:)`-parsed before the 60k char clip ever runs, spiking memory across up to 6 parallel workers. The JS path caps innerText in-page (good) but the static HTML path downloads unbounded bytes first.  
  - **Impact:** Memory pressure / potential OOM on large responses, especially with several workers fetching concurrently; an attacker page can amplify this (zip-bomb-style large PDF).  
  - **Fix:** Set and check a max-bytes ceiling: inspect `Content-Length` and reject oversized responses early, or switch to `URLSession.bytes(for:)` and stop reading after N MB. Cap PDF download size (e.g. 25-50 MB) before `PDFDocument(data:)`.  
  - **Verify:** Verified PDFReaderTool.swift:52 uses dataWithRetry -> session.data(for:) which buffers the full HTTP body into Data before any inspection; the HTML-mime guard (line 59) and PDFDocument(data:) parse (line 81) and the maxPages/60k-char caps (lines 102-107) all run AFTER the entire body is in memory. There is no Content-Length cap. WebReaderTool.swift:103 static path likewise downloads the full body before SwiftSoup parse; only the JS path caps innerText in-page (line 208, slice(0,200000)). With up to 6 parallel workers (thorough budget, AgentBudget.swift:48-54), a multi-hundred-MB PDF/HTML is fully downloaded+parsed per worker. Confirmed at P2.  
- [x] **`research-tools-serial-toolcalls`** — Tool calls within one LLM turn execute serially, not in parallel  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/WorkerAgent.swift:147-193`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** When a completion returns multiple tool calls (e.g. the prompt at WorkerAgent.swift:78-82 explicitly tells the worker to issue 2 web_search calls and fetch_url on 3+ URLs), `for call in completion.toolCalls { … await tool.call(…) }` awaits each one before starting the next. Each `fetch_url`/`read_pdf` is a network round-trip (plus possible 750ms JS settle and retries), so N independent fetches the model batched into one turn run end-to-end instead of concurrently. The SourceCache already makes concurrent fetches safe (in-flight dedup), so the serialization is pure latency.  
  - **Impact:** Worker latency scales linearly with sources read; a worker reading 6 sources takes ~6× a single fetch even though they're independent, materially slowing every run and eating wall-clock budget that could have gone to more sources.  
  - **Fix:** Execute the tool calls of a single turn concurrently with `withThrowingTaskGroup`, preserving result ordering when appending `.toolResult` messages and honoring per-call cancellation. Keep `registerToolCall`/budget checks before spawning. Ordering of `messages.append(.toolResult…)` must match the assistant tool_call order for providers that require it.  
  - **Verify:** Verified WorkerAgent.swift:147 `for call in completion.toolCalls { ... }` with `outcome = try await tool.call(...)` at line 172 inside the loop body -- each tool call is fully awaited before the next begins. No TaskGroup/async-let. The worker prompt (lines 79-86) explicitly tells the model to issue 2 web_search calls and fetch 3+ URLs, so a model that batches N fetches into one assistant turn runs them serially. SourceCache provides in-flight dedup (SourceCache.swift:36) making concurrent fetches safe, so the serialization is pure latency. Confirmed; P2 appropriate (latency bottleneck, not correctness).  

#### Storage, Domain, Shared utilities

- [ ] **`budget-charge-swallowed-4`** — Token budget enforcement defeated by try? at synthesis/DeerFlow charge sites  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/Synthesizer.swift:100; Engine/DeerFlowEngine.swift:264,445; Engine/WorkerAgent.swift:167`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** BudgetMeter.chargeTokens (AgentBudget.swift:67-73) throws EngineFailure(.budgetExceeded) when the cap is crossed — the intended hard stop. But several callers invoke it as `try? await budget.chargeTokens(...)`, swallowing the throw: Synthesizer.swift:100 (synthesis token usage), DeerFlowEngine.swift:264 and :445, and the WorkerAgent charge closure at :167 (`charge: { tokens in try? await self.budget.chargeTokens(tokens) }`). Only WorkerAgent.swift:131 propagates it. tokensUsed still accumulates, but the cap no longer aborts the run at these sites.  
  - **Impact:** The maxTokens cap (60k/300k/800k) is not enforced during synthesis and in the DeerFlow path. A runaway or expensive synthesis can blow far past the user's chosen cost ceiling without the run being cut off, defeating the Settings cost/quality control.  
  - **Fix:** Decide deliberately: if synthesis must always finish, document that and clamp/stop streaming when snapshot.tokenFraction>1 instead of silently swallowing. Otherwise propagate the error (try await) so .budgetExceeded surfaces, or check snapshot after charging and break the stream with a .warning + graceful finalize.  
  - **Verify:** Verified at all four cited sites. BudgetMeter.chargeTokens throws EngineFailure(.budgetExceeded) when cap crossed (AgentBudget.swift:67-73). Synthesizer.swift:100 uses `try? await budget.chargeTokens(p + c)`. DeerFlowEngine.swift:264 `charge: { tokens in try? await budget.chargeTokens(tokens) }` and :445 `try? await budget.chargeTokens(...)`. WorkerAgent.swift:167 charge closure `try? await self.budget.chargeTokens(tokens)`. Only WorkerAgent.swift:131 uses `try await` and propagates. tokensUsed still accrues but the hard stop is swallowed at these sites. P2 appropriate.  
- [x] **`cache-sourcecachehit-never-emitted-3`** — SourceCache never emits .sourceCacheHit — dedup is invisible and miscounts the budget  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Shared/SourceCache.swift:32-45; Domain/ResearchEvent.swift:17`  
  - **Category:** bug · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The .sourceCacheHit(WorkerID, URL) event has full UI/persistence handling (AppEnvironment.swift:552, 636, 666) but is never emitted anywhere — grep for emit(.sourceCacheHit / yield(.sourceCacheHit) returns nothing. SourceCache.fetch returns the cached/in-flight value (lines 35-37) without any signal. Separately, every tool calls budget.registerSource BEFORE cache.fetch (WebReaderTool.swift:48 then :56), so a cache hit still consumes a worker source slot, and the same URL fetched twice by one worker double-charges its source budget even though only one network fetch happened.  
  - **Impact:** Users never see the 'Cache hit' activity that the UI is wired for, so the parallel-dedup optimization is invisible. More importantly, cache hits/duplicate URLs burn the per-worker source budget (maxSourcesPerWorker), prematurely triggering 'Source cap reached' on runs where workers legitimately share URLs.  
  - **Fix:** Have fetch() return whether the result was a hit (or take an onCacheHit closure / emit) and emit .sourceCacheHit; and only call registerSource on a genuine miss. Simplest: move the budget reservation inside the cache miss path, or return a (FetchedSource, wasCached) tuple and have the tool releaseSource on a hit.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. grep for sourceCacheHit across *.swift (excluding logs) shows it is only DEFINED (ResearchEvent.swift:17,43) and CONSUMED (AppEnvironment.swift:552,636,666) — never emitted/yielded. SourceCache.fetch (SourceCache.swift:35-37) returns cached/in-flight values with no signal. registerSource is called BEFORE cache.fetch in every tool (e.g. WebReaderTool.swift:48 then :56; same in PDF/Wikipedia/Reddit at :40/:48, :105/:111, :141/:147), so a cache hit still consumes a worker source slot. Token charge (WebReaderTool.swift:69) also re-runs on a cache hit. Both budget claims hold. P2 appropriate.  
- [x] **`keychain-set-failure-silent-6`** — Keychain set/delete failures are silently dropped (Bool ignored at call sites)  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Shared/KeychainStore.swift:31-60; Interface/SettingsSheet.swift:860-915; Forecast/ForecastOnboarding.swift:190`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** set/delete return Bool but discard the OSStatus (no logging of the actual error code), and the @discardableResult lets callers ignore it. SettingsSheet.swift:862/915 and ForecastOnboarding.swift:190 call `await KeychainStore.shared.set(value, for: account)` and never check the return. If the write fails (e.g. SecItemUpdate returns an error other than errSecItemNotFound, or SecItemAdd fails under a locked keychain / entitlement issue), the user believes the key was saved but get() later returns nil, producing a downstream 'API key missing' failure with no clue why.  
  - **Impact:** Silent key-save failures present to the user as 'provider not configured' errors that are impossible to diagnose, since neither the UI nor the logs record the Keychain OSStatus.  
  - **Fix:** Log non-success OSStatus with SecCopyErrorMessageString via Log.storage in set/delete, and surface a save failure in the Settings UI (e.g. throw or return the status). At minimum, the Settings save path should check the Bool and show an error.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. KeychainStore.set/delete (KeychainStore.swift:31-60) return Bool with @discardableResult and only compare status == errSecSuccess — the OSStatus is never logged. All three cited call sites ignore the return: SettingsSheet.swift:860 (delete), :862 and :915 (set), ForecastOnboarding.swift:190 (set for .zep). A failed write leaves the UI showing 'saved' while get() later returns nil, surfacing downstream as an opaque 'API key missing' failure. P2 appropriate.  
- [x] **`retry-enginefailure-string-match-8`** — RetryPolicy classifies provider errors by lowercased substring of the message — brittle and misclassifies  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Shared/RetryPolicy.swift:77-84`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed
