  - **Problem:** isTransient inspects EngineFailure.providerFailure by substring-matching the message: m.contains("429")||"503"||"502"||"500"||"rate limit". This is fragile: a 500-char body or a model name/URL containing '500' (e.g. a token count '50000', a path '/v500', or an error quoting an HTTP-200 body that mentions 5xx) is treated as transient and retried; conversely a real 429 surfaced without those exact digits (e.g. 'Too Many Requests' only) is treated as fatal. There is no structured status code on EngineFailure to key off.  
  - **Impact:** Non-idempotent or genuinely-fatal provider failures can be retried up to maxAttempts (wasting tokens/time and possibly duplicating side effects), while legitimate rate limits worded differently fail fast.  
  - **Fix:** Add a typed statusCode: Int? (or a retryable: Bool) field to EngineFailure populated by the provider clients, and classify on that. Keep substring matching only as a last-resort fallback. Match whole words / use a regex anchored on HTTP-status context, not bare digit substrings.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. RetryPolicy.isTransient (RetryPolicy.swift:79-83) classifies .providerFailure by lowercased substring contains '429'/'503'/'502'/'500'/'rate limit'. EngineFailure (ResearchEvent.swift:164-181) has no structured status field. Messages interpolate the status (AnthropicClient.swift:276 'Anthropic HTTP \(statusCode)') but also embed raw response bodies (OllamaClient.swift:80 includes the snippet; ModelDiscovery includes snippets), so a body containing '500'/'502' etc. can be misclassified as transient, and a bare 'Too Many Requests' without digits would be treated as fatal. Brittleness is real; P2 reasonable.  
- [ ] **`store-context-save-per-mutation-13`** — ResearchStore calls context.save() on every single mutation — chatty I/O on the main actor during streaming  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Storage/ResearchStore.swift:30,40,64,70,88,102,120,179; AppEnvironment.swift:505 (per-event appendEvent)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Every store method ends in try context.save(), and ResearchStore is @MainActor. During a run, AppEnvironment.ingest persists EVERY ResearchEvent (appendEvent → save) plus upsertSource on each sourceFetched (save) and updateTurnMarkdown — a high-frequency token/tool/progress stream means dozens-to-hundreds of synchronous SwiftData saves on the main actor while the UI is also updating. Each save flushes to the SQLite-backed store, serializing disk writes on the main thread.  
  - **Impact:** Main-actor disk I/O proportional to event volume can cause UI hitching/jank during active research, especially on long runs with many tool calls and token deltas. The cost grows with run intensity exactly when the UI must stay responsive.  
  - **Fix:** Batch saves: insert during streaming and save() on a debounce/coalesce (e.g. every N events or on a timer / at stage boundaries), and always save at terminal events (draftReady, sessionCompleted, error). Or move persistence to a background ModelContext with its own actor and hand SwiftData persistent identifiers back to the main actor.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. ResearchStore is @MainActor (ResearchStore.swift:6) and every mutating method ends with try context.save(): lines 30,40,45,64,70,88,102,120,132,143,179. AppEnvironment.ingest persists EVERY ResearchEvent via appendEvent->save (line 505) plus upsertSource->save on each sourceFetched (line 547) and updateTurnMarkdown->save on each draftReady (line 574). A high-frequency token/tool/progress stream thus triggers dozens-to-hundreds of synchronous SQLite-backed saves on the main actor concurrent with UI updates. Real main-thread I/O bottleneck; P2 reasonable. (Note: tokenDelta events do persist per-event, amplifying the count.)  
- [x] **`store-duplicate-citations-2`** — Citations duplicated on the same turn across reflection rounds  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/AppEnvironment.swift:568-583; Storage/ResearchStore.swift:108-121`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Each .draftReady event re-runs persistence. On a re-draft (reflection produces a new draft for the same turn), AppEnvironment updates the existing draftTurn markdown (updateTurnMarkdown) but then unconditionally calls store.attachCitations(descriptor.citations, to: turn) again. attachCitations always inserts NEW StoredCitation rows (CitationExtractor.swift:69 mints a fresh UUID id per extraction run, so the .unique id never collides and old rows are never replaced). With multi-round reflection the same turn accumulates one full citation set per round.  
  - **Impact:** A turn that went through N reflection rounds persists N× its citations. Exported reports and the citation inspector show duplicated/triplicated citations; storage grows unnecessarily.  
  - **Fix:** Before re-attaching on a re-draft, delete the turn's existing StoredCitation rows (turn.citations is a cascade relationship — clear it, or context.delete each) inside a single attachCitations-that-replaces call, or make StoredCitation.id deterministic (e.g. hash of claim+quote+url) and upsert.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. Synthesizer.synthesize emits .draftReady (Synthesizer.swift:110) and is called once initially (ResearchEngine.swift:122) then re-called inside the reflection loop (ResearchEngine.swift:207, inside `while round < maxRounds`). Each .draftReady in AppEnvironment (lines 568-583) updates the existing draftTurn markdown but then UNCONDITIONALLY calls store.attachCitations(descriptor.citations, to: turn) (line 581). attachCitations always inserts new StoredCitation rows (ResearchStore.swift:108-120). CitationExtractor.swift:69 calls Citation(...) omitting id:, so each run mints a fresh UUID default (Citation.init default, ResearchEvent.swift:147), guaranteeing the .unique id never collides and old rows are never replaced. Multi-round reflection accumulates one citation set per round. P2 appropriate.  

#### Core SwiftUI Interface

- [ ] **`iface-appenv-god-object-5`** — AppEnvironment is a god object spanning four unrelated subsystems  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/AppEnvironment.swift:10-315`  
  - **Category:** architecture · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The single @Observable AppEnvironment owns: research state (live, selectedSessionID, missingKeyHint), the entire forecast pipeline (workspace, forecast, forecastConfig, forecastBackendStatus, onboarding, start/open/new/delete, ensureForecastBackend, syncMiroFishEnv), the backend pipeline browser (backendPipelines, refresh/open/delete), and the provider/keychain plumbing. Any mutation to any one of these (e.g. backendPipelinesRefreshing toggling, forecast polling) invalidates every view observing env, since Observation tracks at property granularity but all these views read `env`.  
  - **Impact:** Cross-subsystem invalidation (a forecast poll can re-evaluate research views and vice-versa), a 300+ line class that is hard to test/reason about, and tight coupling that makes it easy to introduce the orphaning/cancellation bugs above. Forecast refresh churn taxes the research UI even when the user is on the Research tab.  
  - **Fix:** Split into focused @Observable models: ResearchModel (live/session/keys), ForecastModel (pipeline + backend + browser), injected separately via .environment. Keep AppEnvironment as a thin composition root. This isolates invalidation and shrinks the blast radius of each change.  
  - **Verify:** Structurally confirmed: the single @MainActor @Observable AppEnvironment (lines 10-315) owns research state (live/selectedSessionID/missingKeyHint), the whole forecast pipeline (workspace/forecast/forecastConfig/forecastBackendStatus/onboarding + start/open/new/delete/ensureForecastBackend/syncMiroFishEnv), the backend pipeline browser (backendPipelines/refresh/open/delete), and provider/keychain plumbing — four unrelated subsystems in one object. The architecture critique is valid at P2. Caveat: the supporting rationale ('any mutation invalidates every view observing env because Observation tracks per-property but all views read env') is technically imprecise — Swift Observation subscribes at property-ACCESS granularity, so a view reading only env.live is NOT invalidated by env.backendPipelinesRefreshing toggling. The god-object structure is the real, confirmed issue; the invalidation mechanism is overstated but secondary.  
- [x] **`iface-canvas-fetch-in-body-3`** — Synchronous SwiftData fetches executed inside SwiftUI body  
  - **Where:** `ResearchCanvas.swift:17-19,90-96; SourcePanel.swift:92-93,217-222; MainScene.swift:173-178`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** ResearchCanvas.body calls storedSession(for:) which runs a FetchDescriptor #Predicate fetch on modelContext every time body re-evaluates while a stored session is selected. SourcePanel.sourcesTab and MainScene.currentStoredSession do the same. These views re-render frequently (the live timer's TimelineView ticks every second, draftMarkdown mutates per token, inspector toggles), so a DB query runs on the main thread on each pass.  
  - **Impact:** Repeated main-thread DB round-trips during active runs and per-second timer ticks; unnecessary load that compounds the per-token bottleneck and can stutter the canvas when a stored session is open alongside a live one.  
  - **Fix:** Resolve the StoredSession once via @Query with a predicate, or cache it in @State and refresh on .onChange(of: env.selectedSessionID), rather than fetching inside body. SwiftData @Query is designed for exactly this and avoids ad-hoc fetches per render.  
  - **Verify:** The fetch-in-body anti-pattern is real in all three cited locations: ResearchCanvas.storedSession(for:) (lines 90-96, called from body line 19) runs a FetchDescriptor #Predicate fetch; SourcePanel.fetchSession (217-222, called from sourcesTab line 93); MainScene.currentStoredSession (173-179, called from exportMenu/toolbar). However the FREQUENCY justification is overstated: these views read env.selectedSessionID / env.live (the optional identity) — NOT env.live.draftMarkdown — so Observation does NOT re-evaluate them per streamed token, and the per-second TimelineView lives inside ConversationView (a child), not these views. They re-eval on selection/status-identity changes, not 'each pass while the timer ticks'. The pattern is a legitimate cleanup (should be cached or @Query-driven) but it is not a per-token hot path, so P1 is too high; adjusted to P2.  
- [x] **`iface-eventenvelope-encode-cost-8`** — Every event is JSON-encoded twice and AnyJSON-parsed for persistence  
  - **Where:** `AppEnvironment.swift:502-510 (ingest), 683-710 (EventEnvelope), 706-710 (encodeAny round-trips through AnyJSON.parse)`  
  - **Category:** optimization · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** ingest builds an EventEnvelope and JSONEncoder().encode()s it for the payloadJSON on every event. For planEmitted/draftReady/citationAdded, encodeAny encodes the value to a String and then re-parses it through AnyJSON.parse — an encode + parse round-trip — purely to wrap it. A fresh JSONEncoder is also allocated per event (line 503).  
  - **Impact:** Extra allocations and CPU per event on the MainActor; the double-encode is wasteful for the heavier payloads (draftReady can be large). Compounds finding #1's per-token write cost.  
  - **Fix:** Reuse a single JSONEncoder instance; persist the raw encoded Data directly instead of round-tripping through AnyJSON; and only build payloadJSON for the small set of events that actually need a durable payload (the default branch already stores nil data, so skip the encode for those entirely).  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed with a scoping nuance. ingest allocates a fresh JSONEncoder per event and encodes EventEnvelope (AppEnvironment.swift:503). EventEnvelope.encodeAny (706-709) does JSONEncoder().encode(value) -> String, then AnyJSON.parse(str) which re-serializes to Data and JSONDecoder().decode (AnyJSON.swift:38-39) — a full encode + parse round-trip — purely to wrap the value; that wrapped AnyJSON is then re-encoded again by the line-503 encoder (hence 'encoded twice'). This round-trip applies to planEmitted/draftReady/citationAdded only; all OTHER events take the default branch (line 700-702, data=nil) with a single encode. So the title 'Every event is JSON-encoded twice' is overbroad, but the body correctly scopes it to those three event kinds, and the per-event fresh-encoder allocation is universal. Optimization; P2.  
- [x] **`iface-followup-turnhistory-corrupt-7`** — continueResearch archives the wrong role/markdown into turnHistory  
  - **Where:** `AppEnvironment.swift:425-443`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** When archiving the prior turn before a follow-up, the code appends a .user TurnRecord with `query: self.query` and empty markdown, then a .user→assistant pair both using `self.query` (the ORIGINAL session query, not the just-completed turn's question). For the 2nd+ follow-up, self.query is still the first query, so the transcript labels every archived question with the original query text rather than the actual prior follow-up. ConversationView.priorTranscript also computes turn count as turns.count/2 (line 289), which is only correct if entries are always perfectly paired.  
  - **Impact:** The 'Earlier in this conversation' disclosure shows incorrect/duplicated question text for multi-turn conversations, misrepresenting the dialogue history the user relied on. Subtle but user-visible data corruption in the transcript.  
  - **Fix:** Track the current turn's query separately (e.g. a `var activeQuery`) and archive that as the user TurnRecord's query/markdown; stop reusing self.query for both roles. Consider deriving turnHistory from the persisted StoredTurns instead of hand-pairing in memory.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. continueResearch (AppEnvironment.swift:425-443) archives a .user TurnRecord with query: self.query and empty markdown (lines 427-434), then an .assistant TurnRecord also using query: self.query with the draft markdown (435-442). self.query is the immutable original session query (let query, line 324) set once at init — so for the 2nd+ follow-up every archived 'question' is mislabeled with the original query text rather than the actual prior follow-up's question (the per-turn question is passed as the function parameter `query`, which shadows but is never stored into the record). ConversationView.priorTranscript (line 289) computes turn count as turns.count / 2, correct only if entries stay perfectly paired (they are paired here, but it is a brittle coupling). Correctness bug in the visible transcript; P2 fair (cosmetic/transcript-only, does not corrupt the LLM conversation memory which uses the separate ConversationContext).  
- [x] **`iface-inline-citations-tokenize-9`** — InlineCitationsView re-tokenizes and rebuilds the full draft on every token  
  - **Where:** `InlineCitationsView.swift:40-118; DraftCard.swift:14-30 (re-rendered as draftMarkdown grows)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** DraftCard/InlineCitationsView render the streaming draft: as draftMarkdown += text fires per token, the body recomputes `paragraphs` (components(separatedBy: "\n\n") over the whole draft), runs Self.tokenize over each paragraph, and rebuilds AttributedStrings for the entire document every frame. This is O(draftLength) work per token → O(n²) over the full synthesis. MarkdownUI also re-parses each paragraph on each pass.  
  - **Impact:** CPU grows quadratically with draft length during streaming; long syntheses get progressively slower/janky toward the end, exactly when the document is longest. Combined with the per-token save (#1) the streaming experience degrades on big answers.  
  - **Fix:** Throttle the draft view's updates (e.g. update draftMarkdown for the card on a ~100ms timer rather than every token, or render a plain streaming Text during synthesis and only run the full tokenizer/Markdown pass once status flips to .complete). Cache tokenized paragraphs keyed by content hash.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. ConversationView.liveSection renders DraftCard(markdown: live.draftMarkdown) whenever non-empty (line 81), and draftMarkdown += text fires per token (ingest line 558). DraftCard passes stripFinalSourcesSection(from: markdown) (a full-text range scan, DraftCard.swift:107-112) into InlineCitationsView. InlineCitationsView.body recomputes paragraphs = markdown.components(separatedBy: "\n\n") over the WHOLE draft (lines 40-42, body line 22), ForEach over all paragraphs each calling Self.tokenize(raw) (line 46) and rebuilding AttributedStrings / re-instantiating Markdown(raw) views (lines 49-57). This is O(draftLength) per token = O(n^2) over the synthesis, plus MarkdownUI re-parse per paragraph each pass. Mechanism fully accurate. P2 (real UI cost, but SwiftUI/LazyVStack mitigates some re-render; not as catastrophic as the per-token DB save).  
- [x] **`iface-start-precondition-crash-6`** — LiveSession.start() precondition can crash; status guards are inconsistent  
  - **Where:** `AppEnvironment.swift:404-405 (precondition streamTask==nil), 421-422 (continueResearch guard), 495-499 (cancel)`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** start() uses precondition(streamTask == nil) to enforce single-start. startResearch() always constructs a fresh LiveSession so today it holds, but it is a latent landmine: any future path that calls start() twice (or a restored/reused session) hard-crashes the app in release. Meanwhile continueResearch()'s guard `streamTask == nil || status == .complete/.failed/.cancelled` permits a follow-up while a previous streamTask may still be assigned for .cancelled (cancel() nils streamTask, but the ordering coupling is implicit and fragile).  
  - **Impact:** A precondition is a process-terminating assertion on a user-facing action path; if invariants drift the whole app dies rather than degrading. The follow-up guard relies on cancel() having niled streamTask, which is easy to break.  
  - **Fix:** Replace the precondition with a graceful early-return + Log.warning (do not crash on a UI action). Consolidate the in-flight check into a single computed `isRunning` (streamTask != nil && status.isInflight) used by both start and continueResearch, so the rule lives in one place.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed as a latent-robustness finding. start() (AppEnvironment.swift:405) uses precondition(streamTask == nil) which traps in release builds. The only caller today is startResearch (line 282) on a freshly constructed LiveSession, so it holds now (grep confirms no other .start() caller on LiveSession). It is a genuine landmine for any future restore/reuse path. continueResearch's guard (line 422: streamTask == nil || status == .complete/.failed/.cancelled) relies on cancel() (495-499) having nil'd streamTask for the .cancelled case — real but subtle ordering coupling. Speculative ('future path') so P2 is appropriate, not higher.  

#### Settings + Forecast UI

- [x] **`apikey-test-saves-invalid-key`** — Testing an API key writes the unvalidated key to the Keychain before validation  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/SettingsSheet.swift:909-924 (test()), :915`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** APIKeysTab.test() persists the typed value to the Keychain (`await KeychainStore.shared.set(value, for: account)`, line 915) BEFORE calling ModelDiscovery.fetchModels. The comment explains this is so ModelDiscovery can read it, but the consequence is that pressing 'Test' on a mistyped or wrong key silently overwrites a previously-working Keychain key with the bad one. If validation then fails, the broken key is now the persisted key and the next real research/forecast run uses it. There is no revert on failure.  
  - **Impact:** A user testing a key they fat-fingered clobbers their good key and breaks subsequent runs without realizing it — the Test button has a destructive side effect that contradicts its read-only-sounding name.  
  - **Fix:** Validate against a temporary in-memory override instead of writing first (pass the candidate key into ModelDiscovery), or snapshot the prior Keychain value and restore it if the test fails. Only commit on success or on explicit Save.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. APIKeysTab.test() (SettingsSheet.swift:909-924) does `await KeychainStore.shared.set(value, for: account)` (line 915) BEFORE `ModelDiscovery.fetchModels`, and there is no revert in the catch branch (line 920-922 only sets testStates). The comment at line 914 explicitly acknowledges persisting first so ModelDiscovery reads it. So pressing Test on a mistyped key overwrites a previously-working Keychain entry with the bad value and never restores it on validation failure. P2 (data-integrity/robustness, recoverable by re-entering the good key) is reasonable.

The Providers→Forecast role group is a separate surface using ModelProviderManager.forecastEligible + Keychain (lines 352-405). ensureForecastBackend() calls pushForecastProvider(config:) whenever the backend is .running (AppEnvironment.swift:247-252), overriding the manual ForecastTab choice on next launch. - [x] **`settings-persist-every-keystroke`** — onChange(of: env.configuration) persists the entire config on every keystroke  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/SettingsSheet.swift:67-71 (body .onChange); :1557 (Instructions TextEditor)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The root SettingsSheet body attaches `.onChange(of: env.configuration) { env.saveConfiguration() }`. Because EngineConfiguration is a value type with Equatable synthesized over all fields including `systemPromptAddendum: String`, every character typed into the Instructions TextEditor (line 1557, bound to `$env.configuration.systemPromptAddendum`) mutates the struct, fails the Equatable check, and triggers a full JSONEncoder().encode of the whole configuration plus a synchronous UserDefaults write. The same fires for every Stepper tick in Budget/Iteration. saveConfiguration() runs on the MainActor.  
  - **Impact:** Typing a multi-paragraph house-style addendum issues hundreds of synchronous encode+UserDefaults writes on the main thread, causing input latency and disk churn. The comment claims it is 'cheap' but encoding a large addendum string repeatedly is not.  
  - **Fix:** Debounce persistence: keep the onChange but coalesce writes with a Task that cancels-and-resleeps ~300ms, or move the TextEditor to a local @State string committed onSubmit/onDisappear. Alternatively persist only the small fields eagerly and write systemPromptAddendum on sheet dismiss.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. SettingsSheet.body attaches `.onChange(of: env.configuration) { env.saveConfiguration() }` (lines 67-71). EngineConfiguration is `struct ... Codable, Equatable` (EngineConfiguration.swift:38) with `systemPromptAddendum: String` as a field (line 62). InstructionsTab's TextEditor binds to `$env.configuration.systemPromptAddendum` (SettingsSheet.swift:1557). saveConfiguration() (AppEnvironment.swift:82-84) runs `JSONEncoder().encode(configuration)` + synchronous `UserDefaults.standard.set` on the MainActor. So every keystroke and every Stepper tick mutates the struct, fails Equatable, and triggers a full encode+write. P2 (perf/wear bottleneck, not user-blocking) is appropriate.

- [ ] **`error-handling-consistency-1`** — Inconsistent error surfacing: typed EngineFailure in engines vs silent try? and string outcomes elsewhere  
  - **Where:** `AppEnvironment.swift:153,172,183,202 (try?/swallowed); WorkerAgent.swift:176,202 (stringified); ResearchEngine.swift:279 (warning string); DeerFlowEngine.swift:286-288,304-306 (catch → .failed(message:))`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The codebase mixes three error idioms with no clear policy. Engines use the typed `EngineFailure` with a `Kind` enum (good). But: AppEnvironment swallows backend/store errors with `try?` in user-visible flows — deleteForecast (:153), refreshBackendPipelines (:172), createForecast in openBackendPipeline (:202) — so failures vanish with no UI feedback. WorkerAgent converts tool throws to interpolated strings ('Tool X threw: ...', WorkerAgent.swift:176) that become model-visible ERROR text and warning strings (:202), losing structured kind/workerID. ToolOutcome.failed carries only a String (ResearchEvent.swift:99). DeerFlow stringifies tool errors at :287/:305. There is no single convention for which errors degrade silently, which warn, and which fail the run.  
  - **Impact:** Silent try? failures in AppEnvironment mean a delete/import/refresh can no-op with zero user-visible signal (e.g. deleteForecast :153 swallows store delete errors, leaving a phantom record). Stringified tool errors can't be filtered/aggregated and leak into LLM context. Debugging field issues is harder because failure provenance is lost.  
  - **Fix:** Define a policy: (a) replace user-visible `try?` in AppEnvironment with do/catch that sets a surfaced error state or logs via Log.engine.error; (b) give ToolOutcome.failed a typed reason (timeout/http/parse/empty) alongside the message so the worker loop and UI can react; (c) keep EngineFailure as the single thrown type at engine boundaries. Audit the 11 try? sites in Engine/ too.  
  - **Verify:** Central thesis verified: three mixed error idioms with no policy. Typed EngineFailure(kind:) in engines (good). Silent `try?` swallows in user-visible AppEnvironment flows: deleteForecast store.deleteForecast (:152) and client.deletePipeline (:155), refreshBackendPipelines listPipelines (:172), saveChanges (:203). WorkerAgent stringifies tool throws as 'Tool X threw: ...' (:176) and 'ERROR: ...' (:185) becoming model-visible text, plus warning string (:202). DeerFlow stringifies tool errors to .failed(message:) (:287,:305). ToolOutcome.failed carries only a String. ONE cited example is misread: openBackendPipeline's createForecast is NOT a silent try? — it sits in a do/catch that logs via Log.engine.error at :206 (current line numbers drift ~1-3 from cited). Pattern is real and well-evidenced across multiple genuine lines; P2 is appropriate.
