DeerFlowEngine now correctly uses registry.makeClient(role:config:) (ModelProviderManager.swift:37) while ResearchEngine still inlines makeClient(provider:model:...) three times (ResearchEngine.swift:65-88) — so the role-based factory is only half-adopted, exactly the drift this duplication causes.  
  - **Impact:** Two copies of the run-loop boilerplate mean fixes (e.g. the citation/source flow, budget guards, cancellation handling) must be made twice and can silently diverge; ResearchEngine already lags DeerFlow in adopting the unified makeClient(role:) entry point.  
  - **Fix:** Extract a shared `ResearchRunScaffold` (or free function) that builds the stream, clients via role-based makeClient, budget/cache/session, and the standard catch+finish handling, parameterized by a closure that runs the flow body. Migrate ResearchEngine onto makeClient(role:config:) too.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. DeerFlowEngine (lines 43-66, 199-215) and ResearchEngine (lines 47-62, 218-234) duplicate AsyncThrowingStream(.unbounded), the @Sendable emit closure, ContinuousClock start, BudgetMeter/SourceCache creation, providerName assembly, SessionDescriptor, appendUserTurn/appendAssistantTurn, the snapshot+elapsed sessionCompleted emit, and the identical four-arm catch block (CancellationError/EngineFailure/unknown). The half-adopted-factory drift is verified: DeerFlowEngine uses registry.makeClient(role:config:) (DeerFlowEngine.swift:60-62; factory at ModelProviderManager.swift:37-57) while ResearchEngine still inlines makeClient(provider:model:ollamaHost:...) three times (ResearchEngine.swift:65-88). Legitimate maintainability/architecture concern; P3 appropriate.  

#### Forecast / MiroFish Backend Integration

- [ ] **`mirofish-client-1`** — 60s default request timeout is too short for synchronous long-running endpoints; only /chat overrides it  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishClient.swift:28-32, 199-260; reportChat:181-194`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The client uses HTTPClientCommon.defaultSession(timeout: 60), i.e. a 60s timeoutIntervalForRequest (inactivity) for all calls. reportChat correctly bumps to 300s (line 193). But runPipeline (POST /api/research/run) and resumePipeline may block server-side while the backend does synchronous setup before returning a pipeline_id, and graphData on a large graph can take a while to serialize. If any of these exceeds 60s of inactivity the request throws and is mapped to MiroFishError.unreachable (line 248-249), which the poll loop then treats as a transient retry — but for runPipeline a timeout means the pipeline may actually have started server-side while the client gives up, leaking an untracked pipeline.  
  - **Impact:** On slow backends/cold caches, starting a forecast can spuriously fail (or worse, start a backend pipeline the app never records because the POST timed out before returning pipeline_id), orphaning a token-spending run with no local record to cancel it.  
  - **Fix:** Give runPipeline/resumePipeline a generous explicit timeout (the rawData(timeout:) param already supports it) or, better, make the backend return the pipeline_id immediately and asynchronously. On a runPipeline timeout, fall back to listPipelines to discover whether a pipeline was actually created so it can be tracked/cancelled rather than orphaned.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed with a key nuance verified. The client uses HTTPClientCommon.defaultSession(timeout: 60) (MiroFishClient.swift:29); Logging.swift:39-54 confirms `timeout` is timeoutIntervalForRequest — the INACTIVITY window between received bytes (resets per packet), not a wall-clock cap (resourceTimeout defaults to 86400). Only reportChat overrides it to 300 (181-194). So any endpoint that produces NO bytes for >60s (e.g. runPipeline/resumePipeline blocking server-side before emitting the response, or graphData serializing a large graph) throws and is mapped to MiroFishError.unreachable (rawData 246-249), which the poll loop treats as transient — and for runPipeline a timeout could leave a started-but-untracked pipeline. The runPipeline-blocks-60s premise is somewhat speculative (a /run endpoint typically returns pipeline_id quickly for an async job), so likelihood is low. P3 appropriate.  
- [ ] **`mirofish-run-3`** — Restored runs don't seed lastPersistedReportLength / agentLogCursor, causing redundant writes and duplicated agent-log fetches  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/ForecastRun.swift:81-82, 336-346, 416-444 (restored)`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** restored(from:) rehydrates report markdown into run.report (line 432-438) but leaves lastPersistedReportLength = 0 and agentLogCursor = 0. On the first persist() after restore, reportMarkdown.count != 0 is treated as 'changed' and rewrites the (identical) markdown back to SwiftData. More notably, fetchReport calls reportAgentLog(fromLine: 0) on a resumed/hydrated run, re-pulling the entire agent log from line 0 and appending it on top of whatever is already shown (agentLog isn't reset), risking duplicated log entries in the console after a resume.  
  - **Impact:** Minor redundant SwiftData writes plus potential duplicate agent-log lines in the report console after restoring or resuming a run. Cosmetic but user-visible in the live console.  
  - **Fix:** In restored(from:) set lastPersistedReportLength = record.reportMarkdown.count. For the agent log, either persist the cursor on the record or reset agentLog before the first post-restore fetch and rely on total_lines to advance the cursor. Dedupe by MFAgentLogEntry.id when appending.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed for the redundant-write part. restored(from:) (416-444) rehydrates run.report from record.reportMarkdown (432-438) but never sets lastPersistedReportLength or agentLogCursor (both remain 0, declared 81-82). On the first persist() (389-412), reportChanged = (reportMarkdown.count != 0) = true, so it rewrites the identical record.reportMarkdown and calls saveChanges() (403,411). fetchReport calls reportAgentLog(fromLine: 0) (339) re-pulling the full log. The 'duplicated agent-log entries' claim is weaker than stated: restored() leaves agentLog empty, so the first post-restore fetch appends to an empty array (no in-array duplication on a single restore). Net: minor redundant SwiftData write + full-log refetch. P3 appropriate.  
- [ ] **`mirofish-run-4`** — cancel()/resume()/detach() can race the running pollTask because cancellation isn't awaited  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/ForecastRun.swift:111-164 (detach/cancel/resume), 195-239 (pollLoop)`  
  - **Category:** concurrency · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** cancel() and detach() call pollTask?.cancel(); pollTask = nil but never await the task's completion. The poll loop checks Task.isCancelled only at the top of each while iteration and after the runPipeline await; in between it does multiple awaits (pipelineStatus, sideFetches, finalFetches) that can still be in flight when cancel() runs. So after cancel() sets phase = .cancelled and persists, an in-flight sideFetches/ingest can resume and mutate phase/stages/persist again, partially overwriting the cancelled state. resume() guards with `pollTask == nil` but since the old task is nilled without awaiting, resume() can start a new poll loop while the previous one's trailing awaits are still resolving against the same @Observable state.  
  - **Impact:** Cancelled/resumed runs can momentarily flip back to running-looking state, double-persist, or interleave two poll loops mutating the same ForecastRun, producing flickering UI and inconsistent persisted status. All on the MainActor so not memory-unsafe, but logically racy.  
  - **Fix:** Make pollLoop check Task.isCancelled (or `guard !Task.isCancelled` / try Task.checkCancellation()) immediately after each await before mutating state and before persist(). For resume(), capture and await the old task's value (or use a single owned Task you replace atomically) before starting a new loop. Centralize phase transitions so a cancelled phase can't be overwritten by a late-arriving ingest.  
  - **Verify:** Real pattern, overstated impact. cancel() (117-139) and detach() (111-115) do pollTask?.cancel(); pollTask = nil without awaiting. pollLoop (195-239) checks Task.isCancelled only at the top of the while; after the pipelineStatus await (199) it runs ingest/sideFetches/persist with no further isCancelled check, so an in-flight iteration can mutate @Observable state after cancel set phase=.cancelled. resume() guards on `pollTask == nil` (145-146) which is true immediately after cancel nils it, so a new pollLoop can start while the old task's trailing awaits resolve. HOWEVER everything here is @MainActor (12) so there is NO data race — only logical interleaving at await points; ingest/sideFetches do not touch `phase` (only globalProgress/stages/resource fields), and persist writes record.status from the CURRENT phase (.cancelled, terminal), so the cancelled state is not actually reverted. Impact is cosmetic/stale-field, not state corruption. Downgraded to P3.  
- [ ] **`mirofish-run-5`** — ingest() never marks the 'prepare' (Agent Setup) stage and stage statuses can only move forward via backend echo, so a stalled backend leaves stages misleading  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/ForecastRun.swift:251-266, 294; ForecastModels.swift:9-10`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** ingest copies stage status only for keys present in state.stages (line 260). The six stages include 'prepare' (Agent Setup, ForecastModels.swift:10) but sideFetches has no handling for it and there's no fallback if the backend omits a stage key for a stage that's actually done. Because each stage row is updated only when the backend includes that exact key, a backend that reports stages sparsely (or renames a key) leaves rows stuck at .pending even though global_progress advanced — and the simulation side-fetch is gated on stageStatus(.run) (line 294), so a missing 'run' key silently suppresses all simulation telemetry.  
  - **Impact:** The stage stepper can show stages as pending while the run is clearly past them, and simulation telemetry can fail to load if the backend's stage key for 'run' differs from the literal 'run'. User sees a half-rendered pipeline with no error.  
  - **Fix:** Derive a monotonic stage completion from global_progress / current_stage as a fallback when a stage key is absent, and don't gate telemetry side-fetches purely on the local stage status — also fetch when the corresponding id (simulation_id/report_id) is present. Add a debug assertion/log when state.stages contains an unknown key so contract drift is visible.  
  - **Verify:** Behavior confirmed, framing slightly imprecise. Stage statuses are updated in ingest() (259-265) via `if let s = state.stages[stages[i].kind.rawValue]`, so a stage row (including 'prepare', ForecastModels.swift:9) is updated ONLY when the backend includes that exact key — there is no fallback if a key is omitted while global_progress advances, so a sparsely-reporting/renamed-key backend leaves rows stuck at .pending. Also confirmed the simulation side-fetch is gated on stageStatus(.run) == .running/.completed (293-294), so a missing 'run' key suppresses all simulation telemetry. The finding slightly conflates ingest (which DOES handle all six stages incl. prepare) with sideFetches (resource fetches, where 'prepare' legitimately has no streamed resource), but the underlying defensive-correctness gap it describes is real. P3 appropriate.  
- [ ] **`mirofish-supervisor-7`** — quitObserver is registered but never removed; readabilityHandler Tasks can append stale child output across relaunch  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:39, 49-56, 451-455, 474-476`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** registerTermination stores quitObserver but there is no removeObserver anywhere (the actor is a singleton so it lives forever — acceptable, but the field is dead weight and the pattern is a latent leak if ever made non-singleton). More concretely, the launch() readabilityHandler does `Task { await self?.appendLog(text) }` — an unstructured hop. On relaunch, launch() nils the old pipe's handler (line 423) but already-enqueued appendLog Tasks from the previous child can still run after a new child started, appending the dead child's output into the new logTail that statusAfterExit later parses. There's no generation/epoch tag tying log lines to the current process.  
  - **Impact:** Rare but real: a quick fail-then-relaunch can mix old and new child stdout in logTail, so the diagnostic heuristics (ImportError / port-taken / 配置错误) can mis-fire on stale text. The unused observer is minor.  
  - **Fix:** Tag each launch with a monotonically increasing epoch; have appendLog drop text whose epoch != current. Clear logTail on launch (already done) and ignore late log Tasks from a previous epoch. Drop the quitObserver field or actually removeObserver in a deinit-equivalent path if the actor ever stops being a singleton.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed (narrow). quitObserver stored at line 55 and never removed (no removeObserver anywhere); the finding itself concedes the singleton lifetime makes the leak benign. The substantive part: launch() readabilityHandler does `Task { await self?.appendLog(text) }` (451-454) — an unstructured hop. On relaunch, launch() nils the OLD pipe handler (423) and resets logTail = '' (425), but appendLog Tasks already enqueued from the prior child can still run afterward and append the dead child's bytes onto the freshly-reset logTail that statusAfterExit later parses (485-506). No generation/epoch tag ties log lines to the current process. Real but narrow race requiring relaunch with buffered output in flight. P3 appropriate.  

#### Knowledge Base / SeekDB sidecar

- [ ] **`kb-anycodable-silent-null`** — AnyCodableValue silently coerces unknown metadata (e.g. arrays/objects) to .null  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/SeekDBClient.swift:179-187`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** AnyCodableValue.init(from:) tries String/Int/Double/Bool and falls through to `self = .null` for anything else (arrays, nested objects). pyseekdb metadata is currently scalar, but chunk_index is stored as an int and any future structured metadata (lists, nested dicts) would decode to .null with no error. docID/title derivation (lines 93-99) then silently loses that data. Because the catch-all is `self = .null` rather than throwing, a metadata schema change won't surface as a decode error — it just disappears.  
  - **Impact:** Future structured metadata silently vanishes client-side instead of failing loudly, making it hard to diagnose why a hit's title/docID falls back to the raw chunk id.  
  - **Fix:** Either throw DecodingError for unexpected shapes (fail loud) or add explicit array/object cases. At minimum log when the fallback .null is taken so silent data loss is observable.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. AnyCodableValue.init(from:) (SeekDBClient.swift:179-187) tries decodeNil, then String/Int/Double/Bool via try?, and the catch-all is `self = .null` (line 186) rather than throwing. Arrays/nested objects decode to .null with no error. docID/title derivation (lines 93-99) reads metadata?["doc_id"]/["title"]?.stringValue, which would silently yield nil for a structured value. Current pyseekdb metadata is scalar (doc_id string, chunk_index int, title/source strings — set at seekdb_sidecar.py:193-196,207), so this is latent, but a future structured-metadata change disappears silently instead of surfacing a decode error. P3 confirmed.  
- [ ] **`kb-bulk-endpoint-unused`** — /documents/bulk endpoint is implemented in Python but never called from Swift  
  - **Where:** `sidecar/seekdb_sidecar.py:405-421 (upsert_bulk); no Swift caller (grep for 'bulk' in Swift returns nothing)`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The sidecar exposes POST /documents/bulk for batched upserts, but SeekDBClient has no corresponding method and no Swift code calls it. KnowledgeBase.ingestFile does one upsert per file. This is dead surface area today, but it is also the right primitive for efficient multi-file ingestion and for the chunk-batching fix in kb-no-ingest-batching.  
  - **Impact:** Multi-file imports do N sequential round-trips, each re-acquiring the lock and triggering a refresh(); the existing bulk path that would amortize this is unused.  
  - **Fix:** Add a `upsertBulk` method to SeekDBClient and use it for multi-file ingestion, or remove the endpoint to avoid untested dead code. If kept, add at least one test exercising it.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. POST /documents/bulk → upsert_bulk is implemented (seekdb_sidecar.py:405-421). SeekDBClient (SeekDBClient.swift) has no bulk method, and grep for 'bulk' across the Swift sources returns nothing. KnowledgeBase.ingestFile does one upsert per file (line 91). So it is genuinely dead surface area today and is the natural primitive for the batching improvement in kb-no-ingest-batching. P3 improvement confirmed.  
- [x] **`kb-health-doc-count-truthiness`** — Health document count uses truthy guard that hides a legitimately-empty KB nuance  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/KnowledgeBaseTool.swift:73-78`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** On zero hits the tool calls `(try? await client.health().documents) ?? nil` and branches on `if let docCount, docCount > 0`. health().documents is `Int?` from the wire (SeekDBClient.swift:76). If health() throws (sidecar momentarily busy) docCount is nil and the tool reports 'The knowledge base is empty' even when it actually has documents — telling the worker to abandon the KB and use web search when the KB is merely transiently unavailable. The two error states (empty vs unavailable) are conflated into the 'empty' message.  
  - **Impact:** A transient health failure on a populated KB tells the LLM the KB is empty, causing it to stop consulting private documents for the rest of that step — a subtle quality regression in research grounded on user docs.  
  - **Fix:** Distinguish health-failed from documents==0: only emit 'knowledge base is empty' when health() succeeded and returned 0; on a health error, say the count is unknown / try again rather than asserting emptiness.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. KnowledgeBaseTool.swift:73: `let docCount = (try? await client.health().documents) ?? nil`. health().documents is Int? (SeekDBClient.swift:76). If health() throws (try? → nil) or returns documents==nil, docCount is nil and the `if let docCount, docCount > 0` (line 74) is false → the else branch reports 'The knowledge base is empty (no documents indexed). Use web_search instead' (line 77), conflating transient-unavailable with truly-empty. This only runs when hits.isEmpty (line 72), and queryWithRecovery already succeeded to reach here, so the window for health() to fail immediately after is narrow — but real. P3 is appropriate.  
- [ ] **`kb-no-request-size-limit`** — Sidecar has no request body size limit or auth; large/abusive payloads unbounded  
  - **Where:** `sidecar/seekdb_sidecar.py:367-439 (build_app, no middleware), 15 (no auth by design)`  
  - **Category:** security · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The FastAPI app sets no max request body size and no auth. It binds 127.0.0.1 by default (reasonable for a localhost sidecar), but any local process can post arbitrarily large documents that are fully buffered and embedded under the global lock, and can hit /reset to wipe the collection. For a single-user localhost tool this is low risk, but there is no defense-in-depth (no shared-secret token between Swift and the sidecar, no body cap).  
  - **Impact:** A misbehaving or malicious local process could wipe the KB (/reset) or DoS it with a huge document; not exploitable remotely while bound to loopback.  
  - **Fix:** Add a per-request body size limit and a simple shared-secret header that the supervisor passes via env and requires on mutating endpoints, so only the app (not arbitrary local processes) can mutate the KB.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. build_app (seekdb_sidecar.py:367-439) constructs FastAPI(...) and registers routes with no middleware, no max-body-size, and no auth (module docstring line 15 states 'no auth — bound to localhost only by default'). It binds 127.0.0.1 by default (--host default line 481), and /reset (430-433) wipes the collection with no protection. Any local process can post unbounded documents (fully buffered + embedded under the global lock) or call /reset. No shared-secret token between Swift and the sidecar. Low risk for a single-user localhost tool, so P3 is appropriate.  
- [ ] **`kb-pdf-no-ocr-no-error-on-empty-pages`** — PDF extraction silently yields empty text for scanned/image PDFs, then reports 'no readable text'  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/KnowledgeBase.swift:170-182 (extractPDF), 124-126 (empty guard)`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** extractPDF concatenates page.string for each page; for scanned/image-only PDFs every page.string is nil/empty, so the result is empty and the caller throws KBError.emptyText. There is no OCR fallback (Vision/VNRecognizeTextRequest is available on macOS) and no per-page diagnostic. The user just sees 'No readable text was found' with no hint that the PDF is image-based and would need OCR.  
  - **Impact:** A large class of user documents (scanned papers, image PDFs) cannot be ingested at all, limiting the KB's coverage for exactly the academic/paper use case the tool description advertises.  
  - **Fix:** When PDFKit text extraction yields empty/near-empty output, fall back to Vision OCR (VNRecognizeTextRequest over rendered pages) and/or give a specific error telling the user the PDF appears image-based.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. extractPDF (KnowledgeBase.swift:171-182) iterates pages and concatenates `doc.page(at: i)?.string`; for scanned/image-only PDFs every page.string is nil so `out` stays empty. ingestFile then hits the empty guard (lines 124-126) and throws KBError.emptyText → 'No readable text was found in <title>' (line 165). No OCR fallback (no Vision/VNRecognizeTextRequest anywhere) and no per-page diagnostic distinguishing 'image-based PDF needs OCR' from a genuinely empty file. Correctly categorized as an improvement at P3.  
- [x] **`kb-probe-shared-session`** — probeHealth uses URLSession.shared while clients use ephemeral sessions  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/SidecarSupervisor.swift:475-485 (probeHealth)`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** probeHealth() uses URLSession.shared with a 1.5s timeoutInterval, whereas SeekDBClient uses an ephemeral session (no cache/cookie storage). URLSession.shared persists connections/caches and is shared app-wide; for a rapid-poll readiness loop (waitForHealthOrExit polls 180× at 500ms) this is mostly fine, but mixing session types means probe behavior (connection reuse, caching of a 200 /health) differs from real client calls. A cached 200 could in theory mask a sidecar that just died, though /health is dynamic so caching risk is low.  
  - **Impact:** Minor inconsistency; low risk of a cached health response masking a transition. Mainly a maintainability/consistency concern.  
  - **Fix:** Use a small dedicated ephemeral URLSession (cache policy .reloadIgnoringLocalCacheData) for health probing so probe semantics match the client and never serve a cached 200.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. probeHealth (SidecarSupervisor.swift:475-485) uses URLSession.shared with req.timeoutInterval = 1.5, whereas SeekDBClient uses an ephemeral session — HTTPClientCommon.defaultSession builds URLSessionConfiguration.ephemeral (Logging.swift:52). So the readiness probe and the real client use different session types (connection reuse / caching behavior differ). waitForHealthOrExit polls up to 180× at 500ms (lines 496-498) via this shared session. As the finding notes, /health is dynamic so cached-200 masking is a low/theoretical risk. Correctly rated P3 as a minor consistency improvement.  
- [ ] **`kb-utf8-only-text-load`** — Text-file ingestion assumes UTF-8 and fails on other encodings  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/KnowledgeBase.swift:115-122`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** For md/txt/csv/json/etc the code does `String(contentsOf: url, encoding: .utf8)` and only the default branch attempts `try?` fallback. A known text extension with non-UTF-8 content (UTF-16, Latin-1, files with a BOM) throws and ingestion fails. `String(contentsOf:usedEncoding:)` would auto-detect, or the default-branch try? logic should also apply to known extensions.  
  - **Impact:** Common non-UTF-8 text files (Windows-exported CSV/TXT, UTF-16) fail to ingest with a generic decode error.  
  - **Fix:** Use `String(contentsOf: url, usedEncoding:)` to let Foundation sniff the encoding, falling back to UTF-8/Latin-1, for all text extensions rather than hardcoding .utf8.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. KnowledgeBase.swift:115-122: for md/markdown/txt/rtf/csv/json/yaml/yml/html/htm the code does `try String(contentsOf: url, encoding: .utf8)` (line 116) with NO fallback — a known extension holding UTF-16/Latin-1/BOM content throws and ingestion fails (caught at line 128, surfaced as lastError). Only the default branch (line 118) attempts a `try?` and even that is still .utf8, so it doesn't actually help non-UTF-8 either. String(contentsOf:usedEncoding:) would auto-detect. P3 confirmed.  

#### LLM Provider Layer

- [ ] **`anyjson-number-precision-int64`** — AnyJSON collapses all numbers to Double — large integer tool-args/ids lose precision  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/AnyJSON.swift:6-23,37-40`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** `AnyJSON` decodes every numeric value as `.number(Double)`. Tool argument JSON, tool input schemas, and Gemini/Anthropic `args` are round-tripped through AnyJSON (e.g. Anthropic toolUse `input: AnyJSON.parse(args)`, Gemini FnCall args, Ollama WireFnCall arguments). Any integer beyond 2^53 (timestamps in ms are fine, but ids, large counts, financial cents, bigints) loses precision, and integers re-encode as `1.0` rather than `1`, which some strict tool/schema validators reject. There is no integer case.  
  - **Impact:** Tool calls whose arguments contain large integers or that require integer-typed JSON (not float) can be silently corrupted or rejected by the tool/provider, breaking tool execution. Re-encoded schemas may show `"minimum": 0.0` etc.  
  - **Fix:** Add an `.int(Int64)` case (try `Int64` before `Double` in init(from:)), or carry the original number losslessly (e.g. decode to a Decimal / preserve integer-ness). At minimum, encode whole Doubles as integers to avoid `1.0` in tool args/schemas.  
  - **Verify:** Partially correct. AnyJSON decodes every number as .number(Double) (AnyJSON.swift:17) with no integer case, and round-trips through it for Anthropic toolUse input (AnthropicClient 166), Gemini args (147), Ollama args (232). Empirically tested: integers > 2^53 DO lose precision (9007199254740993 -> 9007199254740992), so that part is TRUE. BUT the claim's central assertion that integers `re-encode as 1.0 rather than 1` is FALSE: Swift JSONEncoder serializes whole-valued Doubles without a decimal point ({"count":1} -> {"count":1}, {"page":2,"limit":10} round-trips exactly), so the strict-validator-rejection concern is moot. Only ids/counts beyond 2^53 are affected (vanishingly rare in tool args/schemas). Real but very narrow; the headline mechanism is overstated -> downgrade P2 to P3.  
- [x] **`gemini-toolresult-name-mismatch`** — Gemini tool-call ids are synthesized and not matched to functionResponse names on multi-call turns  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/GeminiClient.swift:156-169,206-219`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Gemini function calls get a fresh synthetic id per parse (`gemini-fn-<name>-<uuid>`), and on the return trip tool results are matched back to Gemini by `functionResponse.name` (lines 159-163), not id. If a single assistant turn calls the SAME function twice with different args (common: two web_search calls with different phrasings — explicitly encouraged in the worker prompt at WorkerAgent.swift:79-80), both functionResponses carry the same `name`, and Gemini cannot disambiguate which response pairs with which call. Gemini's API supports a `functionCall.id`/`functionResponse.id` pairing that is not used.  
  - **Impact:** Multi-call-same-tool turns (which the worker prompt actively encourages) can mis-associate tool outputs on Gemini, degrading multi-source research quality specifically on Gemini.  
  - **Fix:** Emit and round-trip Gemini's `functionCall.id`/`functionResponse.id` (populate `FnCall`/`FnResp` with the id) so parallel same-name calls pair correctly. Preserve the worker's call id in the wire id rather than minting a throwaway uuid.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed: GeminiClient mints a fresh synthetic id per parse `gemini-fn-<name>-<uuid prefix>` (line 215) and discards it on the return trip — tool results are encoded as FnResp(name: name, response:) by NAME only (159-163), no id. If one assistant turn calls the same function twice (the worker prompt at WorkerAgent.swift:78-80 does encourage two web_search calls with different phrasings), both functionResponses carry the same name and Gemini cannot pair them. Gemini supports functionCall.id/functionResponse.id pairing that is unused. Accurate; genuine edge case (only same-name multi-call turns) -> P3 correct.  
- [x] **`openai-compat-done-after-finish-double-terminal`** — OpenAI-compat can emit two .finished chunks (finish_reason then [DONE])  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/OpenAICompatibleClient.swift:102-115,461-471`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** Most OpenAI-compatible servers send a delta with `finish_reason` (which yields `.finished(...)` at line 470) AND then a separate `data: [DONE]` line, which yields ANOTHER `.finished(reason: .stop)` at line 106. So a `tool_calls` finish is immediately followed by a `.stop` finish. In `complete()` (LLMClient.swift:65-66) the last finish wins, so a tool-call turn's finishReason is clobbered to `.stop`. WorkerAgent doesn't branch on finishReason (it keys off `toolCalls.isEmpty`), so it survives, but any consumer that trusts finishReason (and the Reflector/DeerFlow `complete()` callers) get the wrong reason. The `[DONE]` branch also re-emits `toolCallEnd` for buffered calls that were likely already ended.  
  - **Impact:** FinishReason is unreliable for OpenAI-compat tool-call turns (reported as .stop instead of .toolUse); potential duplicate toolCallEnd events. Currently masked by WorkerAgent's logic but a latent correctness trap for any finishReason-driven control flow.  
  - **Fix:** Track whether a finish_reason was already emitted; on `[DONE]` only emit a terminal `.finished` if none was seen yet, and don't re-emit toolCallEnd for calls already closed. Or treat `[DONE]` purely as a loop-break sentinel and never synthesize a second finish when `sawTerminal` is already true.  
  - **Verify:** Factually confirmed: a delta with finish_reason yields `.finished(reason)` (line 470) and a following `data: [DONE]` yields a second `.finished(reason: .stop)` (line 106); complete() last-wins (LLMClient.swift:65) so a tool_calls finish is clobbered to .stop. However the claim's own caveat is decisive: NO consumer branches on finishReason anywhere (grep confirmed; WorkerAgent keys off toolCalls.isEmpty at line 138; Reflector/Planner/DeerFlow read only .text). The `[DONE]` branch re-emitting toolCallEnd is also harmless because toolCallEnd is a no-op (`continue` at LLMClient.swift:61-62) and no streaming consumer reads tool-call events. Zero practical impact -> P2 overstated, downgrade to P3.  
- [x] **`openai-compat-error-body-line-buffer-unbounded-utf8`** — Error-body drain uses bytes.lines and may hang on a server that streams without newlines  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/OpenAICompatibleClient.swift:90-94`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** On a non-2xx, the client drains the error body via `for try await line in bytes.lines`, breaking after 1200 chars. `bytes.lines` only yields on a newline; a misbehaving gateway that returns an error payload with no newline (or a very long single line before any newline) won't yield a line until a newline arrives, so the 1200-char cap can't trigger and the drain blocks until the inactivity timeout (300s). The success-path SSE parser is fine; this is the error path only.  
  - **Impact:** A pathological error response with no early newline can stall error reporting for up to the request timeout instead of failing fast, delaying the user-visible error.  
  - **Fix:** Drain raw bytes with a hard byte cap (as OllamaClient does at lines 69-73: accumulate Data, break at N bytes) rather than `bytes.lines`, then decode. This bounds the drain by bytes regardless of newline placement.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed at OpenAICompatibleClient.swift:90-94: error body drained via `for try await line in bytes.lines` with the `errBody.count > 1200` break INSIDE the loop body. URLSession.AsyncBytes.lines yields only on a newline, so an error payload with no newline (or a very long single line) won't yield a line, the 1200 cap can't fire, and the drain blocks until the 300s inactivity timeout (default session timeout, AnthropicClient.swift:16 pattern). Success-path SSE parser is byte-based (SSEParser splits on 0x0A) and unaffected. Error path only, low frequency -> P3 correct.  
- [x] **`openai-compat-models-url-replace-fragile`** — resolveModelsURL string-replaces /chat/completions and can corrupt custom endpoints  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/OpenAICompatibleClient.swift:230-251`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** low · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `resolveModelsURL` derives the models endpoint by `resolveChatCompletionsURL` then string-replacing the last `/chat/completions` with `/models`. For a user custom endpoint that already points directly at `.../chat/completions` (handled as-is by resolveChatCompletionsURL), this is fine, but for hosts where the chat path and models path don't share a parent (some gateways expose `/chat/completions` and `/models` under different prefixes, or include a deployment segment), the naive replace yields a wrong models URL. Discovery then 404s and the 'Test & fetch models' button reports failure for an otherwise-working endpoint.  
  - **Impact:** Model discovery / key-test can spuriously fail for non-standard OpenAI-compatible gateways even when chat completions work, blocking the Settings 'Test & fetch models' affordance.  
  - **Fix:** Derive the models URL from the original base (strip a trailing `/chat/completions` or `/v1` and append `/v1/models` consistently with how the chat URL is built) rather than mutating the resolved chat URL, and fall back to the user-entered base + `/models` when the structure is unexpected.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed at OpenAICompatibleClient.swift:244-251: resolveModelsURL takes resolveChatCompletionsURL then replaces the last `/chat/completions` with `/models` (range(of:options:.backwards)). For a gateway where chat and models paths don't share a parent (different prefixes, or a deployment segment), this yields a wrong /models URL, so listModels 404s and Settings 'Test & fetch models' reports failure for an otherwise-working chat endpoint. Niche but real -> P3 correct.  
- [x] **`openai-compat-toolcallend-not-emitted-on-finish`** — toolCallEnd only emitted on [DONE]/stream-close, not on finish_reason=tool_calls  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/OpenAICompatibleClient.swift:104-109,461-471`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** `toolCallEnd` for buffered tool calls is flushed only in the `[DONE]` branch (line 105) and in the no-terminal close branch (line 125). If a server emits `finish_reason: tool_calls` and then closes the stream WITHOUT a `[DONE]` line (some gateways/Azure deployments do this), `sawTerminal` becomes true at line 469, so the no-terminal flush at 119-133 is skipped, and `toolCallEnd` is never yielded for those calls.  
  - **Impact:** Downstream consumers relying on `toolCallEnd` to finalize a streamed tool call (e.g. UI that shows tool-call boundaries, or any incremental argument-complete signal) never receive the end event. `complete()` ignores toolCallEnd so workers are unaffected, but the streaming contract is violated for these servers.  
  - **Fix:** Emit `toolCallEnd` for all buffered calls whenever a terminal finish_reason is seen (in parseEvent when finish_reason is set), not only on `[DONE]`. Centralize tool-buffer flushing in one place that runs on any terminal condition.  
  - **Verify:** Control flow confirmed: a finish_reason delta sets sawFinish=sawTerminal=true and yields .finished WITHOUT flushing toolCallEnd (lines 461-471); if the stream then closes without [DONE], the no-terminal flush at 119-133 is skipped (sawTerminal true), so toolCallEnd is never emitted for buffered calls. BUT toolCallEnd is consumed only inside complete() as `continue` (LLMClient.swift:61-62) and grep shows no streaming consumer reads any toolCall* event. So missing toolCallEnd changes nothing downstream. Real wire-level gap but no behavioral consequence -> downgrade P2 to P3.  
- [ ] **`shared-error-mapping-duplication-improvement`** — Factor shared HTTP error-body extraction + status mapping into the common layer  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/LLM/OpenAICompatibleClient.swift:175-221 vs AnthropicClient.swift:270-279 / GeminiClient.swift:76-79 / OllamaClient.swift:65-82`  
  - **Category:** architecture · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Only OpenAICompatibleClient has rich status-code→guidance mapping (`httpError`) and JSON error-message extraction (`extractErrorMessage`). Anthropic emits bare 'HTTP n', Gemini emits bare 'HTTP n' with no body drain, Ollama hand-rolls its own body snippet. The transient-error classifier is likewise duplicated three ways (see gemini-retry finding). This is the root cause behind several of the per-provider error-handling defects above.  
  - **Impact:** Inconsistent, lower-quality error messages on every non-OpenAI provider, and each new provider re-implements (or forgets) body drain + status mapping. Maintenance burden and uneven UX.  
  - **Fix:** Extract a shared `HTTPProviderError` helper (drain bounded body bytes, parse common error shapes incl. Anthropic/Gemini, map 401/402/403/404/422/429/5xx to actionable messages) and a single `isTransient` in the shared layer; have all REST clients call it. This single refactor fixes anthropic-error-body-discarded and the retry-classifier divergence at once.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed architecture observation: only OpenAICompatibleClient has rich status->guidance mapping (httpError 175-206) and JSON error extraction (extractErrorMessage 210-221). AnthropicClient emits bare `Anthropic HTTP n` with no body (270-279), GeminiClient emits bare `Gemini HTTP n` with no body drain (76-79), OllamaClient hand-rolls its own 280-char snippet (65-82). Transient classification is duplicated (RetryPolicy.isTransient vs OpenAICompatibleClient.isTransient). Accurate; this duplication is the root cause behind anthropic-error-body-discarded and gemini-retry findings. Valid refactor, low priority -> P3.  

#### Research Tools

- [ ] **`research-tools-arxiv-no-ratelimit`** — arXiv/Wikipedia/Reddit lack the 200-OK-with-error-body and rate-limit nuance the other paths have  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/ArXivTool.swift:54-58; RedditTool.swift:93-96`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** arXiv returns HTTP 200 with an Atom feed even for malformed queries; the parser (AtomParser) just yields 0 entries, reported as success with 0 papers — no signal the query syntax was wrong. arXiv also imposes a courtesy rate limit (~1 req/3s) and a required descriptive User-Agent which this client does not set (it inherits the generic SwiftDeepResearch UA, not the browser UA, and no email contact), risking throttling under the parallel fan-out. Reddit correctly notes rate-limits in its error string but does not honor Retry-After beyond the generic dataWithRetry, and a 429 on the unauth JSON endpoint is common enough that thread/search will frequently just fail.  
  - **Impact:** Scientific and community sources silently return empty or get throttled during multi-worker runs, weakening exactly the specialized coverage these tools were added to provide.  
  - **Fix:** Set a descriptive arXiv-compliant User-Agent; treat 0 arXiv entries with a hint about query syntax; for Reddit consider a small global rate-limiter/token-bucket shared across workers (an actor) since all workers hit the same unauth endpoint, and surface 429 distinctly so the LLM falls back to web_search.  
  - **Verify:** Verified ArXivTool.swift:54 uses bare URLRequest(url:) (no custom UA), so it inherits the session default UA 'SwiftDeepResearch/2.0' (Logging.swift:28), not browserUserAgent, and sets no email contact -- matches the finding. arXiv returns 200 + Atom even for bad queries; AtomParser yields 0 entries and the tool reports .ok 'arXiv: 0 papers' (line 72) with no syntax-error signal. No client-side ~1req/3s throttle exists. WikipediaTool.swift:66 search path is the same (bare URLRequest). One inaccuracy: dataWithRetry (Logging.swift:80-118) DOES retry 429 and DOES honor Retry-After, so Reddit gets up to 3 attempts -- the finding concedes this ('beyond the generic dataWithRetry'), but its framing slightly understates existing 429 handling. Net robustness gap is real; P3 appropriate.  
- [x] **`research-tools-brave-query-encoding`** — Brave/DDG build URLs by string interpolation; force-unwrap on percent-encoded query is fragile  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/WebSearchTool.swift:233-234,270`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Brave constructs its request URL via `URL(string: "...?q=\(escaped)&count=\(limit)")!` with a force-unwrap. `escaped` uses `.urlQueryAllowed`, which permits `&`, `=`, `+`, and `?` — characters that are valid in a query but will be MIS-parsed when concatenated into a query string the app is assembling by hand (a query containing `&` or `=` corrupts the parameter boundaries). `URLComponents` with `queryItems` (as arXiv/Wikipedia/Reddit correctly use) would encode these safely. The force-unwrap means a query that does produce an invalid URL crashes the tool instead of returning `.failed`.  
  - **Impact:** Queries containing `&`, `=`, `#`, or `+` can silently send the wrong query to Brave (wrong/empty results) or, in edge cases, force-unwrap-crash the search path.  
  - **Fix:** Use `URLComponents` + `queryItems` for Brave (and avoid the force-unwrap), matching the safe pattern already used in ArXivTool/WikipediaTool/RedditTool. `.urlQueryAllowed` is not safe for individual values being concatenated into a query string.  
  - **Verify:** Verified WebSearchTool.swift:233-234 builds the Brave URL by interpolating `escaped` (encoded with .urlQueryAllowed) into a hand-assembled query string with a force-unwrap; DDG line 270 does the same. I compiled a test: .urlQueryAllowed permits &, =, +, ? (all true). Query 'AT&T earnings' -> the parsed q param becomes just 'AT' (the rest splits into broken params); 'what is 2+2' -> q decodes to 'what is 2 2' (+ treated as space). So special-char queries are silently corrupted -- the core, confirmed defect. URLComponents+queryItems (as arXiv/Wikipedia/Reddit correctly use) would encode safely. The force-unwrap-crash sub-claim is largely theoretical: in all tested cases URL(string:) did not return nil after .urlQueryAllowed encoding (it is very permissive). P3 appropriate -- the silent search-quality degradation is the real issue, not a crash.  
- [ ] **`research-tools-cache-poisoning-errors`** — SourceCache caches success only, but in-flight task can hand a thrown error to a waiting worker without retry  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Shared/SourceCache.swift:32-45`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `fetch` shares the in-flight `Task` so a second worker awaiting the same URL receives whatever the first worker's extractor throws (line 36-37 `return try await task.value`). If worker A's fetch hits a transient failure that A's caller would have retried/released, worker B awaiting the same key gets the same thrown error and also fails — a single transient blip fails every concurrent requester of that URL. Errors are (correctly) not cached for future calls, but concurrent waiters are coupled to the first attempt's outcome. Conversely there's no negative caching, so a permanently-bad URL is re-attempted (with full retry budget) by every later worker.  
  - **Impact:** Transient failure on a popular URL fails all concurrent workers asking for it; permanently-bad URLs waste repeated retry budget across the run.  
  - **Fix:** On a thrown error, let waiters fall through to their own fresh attempt rather than inheriting the failure (or wrap the in-flight task so failures aren't shared); add short-lived negative caching for hard failures (4xx/invalid-PDF) to avoid re-fetch storms.  
  - **Verify:** Verified SourceCache.swift:36-37: a second worker awaiting an in-flight key does `return try await task.value`, receiving whatever the first worker's extractor throws -- concurrent waiters are coupled to the first attempt's outcome. store() (line 47) runs only after `try await task.value` succeeds (line 42-43), so errors are correctly NOT cached, but there is no negative caching, so a permanently-bad URL is re-attempted with full dataWithRetry budget (3 attempts) by every later worker. Mitigation noted: each extractor already wraps dataWithRetry, so a single transient blip is retried before reaching the shared task; the residual coupling/no-negative-cache concern is real but low-impact. P3 appropriate.  
- [ ] **`research-tools-capability-gaps`** — Capability gaps: no dedicated news/recency tool, no Scholar/Semantic Scholar, no GitHub/code, no batch-fetch  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/ (tool roster) ; WebSearchTool.swift:39-54`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** L · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The roster (web_search, fetch_url, read_pdf, arxiv, wikipedia, reddit, calculator, datetime, knowledge_base) covers a lot but has notable holes for deep research: (1) no time-bounded/news search — none of the backends are asked for a date range, so 'what happened this week' relies on generic ranking; (2) no Semantic Scholar/Google Scholar/Crossref for peer-reviewed citation graphs (arXiv only covers preprints); (3) no GitHub/code search for technical questions; (4) no batch fetch_url, so reading N sources costs N tool calls against maxToolCallsPerWorker (6 in fast mode), throttling source diversity the prompt explicitly demands (WorkerAgent.swift:80-86 asks for 3+ fetches but fast budget allows only 6 total calls); (5) Tavily's `include_answer:false` (WebSearchTool.swift:157) and unused `include_raw_content` mean the agent re-fetches pages it could have gotten inline.  
  - **Impact:** Research is weaker on recency, peer-reviewed literature, and code-centric questions; the tight tool-call budget plus one-URL-per-call forces shallow source coverage in fast/standard modes despite prompts demanding triangulation.  
  - **Fix:** Add (a) a recency parameter to web_search mapping to Tavily `days`/Brave `freshness`/Exa date filters; (b) a Semantic Scholar / Crossref tool; (c) optionally a batch `fetch_urls` tool (or accept an array in fetch_url) to amortize tool-call budget; (d) set Tavily `include_raw_content:true` to reuse already-crawled page text and cut redundant fetch_url round-trips.  
  - **Verify:** Verified the roster in ResearchEngine.makeTools (ResearchEngine.swift:295-308): web_search, fetch_url, wikipedia, arxiv_search, read_pdf, reddit, current_datetime, calculator, + conditional knowledge_base. Confirmed gaps: (1) no time-bounded/news search -- no backend takes a date range; (2) no Semantic Scholar/Crossref/Scholar (arXiv only, preprints); (3) no GitHub/code search; (4) no batch fetch_url, so N sources cost N tool calls against maxToolCallsPerWorker (6 fast, AgentBudget.swift:36) while the prompt asks for 3+ fetches (WorkerAgent.swift:82); (5) Tavily uses include_answer:false (WebSearchTool.swift:157) and never requests include_raw_content, so the agent re-fetches pages it could have inlined. All accurate as improvement suggestions; P3.  
- [ ] **`research-tools-charge-after-budget`** — Token charge happens after work, so tool output can blow far past maxTokens before the cap trips  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/WebReaderTool.swift:69; PDFReaderTool.swift:73; WebSearchTool.swift:81; WorkerAgent.swift:131,167`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Tools charge `extractedText.count/4` tokens AFTER fetching/extracting (and `context.charge` swallows the thrown budget error via `try?` in WorkerAgent.swift:167). Because charging is post-hoc and the throw is discarded inside the tool context, a worker can fetch several 20-40k-char sources and only discover it's over `maxTokens` on the next `budget.chargeTokens` in the main loop. The estimate `count/4` is also a rough proxy that ignores the actual tokens the LLM will consume when the payload is re-sent in `messages` each subsequent hop (the full payload JSON is appended at WorkerAgent.swift:187 and resent every turn), so real token usage is undercounted multiplicatively across hops.  
  - **Impact:** Budget caps are leaky and under-count the dominant cost (repeatedly resending large tool payloads in the growing message array), so 'thorough' runs can exceed intended token/cost envelopes; conversely, the swallowed throw means a tool that pushes over budget doesn't stop that tool.  
  - **Fix:** Account for the fact that tool payloads are resent each hop (e.g. trim/clip older tool results from the message history, or summarize fetched text before reinjecting); surface the budget-exceeded throw from `context.charge` so the worker stops promptly; consider charging an estimate before doing the expensive fetch when near the cap.  
  - **Verify:** Verified tools call `await context.charge(fetched.extractedText.count / 4)` AFTER fetch/extract (WebReaderTool.swift:69, PDFReaderTool.swift:73, WebSearchTool.swift:81). context.charge is wired in WorkerAgent.swift:167 as `{ tokens in try? await self.budget.chargeTokens(tokens) }`, so the budgetExceeded throw from chargeTokens (AgentBudget.swift:67-73) is swallowed by `try?` inside the tool -- the over-budget condition only surfaces on the next budget.chargeTokens at the main loop (WorkerAgent.swift:131). So a worker can fetch several 20-40k-char sources and only discover it's over maxTokens later. count/4 is a rough proxy; the full tool payload is appended to messages (line 187) and resent every hop (line 129), so real token cost is undercounted multiplicatively across hops. Confirmed; P3.  
- [ ] **`research-tools-no-html-in-pdf-detect-vs-fetch`** — Cross-tool handoff: read_pdf rejects HTML but doesn't auto-fall back to fetch_url  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/PDFReaderTool.swift:59-63`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** When `read_pdf` is given a URL that returns HTML (common: arXiv abstract pages, landing pages, paywalls returning an HTML notice), it correctly detects the mismatch and returns `.failed` telling the LLM to 'use fetch_url for HTML pages'. That relies on the LLM noticing the error string and issuing another tool call — costing a tool-call-budget slot and a round-trip, and the worker may instead give up. Symmetrically, `fetch_url` on a PDF URL will hand binary PDF bytes to SwiftSoup and produce garbage/near-empty text, then either succeed with junk or trigger the JS fallback pointlessly.  
  - **Impact:** Wasted tool-call budget and round-trips on content-type mismatches that the engine has enough information to resolve itself; degraded extraction when fetch_url is pointed at a PDF.  
  - **Fix:** Detect content-type in both tools and transparently route: if read_pdf sees HTML, fall through to the HTML extractor; if fetch_url sees application/pdf (by header or magic bytes %PDF), route to the PDF extractor. This removes a class of avoidable LLM retries.  
  - **Verify:** Verified PDFReaderTool.swift:59-63 detects an HTML/XML mime and returns .failed telling the LLM to 'use fetch_url for HTML pages' -- but does NOT auto-fall back to fetch_url; it relies on the LLM noticing the error string and spending another tool-call slot. Symmetric gap confirmed: WebReaderTool has no PDF content-type detection (grep of WebReaderTool.swift shows Content-Type read only for charset, no application/pdf branch), so fetch_url on a PDF URL hands binary bytes to SwiftSoup, yields near-empty text, trips the <200-char heuristic (line 122), and pointlessly invokes the WKWebView JS fallback. Valid cross-tool-handoff improvement; P3.  

#### Storage, Domain, Shared utilities

- [ ] **`budget-wallclock-not-monotonic-check-14`** — Wall-clock cap is only checked at loop boundaries, not enforced during long streaming synthesis  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Domain/AgentBudget.swift:101-107; Engine/ResearchEngine.swift:142, WorkerAgent.swift:125`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** checkWallClock() is correct (uses ContinuousClock, monotonic) but is only invoked at discrete points (ResearchEngine.swift:142, WorkerAgent.swift:125, DeerFlowEngine boundaries). A single synthesis stream that runs past maxWallClock is not interrupted mid-stream — the cap only fires the next time a boundary check runs, which for a long final synthesis may be never until the stream ends. The comment in AgentBudget even notes synthesis 'consistently runs 4-8 minutes'.  
  - **Impact:** A pathologically slow or hung synthesis stream can exceed the wall-clock budget arbitrarily because nothing cancels it once the final stream begins; the cap is advisory at exactly the stage most likely to overrun.  
  - **Fix:** Wrap the synthesis stream in a Task with a timeout (Task.sleep(for: remainingWallClock) racing the stream, or a withThrowingTaskGroup deadline) so the wall-clock cap can actually cancel an in-flight stream, not just gate the next iteration.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. checkWallClock (AgentBudget.swift:101-107) is monotonic (ContinuousClock) but only invoked at discrete boundaries: ResearchEngine.swift:142 (top of each reflection round) and WorkerAgent.swift:125 (top of each tool hop). The synthesis stream (Synthesizer.swift:93-104) iterates `for try await chunk in stream` with no wall-clock check inside, so a long final synthesis is not interrupted mid-stream — only the next boundary check fires, which for the final synthesis may never run until the stream ends. The 'consistently runs 4-8 minutes' comment is present (AgentBudget.swift:27-31). Accurate; P3 appropriate.  
- [ ] **`cache-extractor-cancellation-poisoning-11`** — SourceCache shares one in-flight Task whose cancellation poisons all concurrent awaiters  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Shared/SourceCache.swift:32-45`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** fetch() registers an unstructured Task and uses `defer { inFlight[key] = nil }`. If worker A's extractor throws (or A's enclosing task is cancelled, propagating CancellationError into the shared Task), every worker B currently awaiting task.value receives A's failure/cancellation even though B is not cancelled. Because the Task inherits the ambient context, A's cancellation can poison B's otherwise-valid fetch. The defer does correctly clear inFlight so a later retry re-fetches, but concurrent awaiters in the same generation all fail together.  
  - **Impact:** One worker's cancellation or transient failure can spuriously fail other workers that happened to request the same URL at the same moment, losing sources they would otherwise have gotten.  
  - **Fix:** Detach the shared fetch from any single caller's cancellation: create the in-flight work with Task.detached (or an unstructured Task that ignores caller cancellation) so the shared fetch's lifetime is the cache's, not worker A's. Keep clearing inFlight on completion so failures are retryable.  
  - **Verify:** Partly verified. SourceCache.fetch (SourceCache.swift:39-44) registers one shared `Task { try await extractor(url) }`, awaited by all concurrent callers via `try await task.value`; `defer { inFlight[key] = nil }` clears it. If the extractor throws (network error), every concurrent awaiter of the same generation receives that same failure — the shared-fate robustness concern is real. The cancellation sub-claim is imprecise: an unstructured `Task {}` is not a child task and does NOT inherit cancellation from the creating task, so cancelling worker A does not automatically cancel the shared Task. Core observation valid; P3 appropriate (and the finding hedges).  
- [ ] **`event-envelope-lossy-payload-12`** — Persisted event payload is lossy for all but three event kinds  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/AppEnvironment.swift:683-712; Storage/ResearchStore.swift:165-180`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Every ResearchEvent is persisted via appendEvent, but EventEnvelope only encodes structured `data` for planEmitted, draftReady, and citationAdded; all other kinds (toolResult, sourceFetched, workerCompleted, reflectionEmitted, error, etc.) fall into the default branch with data=nil — only a kind string and timestamp survive. ResearchEvent itself is not Codable (it has Duration and Reflector.Critique associated values), which is why the envelope hand-rolls encoding, but the result is that the persisted event log cannot reconstruct most of a run.  
  - **Impact:** The StoredEvent timeline is largely un-replayable: a completed session cannot be reconstructed from its events (tool outcomes, fetched-source details, reflection critiques, error details are all dropped). Limits any future 'resume/replay/audit' capability and debugging of past runs.  
  - **Fix:** Either give the domain types full Codable conformance (encode Duration as a TimeInterval, make Reflector.Critique Codable) and persist the whole event, or expand EventEnvelope to cover the high-value kinds (toolResult, sourceFetched, workerCompleted, reflectionEmitted, error). Storing the already-computed summary plus a typed payload for each would make the event log auditable.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. EventEnvelope.init (AppEnvironment.swift:688-704) only encodes structured `data` for planEmitted, draftReady, citationAdded; the default branch (line 700-702) sets data=nil, persisting only a kind string + timestamp. appendEvent persists every event (AppEnvironment.swift:505 -> ResearchStore.swift:165-180). ResearchEvent is indeed not Codable (Duration/Reflector.Critique associated values), explaining the hand-rolled envelope. Net result: toolResult/sourceFetched/workerCompleted/reflectionEmitted/error payloads are lost. Accurate improvement; P3 fine.  
- [ ] **`keychain-accessible-attr-update-path-7`** — Keychain item not pinned against iCloud sync / data-protection keychain  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Shared/KeychainStore.swift:34-49`  
  - **Category:** security · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Items are created as kSecClassGenericPassword without kSecAttrSynchronizable=false, without kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain, and without an access-control object. kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlock is applied on both add and update paths (good), but because synchronizable is unpinned, on a login keychain configured for iCloud Keychain these provider API keys could sync off-device. Accessibility is also broader than strictly needed for interactive-use keys.  
  - **Impact:** Potential unintended iCloud-Keychain propagation of provider API keys, and broader-than-necessary in-process accessibility. Low likelihood on a sandboxed macOS app but currently unpinned and undocumented.  
  - **Fix:** Explicitly set kSecAttrSynchronizable: false in the base query, and consider kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain: true. Keep kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlock or tighten to WhenUnlocked since these are interactive-use keys.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. set() query/attributes (KeychainStore.swift:34-49) use kSecClassGenericPassword with no kSecAttrSynchronizable, no kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain, no access-control object. kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlock is applied via `attributes` on both update (SecItemUpdate) and add (add.merge(attributes), line 46) paths. The sync claim is somewhat conservative for the default macOS file/login keychain (synchronizable defaults to false unless set true), but pinning these attributes explicitly and tightening accessibility is a legitimate hardening item. P3 as rated is correct.  

#### Core SwiftUI Interface

- [x] **`iface-dropload-mainactor-hop-13`** — Document drop loadObject completion hops to MainActor only to call async ingest  
  - **Where:** `DocumentUploadView.swift:312-325`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** handleDrop returns consumedAny based on canLoadObject before the async loadObject completion runs, so the boolean is a best-effort guess rather than a real result. Each provider's completion dispatches Task { @MainActor in await kb.ingestFile(...) }, serializing nothing and pinning ingest setup to the main actor; multiple dropped files ingest with no ordering/progress feedback and errors inside the closure are swallowed (the `_` on loadObject return).  
  - **Impact:** Dropping several files gives no progress and silently ignores load failures; the return value reported to the drop system may not reflect actual success. Low severity but degrades the KB ingestion UX.  
  - **Fix:** Collect the loaded URLs and ingest them through a single awaited loop with per-file progress/error surfacing (kb.lastError), and base the onDrop return on whether any fileURL provider was actually present. Consider a TaskGroup for parallel ingest with a visible counter.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. handleDrop (DocumentUploadView.swift:312-325) sets consumedAny from provider.canLoadObject(ofClass: URL.self) (315-316) and returns it (324) BEFORE the async loadObject completion (317-322) runs, so the Bool is a best-effort guess, not a real load result. Each completion does Task { @MainActor in await kb.ingestFile(at: url) } (319-321), pinning ingest setup to the main actor with no ordering/progress feedback across multiple dropped files. Errors are swallowed: loadObject's return is discarded (`_ =`, line 317) and its error parameter is ignored (closure signature `{ url, _ in`, line 317) — only a non-nil url is handled (318). All claims accurate; P3 improvement.  
- [x] **`iface-html-export-not-markdown-12`** — HTML/Markdown export escapes raw markdown into <pre>, losing all formatting  
  - **Where:** `SessionExporter.swift:80-123 (html), 58-76 (markdown)`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The HTML export wraps turn.markdown in <pre class="md"> after escapeHTML, so the exported HTML shows raw markdown source (#, [N], **bold**) as preformatted text rather than rendered HTML — citations and headings are not real links/structure. The inline [N] citation markers in the body never resolve to the numbered Source archive (no anchors). The filename slug regex strips non-ascii, so a non-English query collapses to 'research.md'.  
  - **Impact:** Exported HTML/PDF is visually poor (monospaced raw markdown), and citation markers are dead in the export. Reduces the usefulness of the otherwise-complete export feature; non-Latin queries all get the same generic filename.  
  - **Fix:** Render markdown to HTML (reuse a markdown→HTML pass) and emit anchor links from [N] markers to the Source archive list; in markdown export keep [N] but ensure the Sources numbering matches the synthesizer order. For filenames, transliterate or fall back to a timestamp instead of dropping all non-ascii.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed on all three sub-claims. html() wraps turn.markdown in <pre class="md">escapeHTML(...)</pre> (SessionExporter.swift:85-86), so exported HTML shows raw markdown source (#, [N], **bold**) as preformatted text, not rendered HTML — headings/bold/links are not real structure. Inline [N] body markers never resolve to the numbered Source archive: the <ol> source items (89-96) carry no id/anchor and the body [N] are plain text, so there are no in-page links. filename() lowercases then strips via [^a-z0-9]+ regex (200-201), so a fully non-ascii (e.g. Chinese) query collapses to empty -> 'research.<ext>' (line 204). All accurate; P3 improvement.  
- [x] **`iface-inspector-autoreveal-once-11`** — Inspector auto-reveal/visibility logic can desync from session lifecycle  
  - **Where:** `MainScene.swift:51-58,79-87,102-107`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** showInspector is local @State auto-set to true on first non-idle live status (onChange line 102-107) but never reset when the run ends or the user starts New Research / picks another session. The detail pane also gates the inspector on `env.live != nil || env.selectedSessionID != nil`; after ⌘N (both niled) the toggle disappears but showInspector stays true, so re-entering a session can flash the inspector open unexpectedly. The toolbar toggle and the auto-reveal can also fight each other.  
  - **Impact:** Minor UX inconsistency: inspector state leaks across sessions and doesn't reflect the current context; users may see it open/closed contrary to expectation after New Research.  
  - **Fix:** Reset showInspector in the New Research / newSession handlers, and treat auto-reveal as a one-time per-session hint (track a `didAutoRevealForSession` keyed on sessionID) rather than firing on every status change.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. showInspector is local @State (MainScene.swift:18), auto-set true on first non-idle live status via onChange (104-105), never reset to false anywhere (grep shows only :18 declaration, :81 toolbar Toggle binding, :105 the one true-assignment). The detail pane gates on showInspector && (env.live != nil || env.selectedSessionID != nil) (line 51); after ⌘N nils both (67-70) the panel hides but showInspector stays true, so selecting another session re-satisfies the gate and the inspector flashes open unexpectedly. The auto-reveal at 104-105 fires only `if !showInspector`, so after the user manually closes it via the toolbar Toggle, the next status change re-opens it — the toggle and auto-reveal do fight. P3 UX improvement, correctly scoped.  
- [x] **`iface-sidebar-filter-onbody-10`** — Sidebar search filter does full-text scan over all turns/sources inside body  
  - **Where:** `SessionSidebar.swift:67-77`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `filtered` lowercases the needle and scans every session's query, titleSummary, all turns' markdown, and all sources' title+snippet on each computed-property access (i.e., each body re-eval while searching). lowercased() is allocated repeatedly and full draft markdown is walked. With many sessions and long drafts this is a notable per-keystroke cost on the main thread.  
  - **Impact:** Typing in the sidebar search becomes laggy as the session corpus grows, since every keystroke re-walks all stored markdown/sources. Faulting in turns/sources relationships per session also hits SwiftData lazily.  
  - **Fix:** Debounce the search term, precompute a lowercased searchable blob per session (or use a SwiftData #Predicate on the @Query to filter at the store level), and avoid touching full turn.markdown for every session on every keystroke.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. SessionSidebar.filtered (lines 67-77) is a computed property recomputed on each body access while searching: it lowercases the needle once (line 69) but then calls .lowercased() repeatedly inside the filter for every session.query, titleSummary, every turn.markdown (line 73 loops all turns), and every source.title/snippet (line 74 loops all sources) — allocating new lowercased strings each comparison and walking full draft markdown. With many sessions and long drafts this is a per-keystroke main-thread cost. Accurately a P3 (bounded by session count; only runs while the search field is non-empty).  

#### Settings + Forecast UI

- [ ] **`budget-preset-desync-no-custom-state`** — Editing a budget/iteration cap silently clears the preset highlight with no 'Custom' indication; maxWallClock has no UI
