Additionally, AgentBudget.maxWallClock (a documented hard cap that actually kills runs, AgentBudget.swift:101-107) has no control in BudgetTab at all, so users cannot adjust the wall-clock limit that the comment notes was previously killing local-Ollama runs mid-synthesis.  
  - **Impact:** Minor confusion (no visible selection after a manual tweak) and a missing capability: the wall-clock cap — the one most likely to abort long local runs — is not user-editable despite being surfaced as a configurable budget concept.  
  - **Fix:** Add an explicit 'Custom' capsule that highlights when budget matches no preset, and add a wall-clock Stepper/picker (e.g. 1–60 min) to the Caps group so all five AgentBudget fields are adjustable.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. BudgetTab preset capsules use `isOn: env.configuration.budget == .fast/.standard/.thorough` (SettingsSheet.swift:953-961), full-struct equality, so nudging any Stepper de-highlights all three with no 'Custom' state. AgentBudget.maxWallClock exists (AgentBudget.swift:13) and is actively enforced (checkWallClock throws budgetExceeded, lines 101-107), with a comment that the previous caps killed local-Ollama runs mid-synthesis (lines 27-31), yet BudgetTab's Caps group (lines 965-995) exposes only maxTokens/maxWorkers/maxSourcesPerWorker/maxToolCallsPerWorker — no maxWallClock control. Both sub-claims hold. P3 (improvement) appropriate.  
- [ ] **`forecast-sidebar-selection-binding-noop-setter`** — Forecast sidebar List selection binding has a no-op setter, so native list selection never drives navigation  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/Forecast/ForecastSidebar.swift:10-13, :37-38`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The sidebar List is given a selection Binding whose setter is `set: { _ in }` (lines 10-13) and rows are opened via a separate `.onTapGesture { env.openForecast(record:) }` (line 38). The List's built-in selection (keyboard arrow navigation, accessibility selection, click-to-select highlight) is therefore inert — the visual selection comes only from passing the get value, and any selection change made by the system is discarded. Tap handling is bolted on with contentShape+onTapGesture, which competes with List's own row hit-testing on macOS.  
  - **Impact:** Keyboard navigation and accessibility (VoiceOver) cannot move between forecasts; the row highlight and the actual open action can disagree. Relying on onTapGesture over List rows is fragile on macOS where the List manages selection itself.  
  - **Fix:** Use the selection binding properly: set it to the tapped pipelineID and react in onChange to call openForecast, or use List(selection:) with a value-based selection and NavigationLink/selection-driven detail rather than onTapGesture overlays.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. ForecastSidebar.body gives `List(selection:)` a Binding with `get: { env.forecast?.pipelineID }, set: { _ in }` (lines 10-13), so any system-driven selection change is discarded — native keyboard arrow navigation / accessibility selection is inert. Rows open via a bolted-on `.contentShape(Rectangle()).onTapGesture { env.openForecast(record:) }` (lines 37-38), which competes with List's own row hit-testing/selection on macOS. Robustness concern is real. P3 appropriate.  
- [ ] **`graph-force-sim-reseed-on-poll`** — KnowledgeGraphView re-seeds the force simulation every 2s while the graph stage runs, causing layout thrash  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/Forecast/KnowledgeGraphView.swift:61-86; Swift Deep Research/Forecast/ForecastRun.swift:286-290`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** ForecastRun.sideFetches reassigns `run.graph = next` (a brand-new ForecastGraph value) on every 2s poll while `stageStatus(.graph) == .running` (ForecastRun.swift:286-289). KnowledgeGraphView takes `graph` by value and rebuilds `Series(graph.nodes)`/`Series(graph.edges)` in its body each time, while `graphStates` (ForceDirectedGraphState) is plain @State tied to the stable view identity, so it is NOT reset. Grape diffs the node set and emits newly-arrived nodes via `emittingNewNodesWithStates: { _ in KineticState(position: .zero) }` (line 84-86) — every incremental Zep entity spawns at the origin, so the layout visibly jumps and re-converges every 2 seconds during the (often minutes-long) graph stage. The `nodeByID` and `degree` computed properties (lines 20-27) also fully recompute on each body pass.  
  - **Impact:** During the knowledge-graph stage the graph animates chaotically (nodes flying from center) every poll, and the force sim never settles — degraded UX and continuous CPU on the main thread for the duration of graph extraction.  
  - **Fix:** Throttle graph updates during the running stage (e.g. only reassign run.graph when node/edge count changed by a meaningful delta, or refresh on a slower cadence like every ~10s), and/or pause `graphStates.isRunning` re-kicks. Cache `nodeByID`/`degree` as @State recomputed only when graph.nodes/edges identity changes rather than per body eval.  
  - **Verify:** Mostly real but the title overstates it. ForecastRun.sideFetches reassigns `graph = next` every 2s while stageStatus(.graph)==.running (ForecastRun.swift:286-289), poll interval is 2s (line 237). KnowledgeGraphView takes `graph` by value and rebuilds Series in body; `@State graphStates` is NOT reset (lines 12-15). However Grape diffs the node set: existing nodes retain their KineticState; only NEWLY-arrived nodes are emitted, and those spawn at `.zero` via `emittingNewNodesWithStates: { _ in KineticState(position: .zero) }` (lines 84-86). So it is NOT a full force-sim re-seed/whole-layout thrash each 2s — only incremental Zep entities pop in at the origin and re-settle. Visible-jump concern is real but bounded (graph capped ~140 nodes); the recompute of nodeByID/degree per body is real but cheap at this scale. Severity adjusted to P3.  
- [ ] **`graph-tap-hit-test-degree-recompute`** — Knowledge graph node symbol size and palette recompute degree/type lookups on every render pass  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/Forecast/KnowledgeGraphView.swift:62-83, :150-156, :173-206`  
  - **Category:** optimization · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** graphCanvas captures `let deg = degree` and `let types = graph.entityTypes` once per body (good), but `degree` itself (lines 23-27) rebuilds a full [String:Int] over all edges each body invocation, and GraphPalette.color(for:in:) does an O(types) firstIndex lookup for every node, every legend row, and every inspector connection. With the 2s graph refresh re-running body, these recompute continuously. The node inspector additionally filters all edges (`graph.edges.filter`) on every render while shown (line 174).  
  - **Impact:** For the ~140-node/edge cap this is bounded, but combined with the 2s re-render cadence it is steady avoidable main-thread work; the per-node linear color lookup is O(nodes×types) each pass.  
  - **Fix:** Precompute degree and a `[type: Color]` map once when graph changes (store in @State updated via onChange(of: graph)), and precompute an adjacency map for the inspector instead of filtering all edges per render.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. graphCanvas captures `let deg = degree` and `let types = graph.entityTypes` once per body (lines 62-63, good), but the `degree` computed property rebuilds a full [String:Int] over all edges on each access (lines 23-27), and GraphPalette.color(for:in:) does `types.firstIndex(of:)` — O(types) — per call (ForecastModels.swift:154-156), invoked per node symbol, per legend row (KnowledgeGraphView:153), and per inspector connection (178/189). nodeInspector additionally runs `graph.edges.filter` on every render while shown (line 174). With the 2s graph refresh re-running body during the graph stage, these recompute continuously. All cited behaviors accurate; impact bounded by ~140-node cap so P3 (optimization) is right.  
- [x] **`host-validation-allows-malformed-urls`** — LM Studio / custom / forecast host commits accept malformed URLs (scheme-only checks, no host requirement)  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/SettingsSheet.swift:760-765 (commitLMStudioHost), :767-774 (commitCustomHost), :1538-1545 (ForecastTab commitHost)`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** commitLMStudioHost accepts any URL whose `scheme != nil` (line 762) — so 'http:' or 'mailto:foo' passes and becomes the LM Studio host. commitCustomHost is the same. By contrast KnowledgeTab.commitHost (line 1206-1219) and commitQwenHost (line 804-810) correctly require scheme==http/https AND url.host != nil. The validation is inconsistent: the two cards that feed an OpenAI-compatible client are the laxest. There is also no user feedback when a host string is rejected for being invalid — the field silently keeps the old value.  
  - **Impact:** A typo like 'localhost:1234' (no scheme) is silently ignored (keeps old host) while 'http:1234' may be accepted as a garbage URL, later producing an opaque network failure during discovery or a forecast run rather than an actionable 'invalid host' message.  
  - **Fix:** Apply the KnowledgeTab/Qwen validation (require http/https scheme and non-nil host) uniformly to commitLMStudioHost, commitCustomHost, and ForecastTab.commitHost, and surface a visible 'Invalid host' status when rejected (as KnowledgeTab already does).  
  - **Verify:** Partially correct but one cited location is wrong. commitLMStudioHost (lines 760-765) and commitCustomHost (767-774) only check `url.scheme != nil`, so 'http:' or 'mailto:foo' passes — lax, as claimed. KnowledgeTab.commitHost (1206-1218) and commitQwenHost (804-810) correctly require http/https AND url.host != nil — confirmed stricter. HOWEVER the finding also cites ForecastTab commitHost at :1538-1545 as needing fixing/being part of the problem, but that function ALREADY enforces `scheme==http/https && url.host != nil` (lines 1540-1541) — it is strict, not lax. And LM Studio/custom silently keep the old value with no feedback (true). So the core inconsistency (2 lax OpenAI-compat host commits) is real, but the ForecastTab citation is a misread. Net: real but narrower than stated; P3 appropriate.  
- [ ] **`providers-discovery-state-lost-on-close`** — Per-provider discovery results live in transient @State and are discarded when the sheet closes  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/SettingsSheet.swift:278-288 (ProvidersTab @State), :669-679 (liveModels)`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** discovered, discoveryStates, ollamaModels, and the host strings are all @State local to ProvidersTab. Closing and reopening Settings destroys them, so the curated static model list (provider.availableModels) is shown again until the user re-runs 'Test key & fetch' for each provider. The fetched live catalogue is never cached. The Ollama task also only refetches on host change or provider change, not on each open of a still-empty list.  
  - **Impact:** Users who discover their live models (e.g. a fresh LM Studio or custom-endpoint catalogue) lose that list every time they reopen Settings and must re-test each provider, which costs a network round-trip and a key re-validation each time.  
  - **Fix:** Cache discovered model lists (and last-discovery timestamps) in AppEnvironment or UserDefaults keyed by provider so they survive sheet close/open, with a manual refresh to re-fetch. This also lets the engine reuse the live catalogue.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. ProvidersTab declares `discovered`, `discoveryStates`, `ollamaModels`, `lmStudioHostString`, `customHostString`, `qwenHostString` all as `@State` local to the tab (SettingsSheet.swift:278-287). None are persisted; closing the sheet destroys them. liveModels(for:) falls back to provider.availableModels (curated static list) when `discovered[provider]` is empty (lines 669-678), so the fetched catalogue reverts after reopen until re-fetched. The Ollama task keys on `.task(id: env.configuration.ollamaHost)` + provider-change onChange (lines 330-346), not on each open of an empty list. Improvement is genuine. P3 appropriate.  
- [x] **`research-console-lazyvstack-offset-id`** — Research/agent log views key list rows on enumerated offset, breaking scroll stability and diffing  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Interface/Forecast/ResearchConsoleView.swift:37 (id: \.offset); Swift Deep Research/Interface/Forecast/ForecastOnboardingView.swift:216 (id: \.offset)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** ResearchConsoleView streams DeerFlow lines into a LazyVStack with `ForEach(Array(run.researchLines.enumerated()), id: \.offset)`. researchLines is fully replaced on each poll (ForecastRun.swift:273-275 sets `researchLines = p.lines` with the last 200 lines). When the window slides, identical content shifts to new offsets, so SwiftUI treats every row as changed and rebuilds the entire visible list each 2s tick instead of diffing appends. The onboarding console (line 216) has the same pattern. ResearchConsoleView also re-creates the whole Text array per update.  
  - **Impact:** Each 2s research poll re-renders the entire console body and can disrupt scroll position / auto-scroll-to-bottom because row identity is unstable, on top of wasted layout work for a 200-line monospaced list.  
  - **Fix:** Append lines incrementally and key rows on stable content-derived identity (e.g. a monotonically increasing line sequence number from the backend), or at minimum hash the line content into the id so unchanged lines keep identity across the sliding window.  
  - **Verify:** Confirmed. ResearchConsoleView uses `ForEach(Array(run.researchLines.enumerated()), id: \.offset)` with `.id(idx)` (ResearchConsoleView.swift:37-43). ForecastRun.sideFetches sets `researchLines = p.lines` (last 200) on each 2s poll (ForecastRun.swift:271-274), fully replacing the array. When the 200-line window slides, identical content shifts to new offsets so SwiftUI treats rows as changed rather than diffing appends. ForecastOnboardingView.console has the identical pattern at line 216 (`id: \.offset`). Both citations accurate. Magnitude is bounded by the 200-line cap, so P3 (perf opportunity) is right.

#### Cross-cutting: Architecture, Build Health & Dead Code

- [ ] **`discovered-models-lost-1`** — Live-discovered model lists are thrown away when the Settings sheet closes  
  - **Where:** `Interface/SettingsSheet.swift:282 (@State discovered), :535-558 (discoverModels/modelSuggestions)`  
  - **Category:** improvement · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `ProviderRegistry.ProviderID.availableModels` is a hardcoded static catalogue (ProviderRegistry.swift:46-93). The 'Test & fetch models' flow fetches the real live catalogue via ModelDiscovery (SettingsSheet.swift:550) but stores it only in view-local `@State private var discovered` (line 282). When the sheet closes, the discovered list is discarded; reopening shows the stale hardcoded list again until the user re-tests. There is no persisted source of truth that merges discovered models with the static defaults.  
  - **Impact:** Users with newer/custom models must re-run discovery on every settings visit; the hardcoded lists (e.g. anthropic snapshots, openai 'gpt-5.5') drift from reality and the freshly-discovered truth doesn't stick. Minor UX friction and stale model menus.  
  - **Fix:** Persist discovered model lists per provider (UserDefaults keyed by providerID, with a fetch timestamp) and have `modelSuggestions` merge persisted-discovered ∪ static defaults. Optionally expire after N days. This makes discovery the durable source of truth without removing the offline fallback list.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. `discovered` is @State (SettingsSheet.swift:282, view-local), populated only inside discoverModels() (:552) from ModelDiscovery.fetchModels (:550). modelSuggestions() (:535-541) merges provider.availableModels (hardcoded static, ProviderRegistry.swift:46-93) with the view-local discovered dict. There is no persisted store of discovered models — when the sheet closes the @State is discarded, so reopening shows the static list until re-tested. (Note: commitDiscoveredDefault at :563 does persist the first discovered model as the selected model for a role, but the full discovered list itself is not persisted.) P3 improvement is appropriate.  
- [ ] **`forecast-deerflow-confusion-1`** — Three overlapping 'DeerFlow' concepts (in-app DeerFlowEngine, backend DeerFlow, forecast role) blur the architecture  
  - **Where:** `Engine/DeerFlowEngine.swift:25 (in-process), LLM/ModelProviderManager.swift:131-244 (deerflowModel env seeding for backend), EngineConfiguration.swift:11 (ResearchFlow.deerflow)`  
  - **Category:** architecture · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The word 'DeerFlow' names three distinct things with no unifying doc: (1) the in-process Swift DeerFlowEngine research flow selected via ResearchFlow.deerflow; (2) the external MiroFish backend's DeerFlow subprocess, configured by ModelProviderManager via DEERFLOW_MODEL/deerflowModel env seeding (ModelProviderManager.swift:133-138,236); and (3) the forecast ModelRole that drives that backend. The native research flow uses the in-process engines + ProviderRegistry; the forecast flow bypasses all of that and reconfigures a Python backend over HTTP/.env. A reader cannot tell from EngineConfiguration whether selecting 'deerflow' runs in-process or hits the backend — it's the former, while the visually-similar forecast path is the latter.  
  - **Impact:** High cognitive load and real risk of mis-wiring: e.g. someone could assume forecast obeys EngineConfiguration's orchestrator/worker/synthesis providers, when it actually only obeys forecastAssignment. The deerflowModel mapping is also lossy (every non-native provider maps to deerflowModel 'claude' at ModelProviderManager.swift:157-198), which may silently route the backend to the wrong model family.  
  - **Fix:** Rename to disambiguate (e.g. in-process flow → 'PlanExecuteEngine', backend → 'MiroFishForecast') and add a short architecture note in handoff.md/README mapping the three flows to their config sources. Audit the deerflowModel='claude' fallback (ModelProviderManager.swift:157,167,187,192,198) — verify the backend actually honors LLM_PROVIDER/LLM_MODEL_NAME over DEERFLOW_MODEL for OpenAI-compatible providers, else the backend may ignore the user's chosen model.  
  - **Verify:** Verified the three distinct 'DeerFlow' concepts: (1) in-process Swift DeerFlowEngine selected via ResearchFlow.deerflow (EngineConfiguration.swift:11-12 enum case .deerflow; DeerFlowEngine.swift:25 struct); (2) the external MiroFish backend's DeerFlow subprocess, configured by ModelProviderManager via DEERFLOW_MODEL / deerflowModel env seeding (ModelProviderManager.swift:133-138 payload field, :235 env var, per-provider mapping :157-198); (3) the forecast ModelRole driving that backend (ModelRole.forecast). EngineConfiguration's doc comment doesn't disambiguate that selecting 'deerflow' runs in-process while the visually-similar forecast path hits the Python backend over HTTP/.env. Real naming/conceptual ambiguity. P3 (documentation/clarity improvement) is appropriate.  
- [x] **`native-engine-dedup-gap-1`** — Native engine dedups follow-up subtasks by question text but never threads accumulated sources into later-round workers  
  - **Where:** `Engine/ResearchEngine.swift:172-205 (round loop, runWorkers call) vs DeerFlowEngine.swift:155,168 (observationDigest extraContext)`  
  - **Category:** correctness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** DeerFlow threads prior observations into each sequential step via `extraContext` (DeerFlowEngine.swift:155 observationDigest → worker.run(extraContext:)), so later steps build on earlier findings. The native engine's multi-round loop (ResearchEngine.swift:193-205) re-runs `runWorkers` for new subtasks but passes no extraContext — every follow-up worker starts cold with no memory of what rounds 1..N-1 already found, even though `accumulatedOutputs` is available right there. The only cross-round guard is a lowercased question-string dedup (:172-173), which misses semantically-duplicate-but-differently-worded subtasks.  
  - **Impact:** Thorough mode (6 rounds) re-researches overlapping ground and can produce redundant or contradictory findings across rounds because later workers can't see earlier evidence; wastes budget/tokens that the deepening rounds are specifically meant to spend productively.  
  - **Fix:** Pass an observation digest of `accumulatedOutputs` (reuse DeerFlow's observationDigest pattern) as `extraContext` into the native engine's later-round `runWorkers`, so deepening/gap-filling workers build on prior rounds rather than restart. Tighten dedup beyond exact lowercased string match if needed.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. WorkerAgent.run accepts extraContext (WorkerAgent.swift:49-51, default ""; injected into prompt at :98-100 as 'Findings from earlier steps'). DeerFlowEngine threads observationDigest into each step via worker.run(extraContext: observations) (DeerFlowEngine.swift:155,170). But ResearchEngine.runWorkers (242-289) has no extraContext parameter and its two call sites (108-119 round 1; 193-204 follow-up rounds) forward none, so every follow-up worker starts cold despite accumulatedOutputs being in scope. The only cross-round dedup is `asked = Set(plan.subtasks.map { $0.question.lowercased() })` filtered against newSubtasks (:172-173) — a literal string match that misses semantically-duplicate-but-reworded subtasks. P3 correctness is appropriate (quality gap, not a crash).  
- [ ] **`untracked-backend-trees-1`** — Large untracked vendored backend trees (deer-flow, MiroFish, src, frontend, backend) sit unmanaged in the repo root  
  - **Where:** `repo root: deer-flow/, MiroFish-0.1.2/, src/, frontend/, backend/, deerflow_bridge/, dist/, tests/, public/, scripts/ (all untracked, none gitignored)`  
  - **Category:** architecture · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** git status shows ~10 untracked top-level directories (deer-flow 28K, MiroFish-0.1.2 48K, src 32K, deerflow_bridge 20K, plus backend/frontend/dist/tests/public/scripts) that are neither committed nor gitignored. These are the Python/JS MiroFish-DeerFlow backend the Forecast workspace talks to (per ModelProviderManager/MiroFishClient), but their relationship to the Swift app is undocumented in version control and they pollute `git status`. The Swift app references them only at runtime via forecastConfig.repoRoot (AppEnvironment.syncMiroFishEnv at :259-265), so their on-disk location is load-bearing yet unmanaged.  
  - **Impact:** Contributors can't tell which backend revision the app targets; `git status` noise hides real changes; CI/clean-checkout machines won't have the backend, so Forecast silently fails. The dependency boundary between the Swift app and the external backend is implicit.  
  - **Fix:** Decide the boundary explicitly: either add these as a git submodule / pinned dependency with a documented expected layout, or gitignore them and document the required backend version + clone path in the README and ForecastConfiguration. Don't leave load-bearing trees untracked-and-unignored.  
  - **Verify:** Verified. `git status --porcelain | grep '^??'` shows MiroFish-0.1.2/, backend/, deer-flow/, deerflow_bridge/, dist/, frontend/, public/, scripts/, src/, tests/ all untracked. `git check-ignore` returns NOT ignored for every one of these 10 dirs. They are the Python/JS MiroFish-DeerFlow backend the Forecast workspace drives, referenced at runtime via forecastConfig.repoRoot (AppEnvironment.syncMiroFishEnv :259-265, MiroFishSupervisor). Their on-disk location is load-bearing yet neither committed nor gitignored, polluting git status. P3 architecture is appropriate.  

#### Cross-cutting: Concurrency & Data Races

- [ ] **`concurrency-emit-closure-reentrancy-ordering`** — emit closure yields from multiple worker actors concurrently; event ordering across workers is non-deterministic  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Engine/ResearchEngine.swift:59-61 (emit) consumed by Swift Deep Research/Engine/WorkerAgent.swift:52,135,151,181,207 across parallel workers`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The single `@Sendable emit` closure (a thin `continuation.yield`) is shared across all parallel workers in the TaskGroup and the synthesis loop. `AsyncThrowingStream.Continuation.yield` is itself thread-safe, so there's no data race. However, because N workers emit concurrently, interleaved events (`toolInvoked`/`toolResult`/`reasoningDelta`) from different workers arrive in non-deterministic order. The UI handles this correctly by keying on `WorkerID` (`workers[id]?...`), so cross-worker interleaving is tolerated. The only fragile spot is `toolResult` matching `toolHistory.lastIndex(where: { $0.invocation.id == inv.id })` — if a worker reused a tool-call id it could mismatch, but ids are provider-minted/unique. Noted to confirm the parallel emit path was audited and found safe.  
  - **Impact:** No data race or corruption today; event interleaving is handled by per-WorkerID keying in LiveSession.ingest. Risk is purely future-fragility if event handling ever assumes global ordering.  
  - **Fix:** No change required. If stricter ordering is ever needed, route emits through a serializing actor; not warranted now.  
  - **Verify:** The single @Sendable emit (ResearchEngine.swift:59-61, thin continuation.yield) is shared across the TaskGroup workers (withThrowingTaskGroup line 258, group.addTask line 272) and the synthesis loop. AsyncThrowingStream.Continuation.yield is thread-safe, so concurrent emits from N workers have no data race — only non-deterministic ordering. The UI tolerates interleaving by keying on WorkerID (ingest cases `workers[id]?...` AppEnvironment 528-567). toolResult matches `toolHistory.lastIndex(where: { $0.invocation.id == inv.id })` (lines 534-535) within a single worker's history, keyed by provider-minted unique tool-call id. Safe as the audit concludes. P3 confirm-only.  
- [ ] **`concurrency-mirofish-syncenv-blocking-io-on-actor`** — MiroFishSupervisor.syncEnv does synchronous file I/O on the actor's executor  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:136-164 (syncEnv) and :328-346 (venvIsPython312 with process.waitUntilExit)`  
  - **Category:** bottleneck · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `syncEnv` is a synchronous actor method that reads and writes `.env` via `String(contentsOf:)` / `output.write(to:)` directly on the actor. `venvIsPython312` runs a Process and calls `process.waitUntilExit()` — a *blocking* call — on the actor's executor (line 343), holding the actor for the duration of `python --version`. Unlike the other process paths in this file (which use async `Task.sleep` polling loops), `venvIsPython312` blocks the cooperative thread synchronously while the subprocess runs.  
  - **Impact:** `waitUntilExit()` blocks the MiroFishSupervisor actor (and thus serializes any concurrent `ensureRunning`/`checkHealth` callers) for the ~100-300ms a python interpreter takes to start and print its version. Small, but it's a blocking call inside an actor that the rest of the file deliberately avoids. syncEnv's file I/O is fast but also blocks the actor.  
  - **Fix:** Replace `process.waitUntilExit()` in venvIsPython312 with the same async pattern used by `runRepairStep` (poll `process.isRunning` with `Task.sleep`), or route it through the existing `runProcess`-style continuation helper. Move syncEnv's file I/O to a detached task if it ever grows.  
  - **Verify:** MiroFishSupervisor is `public actor` (line 24). syncEnv (lines 137-164) is an actor-isolated (not nonisolated) synchronous method that reads via `String(contentsOf:)` (line 141) and writes via `output.write(to:)` (line 158) directly on the actor's executor. venvIsPython312 (lines 328-346, actor-isolated) runs a Process and calls the blocking `process.waitUntilExit()` at line 343, holding the cooperative thread for the duration of `python --version` (called from resolveInterpreter at line 309). Both are cold paths (launch / onboarding via ForecastOnboarding:191,254 and AppEnvironment:264), short duration, so impact is low — P3 accurate.  
- [ ] **`concurrency-sidecar-ensuretask-clear-race`** — ensureTask is cleared after await without re-entrancy check; a fast second caller can observe a stale completed task  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/SidecarSupervisor.swift:74-87 (ensureRunning) and Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:73-82 (ensureRunning)`  
  - **Category:** concurrency · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Both supervisors coalesce concurrent launches with `if let ensureTask { return await ensureTask.value }`, then after `await task.value` they do `ensureTask = nil`. Because the actor is suspended during `await task.value`, multiple callers correctly join the same task. The clear-to-nil is fine for correctness (the comment about a single synchronous step is accurate for the check-and-assign). The minor wrinkle: after the first caller resumes and sets `ensureTask = nil`, a later caller starts a fresh launch even if the previous one just succeeded — but the fast-path `probeHealth` at the top catches the now-running sidecar, so a duplicate launch is avoided. This is correct, noted only to document the audit verified the coalescing is race-free.  
  - **Impact:** No data race; coalescing works as designed. Worst case is a redundant `probeHealth` HTTP call, not a duplicate process. Documented for completeness of the concurrency audit.  
  - **Fix:** No change required. The check-probe-then-coalesce ordering is correct under actor isolation.  
  - **Verify:** SidecarSupervisor.ensureRunning (74-87) and MiroFishSupervisor.ensureRunning (74-82) both do fast-path probeHealth, then `if let ensureTask { return await ensureTask.value }` (sidecar 81 / mirofish 76), assign task, `await task.value`, then `ensureTask = nil`. The check-and-assign has no await between them (single synchronous actor step) so two callers cannot both launch — the second joins. After the first resumer nils the task, a later caller's fast-path probeHealth (sidecar 77 / mirofish 75) catches the now-running sidecar, avoiding a duplicate launch (performEnsureRunning also re-probes: sidecar 92, mirofish 85). The audit's race-free conclusion is correct. P3 confirm-only.  
- [ ] **`concurrency-sourcecache-inflight-defer-clear`** — SourceCache clears inFlight via defer before storing result; a failed fetch correctly drops the in-flight slot  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Shared/SourceCache.swift:32-45 (fetch)`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** `fetch` sets `inFlight[key] = task`, uses `defer { inFlight[key] = nil }`, then `await task.value`. Under actor isolation this is correct: concurrent callers awaiting the same key get `task.value` (dedup works), and on throw the defer clears the slot so a later retry isn't permanently wedged on a failed task. One subtlety: the result is `store`d only on success, so a transient fetch failure isn't negatively cached — correct behavior. The audit confirms no double-fetch and no stale-task wedge.  
  - **Impact:** No defect — in-flight dedup and failure recovery are correct under actor isolation. Documented to confirm the shared-across-workers cache actor was audited.  
  - **Fix:** No change required.  
  - **Verify:** SourceCache.swift:32-45 (actor). fetch sets `inFlight[key] = task` (40), `defer { inFlight[key] = nil }` (41), `await task.value` (42), then `store` only on success (43). Concurrent same-key callers hit `inFlight[key]` and `await task.value` (36-37) so the extractor runs once. On throw, the defer clears the slot (no permanent wedge on a failed task) and nothing is negatively cached (store at 43 is past the throwing await). Under actor isolation the set-then-defer-then-await sequence is correct. Audit's no-double-fetch / no-stale-wedge conclusion holds. P3 confirm-only.  
- [ ] **`concurrency-unchecked-sendable-justification`** — Four @unchecked Sendable helpers are correctly justified (NSLock-guarded process-callback bridges)  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Knowledge/SidecarSupervisor.swift:531 (ResumeOnce), :546 (TaskHolder), :555 (OutputBuffer); Swift Deep Research/Forecast/MiroFishSupervisor.swift:245 (LineBuffer)`  
  - **Category:** concurrency · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** high · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** These are the only four `@unchecked Sendable` sites in the app tree. Each exists to bridge `Process` pipe `readabilityHandler` / `terminationHandler` closures (which fire on arbitrary queues) into the actor world. All mutable state in each is guarded by a private `NSLock` (ResumeOnce.done, TaskHolder.task, OutputBuffer.storage, LineBuffer.pending), and each has a doc comment explaining the race it guards. This is the correct pattern for `@unchecked Sendable` and the justification holds — flagged here only to confirm the audit reviewed each and found no unguarded access.  
  - **Impact:** No defect. Confirms the four flagged sites are sound and do not need conversion to actors (an actor can't be called from a synchronous Process callback without hopping, which is exactly what these avoid).  
  - **Fix:** No change required. Optionally migrate to `Mutex<T>` (Synchronization framework, macOS 15+) for a slightly cleaner Sendable story, but NSLock is correct.  
  - **Verify:** Grep confirms exactly four @unchecked Sendable sites in source: SidecarSupervisor.swift ResumeOnce (531), TaskHolder (546), OutputBuffer (555), and MiroFishSupervisor.swift LineBuffer (245). Each guards all mutable state with a private NSLock (ResumeOnce.done lines 532-540; TaskHolder.task 547-550; OutputBuffer.storage 556-566; LineBuffer.pending 245-260) and carries a doc comment naming the race (terminationHandler vs watchdog; readabilityHandler vs terminationHandler). This is the correct @unchecked Sendable pattern; the justification holds. P3 confirm-only is correct.  

#### Cross-cutting: Security & Process/Network Safety

- [x] **`sec-keychain-no-error-surface-11`** — KeychainStore swallows all OSStatus errors, returning nil/false silently  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/Shared/KeychainStore.swift:13-60`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** get() returns nil and set()/delete() return Bool with the underlying OSStatus discarded (no logging of the SecItem* status). A failure such as errSecInteractionNotAllowed (item not accessible because device is locked — directly caused by the AfterFirstUnlock choice in sec-keychain-accessibility-4) is indistinguishable from 'no key configured'. Callers like WebSearchTool.makeDefault and mirofishPayload then silently treat a transient Keychain failure as 'no key', degrading the workflow with no diagnostic.  
  - **Impact:** Keychain access failures are silently misreported as missing keys, producing confusing 'no backends configured' / missing-key errors that are hard to diagnose.  
  - **Fix:** Log non-success, non-errSecItemNotFound statuses via Log.provider (status code only, never the secret), and consider distinguishing 'not found' from 'access denied' in the return type (e.g. Result) so callers can tell the user 'unlock your Mac' vs 'add a key'.  
  - **Verify:** KeychainStore.get (lines 13-29) returns nil whenever status != errSecSuccess, discarding the OSStatus with no Log call; set/delete return Bool and drop the SecItem* status (lines 31-60). A transient errSecInteractionNotAllowed (device locked — exactly the AfterFirstUnlock case in sec-keychain-accessibility-4) is therefore indistinguishable from 'no key configured'. Confirmed at the call sites: WebSearchTool.makeDefault (lines 42-50) does `if let key = await store.get(.tavily), !key.isEmpty` and silently omits the backend on nil; mirofishPayload (ModelProviderManager.swift:147-151) likewise maps a nil get to 'no key' and pushForecastProvider then reports a misleading 'needs an API key'. No diagnostic logging of the underlying status. P3 robustness accurate.  
- [ ] **`sec-no-ats-config-9`** — No App Transport Security configuration; cleartext localhost traffic relied on implicitly  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:423 (GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE=YES, no INFOPLIST_KEY_NSAppTransportSecurity); Swift Deep Research.app/Contents/Info.plist (no NSAppTransportSecurity key)`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** The generated Info.plist contains no NSAppTransportSecurity dictionary. The app makes many http:// (cleartext) requests to localhost backends (ollama:11434, lmstudio:1234, seekdb:9100, mirofish:5001). Because App Sandbox is off and Hardened Runtime does not enforce ATS for cleartext the way the sandbox+ATS combo would, these currently work, but there is no explicit policy pinning cleartext to loopback only. If the app is ever sandboxed or distributed, arbitrary http to remote LLM endpoints (Ollama/LM Studio/custom can be pointed at remote http) would be silently allowed or silently blocked depending on defaults.  
  - **Impact:** Implicit, undocumented transport policy: remote cleartext could be allowed unintentionally, or localhost cleartext blocked after enabling the sandbox, with no clear configuration to reason about.  
  - **Fix:** Add an explicit NSAppTransportSecurity policy: keep NSAllowsArbitraryLoads = false, add NSExceptionDomains for localhost/127.0.0.1 with NSExceptionAllowsInsecureHTTPLoads = true (loopback only), and require https for all remote endpoints. This documents intent and prevents accidental remote cleartext.  
  - **Verify:** No NSAppTransportSecurity anywhere: project.pbxproj has GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE=YES (lines 423/461) with only INFOPLIST_KEY_CFBundleDisplayName/NSHumanReadableCopyright set, no INFOPLIST_KEY_NSAppTransportSecurity; built Swift Deep Research.app/Contents/Info.plist contains zero NSAppTransportSecurity occurrences; no source .plist declares it. App makes cleartext http to default ollama localhost:11434, lmstudio localhost:1234 (EngineConfiguration.swift:75-76), seekdb 127.0.0.1:9100, mirofish 127.0.0.1:5001. Accurate as a robustness finding — no explicit policy pinning cleartext to loopback; behavior would change if the app is ever sandboxed/distributed. P3 appropriate (note: on macOS, non-sandboxed apps get permissive ATS defaults, which is why this currently works).  
- [x] **`sec-reddit-subreddit-injection-7`** — Reddit subreddit string-interpolated into URL path before parsing (path/host injection)  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/RedditTool.swift:74-79`  
  - **Category:** security · **Effort:** S · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** adjusted  
  - **Problem:** The subreddit (LLM-controlled `args.subreddit`) is interpolated directly into the base string `https://www.reddit.com/r/\(sub)/search.json` and only then parsed by URLComponents(string:). Because it is concatenated before parsing, a value like `foo/../../x` or one containing `@`, `?`, `#`, or `/` can alter the path or query, and an `@` could even shift the authority — e.g. a crafted sub could redirect the request away from the intended path. URLComponents does not re-encode an already-formed string, so the percent-encoding happens too late.  
  - **Impact:** An LLM/prompt-injected subreddit value can manipulate the request path/query (and potentially the effective host), a lesser SSRF/parameter-injection vector.  
  - **Fix:** Build the URL with URLComponents path components / percent-encode the subreddit with addingPercentEncoding(.urlPathAllowed) before interpolation, and reject subreddit values containing '/','?','#','@','..'. Validate the resulting URL's host equals www.reddit.com before fetching.  
  - **Verify:** Partly real, partly wrong. RedditTool.swift:74-79 interpolates the LLM-controlled subreddit into `https://www.reddit.com/r/\(sub)/search.json` before URLComponents(string:) parses it, with no sanitization. Empirical test (swift) confirms path/query/fragment injection IS real: sub='foo?x=1' truncates path to /r/foo and drops the .json suffix; sub='foo#frag' injects a fragment; sub='foo/../../x' alters the path. BUT the finding's headline 'an @ could shift the authority / redirect the request away from the intended host' is FALSE — because the literal host www.reddit.com precedes /r/, the authority is already terminated; sub='foo@evil.com' yields host=www.reddit.com (verified). So this is a correctness/robustness bug (malformed Reddit query, broken .json suffix), not host-level SSRF/exfiltration. Severity P3 retained, but verdict adjusted because the central authority-redirect mechanism is misread.  
- [ ] **`sec-untrusted-html-pdf-parse-10`** — Untrusted HTML/PDF parsed and JS-rendered with no resource ceilings on the WKWebView path  
  - **Where:** `Swift Deep Research/ResearchTools/WebReaderTool.swift:152-216 (HiddenWebView/extractJavaScript); ResearchTools/PDFReaderTool.swift:80-123`  
  - **Category:** robustness · **Effort:** M · **Confidence:** medium · **Verdict:** confirmed  
  - **Problem:** Untrusted pages are loaded into a real WKWebView that executes arbitrary remote JavaScript (extractJavaScript), with default WKWebViewConfiguration — no JS allowlist, no content blocking, and (with sandbox off) full network/process context. The body slice is capped at 200k chars in JS (good), and a 750ms settle plus 30s load timeout exist, but there is no cap on subresource loads, no disabling of file access (allowFileAccessFromFileURLs), and no media/autoplay restriction. PDFReaderTool feeds attacker-controlled bytes to PDFKit (a historically CVE-prone parser); the maxPages/char caps bound output but not parser memory on a malicious PDF.  
  - **Impact:** Rendering hostile pages in a privileged, non-sandboxed WKWebView is a meaningful attack surface (JS exploitation, beacon/exfil via subresources); malformed PDFs can stress PDFKit.  
  - **Fix:** Harden the WKWebViewConfiguration: set a non-persistent data store (already implied), disable JavaScript where the static path suffices, set preferences to forbid file access from file/universal URLs, and add a WKContentRuleList to block third-party subresource loads. Run PDF parsing under a timeout and bounded memory, and reject oversized downloads before handing bytes to PDFKit. Combined with the sec-ssrf guard, restrict the JS-render path to https public hosts only.  
  - **Verify:** WebReaderTool.HiddenWebView (lines 154-216) creates a WKWebView with a bare default WKWebViewConfiguration() (line 159) — no JS allowlist, no WKContentRuleList/content blocking, no disabling of file access (no allowFileAccessFromFileURLs/allowUniversalAccessFromFileURLs handling), no media restriction — and executes remote-page JS via evaluateJavaScript (line 189). The body slice is capped at 200k chars in JS (line 208) and there is a 750ms settle + 30s load timeout, but no cap on subresource loads — accurate. PDFReaderTool.extract (lines 80-123) feeds attacker-controlled bytes to PDFDocument(data:) (line 81, PDFKit); maxPages/40k-char caps bound output but not parser memory on a malicious PDF — accurate. Robustness P3 fair.

## Refuted Findings (5) — excluded

These were proposed by auditors but the verification pass found them inaccurate on re-reading the code. Recorded for transparency; not actioned.

- **`mirofish-supervisor-3`** (forecast-mirofish) — ensureRunning launch-coalescing has an actor-reentrancy race that can spawn duplicate backends
  - _Why refuted:_ Refuted. The finding misreads actor reentrancy. Lines 76-78: `if let ensureTask { return await ensureTask.value }` / `let task = Task {...}` / `ensureTask = task`. The only await in this region (line 76) is inside the taken branch (ensureTask != nil). When ensureTask == nil, control runs lines 76->77->78 with NO suspension point (Task{} merely schedules; it does not await). Actor reentrancy can only interleave callers at await points, so two callers cannot both pass the nil-check and both create a task: the first caller to resume after probeHealth runs the check-and-assign atomically and sets ensureTask, and the second then sees ensureTask != nil and joins it. The sibling SidecarSupervisor.swift:78-80 documents exactly this reasoning ('single synchronous actor step (no await between them), so two callers can never both start a launch'). Coalescing works as intended; no duplicate-backend race.
- **`retry-non-idempotent-streaming-9`** (storage-domain-shared) — RetryPolicy.run retries whole operations with no idempotency guard
  - _Why refuted:_ Refuted as a present defect. RetryPolicy.run is only ever wrapped around non-streaming llm.complete() calls (Planner.swift:61-63, DeerFlowEngine.swift:442-444) — idempotent request/response, no side effects until return. Streaming paths do NOT use RetryPolicy.run; AnthropicClient/GeminiClient implement their own retry guarded by `serverResponded` (AnthropicClient.swift:72,87) that only retries while !serverResponded (before any tokens/usage are produced), exactly preventing the double-count/duplicate-delta scenario described. The finding even hedges ('if used around a streaming call'); since that never occurs, the claimed harm does not materialize.
- **`event-id-hashvalue-instability-10`** (storage-domain-shared) — ResearchEvent.id and dedup rely on String/Int hashValue, which is randomized per process
  - _Why refuted:_ Factually correct that ResearchEvent.id (ResearchEvent.swift:38-54) derives from String/URL .hashValue (process-seeded SipHash, unstable across launches, intra-run collisions possible). But the claimed concrete impact is refuted: ResearchEvent is only ever a stream element type — grep shows no [ResearchEvent] array rendered via ForEach. The UI consumes events through ingest() into derived collections (ActivityEntry/TokenStreamEntry) that each carry their own fresh UUID id (AppEnvironment.swift:347,366,373), never ResearchEvent.id. Persisted StoredEvent.id uses UUID().uuidString (ResearchStore.swift:171), not the hashValue id. So no SwiftUI list identity or persistence depends on the unstable id; the described view-drop/collision harm cannot occur.
- **`onboarding-backend-status-not-shared`** (ui-settings) — Onboarding 'Start backend' updates env status but workspace .task can race / double-launch the backend
  - _Why refuted:_ Refuted. The 'double-launch' concern is explicitly guarded: MiroFishSupervisor is an `actor` (line 24) that coalesces concurrent ensureRunning callers via `ensureTask` (lines 42-43, 76-81) and skips spawning when `process.isRunning` (line 88) — so the workspace .task's ensureForecastBackend() and the onboarding startBackend() cannot double-spawn. The 'optimistic .running can mask a still-launching/failed backend' claim is also wrong for the live path: finish() only sets `.running` when `onboarding?.completed == true` (line 72), and `completed` is set to true ONLY after ensureRunning returned `.running` (ForecastOnboarding.swift:256-260). The single residual edge — `completed` persisted from a prior session via UserDefaults could make finish() set a stale `.running` on a fresh launch — is immediately corrected by the workspace .task calling ensureForecastBackend()/checkHealth. The described race/concurrency defect does not hold.
- **`concurrency-runprocess-watchdog-race`** (xc-concurrency) — runProcess watchdog can resume the continuation while output is still draining; relies on best-effort NSLock guards
  - _Why refuted:_ SidecarSupervisor.swift:444-468. The central claim — 'on the timeout branch the readabilityHandler is never nil'd out' and 'keeps firing on a SIGKILL'd process, leaking the handler registration' — is incorrect. The watchdog resumes only AFTER it terminates/SIGKILLs the child (lines 460-464: `process.terminate()`, then `kill(SIGKILL)`). Killing the process causes it to terminate, which fires `process.terminationHandler` (line 444), and that handler nils the readabilityHandler (line 445) and cancels the watchdog holder (line 446). So the handler IS cleared on the timeout path — via process termination the watchdog itself caused. Double/spurious resume is prevented by `ResumeOnce` (line 447/465) and by `if Task.isCancelled { return }` (line 459) after timeoutHolder.cancel(). The @unchecked Sendable + NSLock guards are sound (the finding agrees). The described lifecycle gap does not exist.
